It is built from what the export sends, not the canvas as held (#297, #301). `cli render --irc [--budget N] [--fit] [--pad] [--swap-shades]` writes it as a PNG and prints how many rows are cut; it reads the piece, the budget (a project's own unless `--budget` is given) and the encoder options through the same code as `export --irc` (`ircSend` in `cli/main.cpp`), so it shows the lines that command writes. The IRC View dock (§11) shows it live, cut at the document's budget (the project's `ircBudget`, View > IRC > Budget...), the one the row meter, fit and Export use, so they work to one number; it follows the budget through undo and redo and takes the new one when another document is opened or started. It sends what the row meter measures and Export writes: each cell as saved with the document's iCE setting (`irc::sentCell`, `irc::sentCanvas`: with iCE off a bright background goes as its dark colour), encoded with the row meter's options (`IrcMeter::options`: View > IRC > Fit, Pad and Swap Shades), and it is redrawn at once when one of those is turned on or off, and after an iCE change as after any format change.

**Micron** (`core/` `micron.h`; #475): a NomadNet page, the canvas as the editor shows it (iCE through `iceCell`, the piece's own palette, an RGB cell as it is; a project's composite unfolded under either target, so `export --micron` of a project writes what File > Export Micron... does; a flat file is opened as the editor opens it, `openAsEditor`, so an iCE-off file holding a bright background is written with iCE on, as the app writes it), glyphs as the IRC export writes them (`irc::mircRow`), and a code point the NomadNet browser strips before parsing (`droppedByNomadNet`: marks, Cf, joiners, variation selectors, controls) written `?` and reported as `Loss::NoCode`. Colours are `` `Frgb ``/`` `Brgb `` when exact in three digits (the 16 colours), else `` `FTrrggbb ``, written only where they change and the cell shows them (no foreground for a blank that isn't underlined, no background for a █ that covers its cell: bold and underline leave it whole, but italic shears it off its background, as `formatRows` draws it (#360), so an italic █ has its background written). Every line opens with a colour code, so no text can start a line with Micron's line markup; `` ` `` and `\` are escaped with `\`, and a `!` after `#` too, since the browser reads `#!bg=`/`#!fg=` anywhere in a page. Lines end with ``` `` ```; a centred line (`--centre`, `` `c `` on every line) ends with its formatting toggles and `` `f`b `` instead, because ``` `` ``` resets the alignment and NomadNet aligns a line by its state at the end. Trailing blanks and blank rows are kept. `--cache S` writes `#!c=S` as the first line; neither option is on by default. `micron_nomadnet` reads the golden pages back with NomadNet's own parser where nomadnet is installed. A document under the Micron target (§17) is drawn for this export.

## 9. Pipeline integration

- **Publish to Library and Send to WeeChat: removed** (the maintainer's ruling, 2026-11-14). Phase 4c built them (#16): Publish wrote a piece's `.ans` and `.txt` into an ascii-script library folder and updated its `.tags.json` and `.dates.json`; Send copied a round over ssh to a WeeChat host's wip folder and reloaded the script through its fifo. Both fitted one person's own setup, not a general editor, so they were taken out with their core module (`pipeline.h`), their settings and their tests. A piece reaches IRC through File > Export IRC Text and `cli export --irc` (§8), which hold lines to the document's budget. Settings the two remembered (`pipeline/library`, `pipeline/host`, `pipeline/wipDir`, `pipeline/rounds/<slug>`) may linger in a user's config file; nothing reads them.
- **CLI:** `render`, `lint` (the lint rules; built, below), `export --irc`, `shade` (built, §10: the rung step, and the form, despeckle and rim-light brushes as modes over a `--box`, a `--mask` piece or both, #107), `add-generator` and `run-generators` (built, §7: `add-generator <project> <dir>` adds the generator in a directory as a new top layer, hidden files skipped and links refused, and runs it once; `run-generators <project>` re-runs every visible generator layer, or each `--layer ID` (hidden or not), bottom first, and lists each hidden one it skipped as `layer N (name): hidden, not run` before the runs (#440); both take `--timeout S`, save the project in the form it was read and write nothing if any run fails; `import-scene [--bake] [--author NAME] [--date CCYYMMDD] <scene.py> <out.bwp|dir>` writes a new project with one generator layer per scene layer, refuses an existing file or a non-empty directory, and writes nothing if any layer fails; `--bake` writes each layer as the cells it made without its `scripts/<id>/`, `--author` and `--date` set the SAUCE fields, and `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` stands in for today, so a pipeline rebuilds the same project byte for byte (§14, #308)).
- **Same-file output:** `cli convert x.ans x.ans` rewrites the file in place through a temporary file (normalising a piece is a real use), while `export` and `render` refuse an output that is their input under any name, since they would put a different format over the source.
- **Errors (#380):** a command wraps every error it can name a file for as a `Failure`, printed `blockwright-cli: <path>: <what>`, exit 1. `main()` ends its catch list with a `std::exception` backstop, after `std::bad_alloc` ("out of memory") and `std::length_error` ("input too large"): any other exception a command lets out (a core error it didn't wrap, a `std::filesystem` error, whose message names its path) is `blockwright-cli: <command>: <what>`, exit 1, the code for bad input and I/O failures, rather than `std::terminate`'s abort (exit 134). The backstop is for what no command expects, not the rule: `snapshot` of a project whose last round is r999999999 had let the core's "no rounds left" out and now names the project. The tests reach the backstop through a test-only hook, `BLOCKWRIGHT_CLI_FAULT=save`, which throws a `std::runtime_error` in the CLI's `save()` once the temporary is written and before the commit.
- **Replay (#208, built):** `blockwright-cli replay <steps.json> -o <dir> [--project] [--render [--scale N]]` applies a steps file's edits to its start through the core brushes, shapes, selection and layer operations the editor's tools use, in the order Document::edit applies them, and writes the flattened canvas with SAUCE after each step as `NN-<name>.ans` (`NN-<name>-before.ans` and `-after.ans` for a step marked `"capture": "before-after"`), with `.bwp` and `.png` beside it when asked. Every step is applied in memory first, so a step that fails writes nothing: the command exits 1 with the step's number and name ("step 3 (outline-head): ..."). Every output file is made in memory too, then each is written as `save` writes one, through its own `QSaveFile` (a temporary beside it), and committed into place only once all are written, so a write that fails (a full disk, a directory in the way, which is refused before anything is written) leaves no new file, and an existing capture is treated as every other command treats its output: a symlink is written through, the file keeps its permissions, and one that isn't writable refuses the run. Every output stays open until the commit, so the soft open-file limit is raised as far as the hard one for them; a run that needs more than that allows (one open file an output, and 64 to spare for the program's own) is refused before any file is opened or the directory made, naming the limit in force (the old one when it can't be raised). Captures are CP437 unless `--output-encoding utf-8` is given, even from a project whose `ansEncoding` is UTF-8: they are pictures for the docs, read outside Blockwright as `convert`'s output is, not a project's own rounds as snapshots are (#336). `--target` (#350) isn't replay's option: replay writes `.ans` files, whose bytes don't change with the target. A capture keeps its start's SAUCE record; a start with none (a blank canvas, a project saved without one) gets the record `Sauce::fresh` makes for a new piece, dated today, so `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` stands in for today as it does for import-scene (the UTC day of its seconds; an empty value counts as unset and anything else that isn't seconds as digits up to the end of 9999 is a usage error, exit 2, before the steps file is read) and the same steps file then writes the same bytes on any day. It only dates that record, never makes one, so a `--project` capture still has none, and a start's own record keeps its date. Files already in the directory are left as they are unless the run writes one of the same name: a shorter script replayed into a directory used before leaves the old captures past its last step, so replay into an empty directory to have only its captures. Refusing a non-empty directory was the alternative; it would stop the common rerun into the same directory for no gain when the names match. The tutorials' step pictures come from it, and the window's replay (#295) reads the same files through the same parser, `core/include/blockwright/steps.h`. The format:
  - A JSON object: `"version": 1`, a `"start"` (`{"file": ...}`, an `.ans`, `.xb`, `.bin` or project relative to the steps file, never an absolute path (#422, below); `{"sample": ..., "variant": ...}`, a bundled sample with one of its notes' variants made; or `{"blank": [w, h]}` with an optional top-level `"ice"`), and `"steps"`, at least one.
  - Each step has a `"name"` (1-64 of `a-z 0-9 - _`, it names the files) and an `"action"`, the editor's name for it: a tool's id for a stroke (`shade`, `rampShade`, `form`, `soft`, `despeckle`, `smudge`, `glyph`, `colorize`, `outline`, `rimLight`, `texture`, `spray` with `"points"`; `clone` with `"source"` and `"points"`, the Clone Stamp (#418, Clone steps below); `halfBlock` with `"points"` in pixels; `line`, `rectangle`, `ellipse` and `gradient` with `"from"` and `"to"`; `fill`, `eyedropper` with `"at"`; `text` with `"at"`, `"text"` and `"textOptions"`, the Text tool (#421, Text steps below); `keyboard` with `"at"` and `"text"`, NFC-composed first and typed under the start's typing policy (§16, Cursor and typing: its `target` and `ansEncoding`, not `--output-encoding`) as the editor's typing is, each line as one input-method commit (`CanvasView::typeText`): a line of combining marks alone joins the cluster before the cursor (`typeMarks`), and any other line goes through `replaceText`, so one that starts with a mark and has more after it is refused and stops the replay; `move` with `"by"`, refused on a locked or hidden layer as the Move tool refuses it, and on a reference layer moving the picture by canvas pixels as the Move tool's drag does; `select` with `"rect"`, corners included), a menu action's object name (`selectAll`, `deselect`, `copy`, `cut`, `deleteSelection`, `paste` with `"at"`, `flipHorizontal`, `flipVertical`, `rotateClockwise`, `rotateCounterClockwise`, `newCellsLayer`, `newPixelLayer`, `deleteLayer`, `raiseLayer`, `lowerLayer`), and three of the replay's own for what the editor does by dragging or in a dock: `moveSelection` (`"by"`), `setActiveLayer` (`"layer"`, a name) and `layerProperties` (`"rename"`, `"visible"`, `"locked"`, `"alphaLocked"`); and `canvasSize` (`"size"`, `"anchor"`) for Format > Canvas Size... (#420, Canvas size steps below). `"button": "right"` is the right mouse button.
  - Any step but a view step (#398) or a canvasSize step (#420) may set Tool Options (`fg`, `bg`, `size`, `tip`, `strength`, `pressure`, `light`, `ramp`, `symmetry`, `form`, `hardness`, `outline`, `keepLint`, `glyph`, `filled`, `pencil`, `pasteMode`, `gradient`, `texture`, `spray`), which hold for the steps after it as they do in the editor; a tool step without points only sets them. `"sampleMerged"` (#419, off by default as in the editor) is Sample merged, and as Tool Options shows it only for fill and the eyedropper, only a `fill` or `eyedropper` step takes it; it holds for the steps after it all the same, and `--screenshot` ticks the box. It runs the editor's own reads (below, Sample merged): fill floods the composite and paints that region into the active layer with what the layers below show in its clear cells, the eyedropper picks from the composite, both leaving out hidden and Reference layers as the canvas does. The clone stamp's `aligned` is a Tool Option too, but only a clone step takes it, as only that tool shows it. So is `textOptions` (#421), the Text tool's settings, which only a text step takes.
  - A brush stroke's point may be `[x, y, pressure]` (0-1; 1 when left out, as a mouse presses). `"pressure"` is the Tool Options Pressure field: `none` (the start's), `size` or `strength` (#382). Each point's pressure scales the size (rounded, at least 1) or the strength as a stylus's does in the editor (`Stroke::stamp`): a stroke at size 4 with `"pressure": "size"` paints one cell at 0.25 and four by four at 1, and with `none` four by four at any pressure. A point on the last dab's own cell is a dab only if it presses harder with pressure on, and is dropped otherwise, as the editor's tool drops the move (`BrushTool::extend`); both go by one rule, `takesDab` (`core/include/blockwright/brush.h`). Stamping the repeat would not paint the same: a brush that carries a value from dab to dab, as smudge does, would carry the dropped repeat on into the cells after it (tests hold every brush to the rule under each mode, with the repeat at a stroke's start, middle and end, and the editor and replay to the same smudge).
  - Every key is checked: an unknown key, a key the action doesn't take, a value of the wrong kind or out of range, a point or a radial symmetry centre off the canvas (the editor fits a set centre onto the canvas, so one off it is a picture no drag makes), a layer that can't take the edit (hidden, locked, a Pixels layer for a tool that paints shades, or for a glyph it can't hold: the message names the glyph, or the ▐ ▌ radial symmetry turns ▀ ▄ into, and that the layer holds only █ ▀ ▄ and blanks, #436) all stop the replay naming the step. `replay --help` gives the whole format. Not yet: TheDraw faces and text styles in a text step, and editing an existing text layer's text.
- **Window replay (#295, built):** `blockwright --screenshot <steps.json> -o <dir>` replays the same steps file in the real window, offscreen, and writes a PNG for each step with a `"screenshot"`: `{"of": "window"}` (the default), a dock by its object name (`layersDock`, or `layers` without the `Dock`), or any widget in the window by its object name, with an optional `"crop"` of `{"cells": [x, y, w, h]}` (needs `"of"` to hold the canvas, and the cells all in view) or `{"pixels": [x, y, w, h]}` inside `"of"`, and `"when"` `before`, `after` (the default) or `both`. The file's top-level `"window": [w, h]` (320-4096 a side) sizes the window, 1280 x 800 without one. `blockwright-cli replay` reads both keys and passes over them, so one file makes the tutorial's `.ans` captures and its window pictures. The replay isn't forked: `bwcli::replay::run` (`cli/replay.h`) takes an observer that sees each step before and after it is applied, and at each moment a screenshot asks for the window is given the replay's project (`Document::replace`), active layer and selection, the step's tool is chosen as the Tools menu chooses it, and the replay's Tool Options and pen (`Moment::settings`, the step's own options taken in before it as after it, and those the steps before it left: size, tip, strength, pressure, light, ramp, symmetry, form, hardness, outline shape, Keep the lint clean, glyph, Filled Shapes, pencil joins, the gradient's shape, banding and rungs, texture, spray and paste mode) are set on the view and the tools, which Tool Options and the palette follow (#381), so an option no step has set yet shows what the replay paints with at the start (steps.h: size 1, square, 100%, no pressure, fire, grey on black), not the editor's own defaults, and a step that leaves an option out shows the value the steps before it left (#391), and a tool step with no points but options and a `"screenshot"` of `toolOptions` pictures a step's settings; a dock asked for is brought to the front of its tabs, each shown dock in the target that follows the document (Lint, IRC View) has its refresh started at once and is waited for until its result is of the moment's document (not after its 250 ms delay, and never an older result), and then the target is grabbed. The cell cursor is hidden and the window has scratch settings (default keys and layout, nothing kept) with no fonts folder, an empty user Library (`screenshot::Scratch`), Fusion with its standard palette and no theme or style from the environment, so a run depends only on the steps file, Qt and the fonts. No fonts folder (#459) because, unset, it would be the default `~/tdfiglet/fonts`, scanned on a worker no picture waits for: a picture caught the face picker on "Scanning fonts…" and, after it, listing the machine's own collection. So the face picker lists the bundled faces and the TheDraw fonts the steps name, and nothing is scanned. Pictures are made in memory and written as replay writes its captures (`bwcli::saveAll`, `cli/save_all.h`): each written and flushed to its own `QSaveFile`, committed only once all are, and a commit that fails removes the ones already put in place that weren't there; a step that fails (a core error it raises, such as layer ids exhausted, included: #383), a dock the window doesn't have (the message lists the docks) or a crop off its target writes nothing and exits 1 naming the step. The start is read as `blockwright-cli replay` reads it, through the same `bwcli::replay::startSaying`, so what reading it changed (a project before 5.0's cleared attribute bits, #370) is said the same way, `blockwright: note: <steps>: start: <file>: ...` on stderr, once, before any step runs and before the window is made (#383). Tested by structure (names, sizes, crop geometry, and the canvas in a picture equal to `renderCanvas` of the CLI replay's capture), not by comparing whole-window PNGs, which change with fonts and Qt. Scale and the fixed screens, and the pointer and the bundled UI font: the next two entries.
- **Scale and fixed screens (#295, built):** a step's `"screenshot"` takes `"scale": 1 | 2` (1 without one; any other value, `1.5`, `"2"` or `true` refuses the file naming the step). A picture at 2 is drawn at device pixel ratio 2, not enlarged: the window is moved to a screen at that ratio, keeps its size in its own pixels and is laid out again there, so it has twice the pixels each way (the default 1280 x 800 window is 2560 x 1600, a `"crop"` of `"pixels"` is in the window's own pixels and gives twice them, a crop of cells is the cells at twice the view's pixels), and its canvas is the replay's canvas rendered at twice the zoom; a picture at 1 after it moves the window back. The default window size is the same at both scales: a 2x picture is the 1x one at twice the resolution, as a high-DPI screen shows the window, which is what a page offering both for one figure needs (the issue's "a default for each scale" read as the same size in the window's pixels; a smaller window at 2, so the PNG stays 1280 x 800, was the alternative, and would lay the window out differently from its 1x picture). The screens are fixed: `blockwright --screenshot` writes the offscreen platform's configuration to a temporary file before the `QApplication` and runs on it (`screenshot::FixedScreens`), two screens side by side, each 4096 window pixels a side (the largest `"window"`) at 96 logical dpi, the first the primary at ratio 1, the second at 2; QT_SCALE_FACTOR, QT_SCREEN_SCALE_FACTORS, QT_FONT_DPI and the other variables that scale screens or fonts are cleared, and a `QT_QPA_PLATFORM` from the environment is replaced. So a picture no longer depends on the machine's screen or dpi (a screen's dpi sets every font's size in pixels), only on the steps file, Qt and the fonts. The ratio-2 screen is Qt's own high-DPI scaling, 192 logical dpi over a base of 96 on an 8192-pixel screen, not the offscreen platform's `"dpr"` key: that one sets the screen's ratio but not its windows' (Qt 6.12's offscreen windows keep a ratio of 1), so a widget would still draw at 1. The platform's arguments are split at `:` and its list of platforms at `;`, so the file goes in the temporary folder when its path has neither (a path with a drive's `:` may be given relative to the current folder), else (on Unix) in `/tmp`, which is also tried when the temporary folder can't be written; the file is removed as soon as the `QApplication` has read it, so a run that dies later leaves nothing behind. In the editor's own tests (`take` without `FixedScreens`) a scale-2 picture goes to the first screen at ratio 2 and fails naming the step when there is none (`step 2 (sharp): no screen draws at scale 2`); the ctest entry "screenshots at scale 2" runs those tests on the same two screens, from the same configuration, and checks that they are. Same steps file, same build, same machine: the same PNG bytes, at either scale, whatever the environment says about scaling (tested by running the binary twice, and again under QT_SCALE_FACTOR, QT_SCREEN_SCALE_FACTORS, QT_FONT_DPI and a `configfile` of its own). The UI font is the bundled one (next entry), so text no longer depends on the machine's fonts.
- **Pointer and UI font (#295, built):** the maintainer's rulings. *Pointer:* a step's `"screenshot"` takes `"pointer": true | false` (false without one; any other value refuses the file naming the step), and with true the picture shows a mouse pointer where the step left it; without it nothing changes and every picture is the same bytes as before. Where it sits (`steps::pointerAt`): after a step, at its last point (a stroke's or line's last `points`, a shape's `"to"`, a click's, fill's, paste's or keyboard step's `"at"`); before it (a `"when"` of `before` or `both`), at its first, where the drag starts. A `select` step's `"rect"` is a drag from its top-left cell to its bottom-right, so before it the pointer is at the top-left and after it at the bottom-right. A step the pointer makes none of (a tool step with no points, the menu, layer and dock actions, `move` and `moveSelection` by an offset, `view`) refuses the file naming the step, as does a pointer the picture can't show: `"of"` not holding the canvas, the point off the canvas, out of the view's scrolled area, or outside the crop, each naming the step and the point. The hotspot is the centre of the cell, or for a pixel action (half-block points) of the half the pixel is (its row's upper or lower half). The arrow is a plain one, not the editor's tool cursor: a black arrow 11 by 18 window pixels with a 1-pixel white edge, drawn over the grabbed picture with antialiasing at the picture's ratio, so at scale 2 it has twice the pixels and stays sharp; its tip is the hotspot. *UI font:* Noto Sans v2.015 (Regular and Bold; the window draws bold in Tool Options, the palette, the start screen and the tool icons, so both ship) is built into the program as a Qt resource (`third_party/noto-sans/`, its OFL 1.1 and source installed in `share/doc/blockwright/noto-sans/`), so it loads the same way on every platform with no font path to find. Only `blockwright --screenshot` uses it: it is registered under a family of its own, `Bw Screen` (the name table rewritten, as the layout tests' `Bw Layout`, so an installed Noto Sans can't shadow it), and is the application font at a pinned 10 pt (96 dpi on the fixed screens, so 13⅓ px at 1 and 26⅔ at 2), with light hinting and greyscale antialiasing; the editor itself keeps the system font. A font that can't be loaded exits 1 before the window is made. The GUI's `--version` and About box credit it (`kUiFontCredit`), as they do the icons, which only the GUI ships too. Limit: Noto Sans has no block or shade glyphs (█ ▓ ▒ ░), so where the window draws them in its own widgets (the Glyphs bar's set names, the status bar's F-keys, Tool Options' help) Qt falls back to a system font for those glyphs, so they look as that font draws them, and a fallback font taller or shorter than Noto Sans changes those widgets' heights, so the docks laid out round them can shift by a pixel or two (the CI container's fonts made the status bar 2 px taller than no fonts at all), and text that wraps round them (Tool Options' help) can wrap differently. The canvas, drawn in Blockwright's own fonts, and the text of every widget are drawn in the bundled font on every machine. *Golden test (option c):* whole-window PNGs are still not stored and compared; the pictures are tested by structure and run to run (same bytes twice, and with no system fonts at all for the Layers dock's title, header and rows), and the default window stays 1280 x 800 at both scales (2560 x 1600 at 2).
- **View steps (#398, built):** the maintainer's ruling: a steps file sets the window's view with an explicit step, `{"name": ..., "action": "view", "zoom": 1-8, "scrollTo": [x, y], "ircFit": bool, "ircPad": bool, "ircSwapShades": bool}`, every field optional and at least one required (an empty view step is refused: it would do nothing, which is a mistake in the file). A field left out leaves that setting as it was. A step's own keys follow the format's convention, so a view step takes `"screenshot"` but not `"at"`, `"fg"` or any tool option, and a view key on another step is refused as any unknown key is. `"zoom"` is a level the app offers, 1-8 (`CanvasView::kMinZoom`-`kMaxZoom`, which Zoom In, Zoom Out and Actual Size step through; a `static_assert` in `app/screenshot.cpp` keeps the parser's limits equal to the view's). `"scrollTo"` is a cell, not pixels: the scroll bars hold view pixels, which change with the zoom, while the IRC View follows the canvas by cell and a screenshot's crop is already in cells, so a cell means the same thing at every zoom. The cell is put at the canvas view's top left, as far as the scroll bars go (at the canvas's right or bottom edge they stop short): each bar is set to the last whole value that keeps the cell's whole widget rect in view, so the rect starts at the viewport's corner and a `"crop"` of `"cells"` of it is taken as on main (`cropOf` is unchanged: a cell even part out of view is refused). At a whole device pixel ratio the corner is then on the cell, as the IRC View reads it. Off one, a cell's edge can fall inside a widget pixel and the rect is rounded out, so no whole scroll value may put the edge on the viewport's: the cell before then shows up to one widget pixel at the edge, and the IRC View, which reads the cell at the corner, may start a column or row early. The alternative, the first value whose corner is on the cell, leaves the part-pixel the cell shares with the one before out of view, so a crop of the cell scrolled to was refused; loosening the crop to allow it would have changed `cropOf` for every file, and a whole cell in view is what a screenshot of it needs. It is read as 0-4095 a side and, when the step is replayed, must be on the canvas as it stands then; one off it is refused naming the step (`step 2 (far): scrollTo cell (20, 0) is off the 20 x 10 canvas`). The three booleans are View > IRC's Fit, Pad and Swap Shades, the toggles the IRC meter offers, set on the same actions, so the meter, its menu and the IRC View follow as they do from the menu. Any other value (zoom 0, 9, 2.5 or `"4"`, a scroll that isn't two whole numbers, a boolean given as `1`, `"on"` or `null`) refuses the whole file with `step N (name): ...`, as every bad step is refused: the format is JSON read through `QJsonDocument`, so there is no line number to give, and the step's number and name are what every other message names. Order: a view step takes effect where it stands and holds until a later view step changes it, so the view is cumulative and each screenshot shows the view as of its own moment, `"when": "before"` on a view step the view the steps before it left (the replay carries it in `Moment::view`, beside the Tool Options). A window that has had no view step keeps a fresh window's view (zoom 2, scrolled to the top left, Fit, Pad and Swap Shades off), so every file written before this replays unchanged. Within a step the zoom is applied before the scroll, since the cell's place in pixels depends on it. `blockwright-cli replay` has no window, so it refuses a file with a view step before reading the start or writing anything, exits 1 (`kBadInput`, `cli/main.cpp`, as every bad step does), and names the first one: `<steps>: step 2 (zoomed): view sets the window's zoom, scroll and IRC options, and blockwright-cli replay has no window; replay this file with blockwright --screenshot`. Passing over it was the alternative, as the CLI passes over `"screenshot"` and `"window"`; it would make captures that silently differ from what the file says the reader sees, and the ruling is to refuse. A view step makes no undo step and doesn't mark the document modified: zoom, scroll and the IRC meter's toggles are the editor's, not the document's, and a project has no field for them, so the window's undo stack and modified state are what the replay's edits leave.
- **Canvas size steps (#420, built):** `{"name": ..., "action": "canvasSize", "size": [w, h], "anchor": ...}` is Format > Canvas Size... (§5, Canvas Size). `"size"` is required, 1 to `kMaxColumns` by 1 to `kMaxRows` cells; `"anchor"` is one of the dialog's nine, `top-left`, `top`, `top-right`, `left`, `centre`, `right`, `bottom-left`, `bottom`, `bottom-right`, and top-left when left out, where the dialog starts. The step applies the resize as the dialog does: a size `canvasSizeProblem` refuses (more cells than a project holds) stops the replay naming the step, the canvas's own size changes nothing (a note, as for any step that changes nothing), and any other resizes every layer with `resizeCanvas` at the document's cell width and moves the selection with the cells, cut to the canvas. The active layer, the clipboard and the Tool Options stay as they are, but for a set symmetry centre the new canvas no longer holds, which is pulled onto its nearest cell as the view pulls it. Later steps' points are checked against the new size. The step takes no Tool Options, as a view step takes none, because its `"size"` is the canvas's, not the brush's; it takes `"capture"` and `"screenshot"` as any step does, and a `"pointer"` is refused, since no point of it is the pointer's. In the window (`--screenshot`) the step is refused as the dialog refuses it when the renderer can't take the new canvas at its cell width ("the canvas is too large to display", the largest canvas at 9 px), whether or not it asks for a picture; a step at the canvas's own size is not checked, as the dialog's OK at the same size checks nothing.
- **Clone steps (#418, built):** `{"name": ..., "action": "clone", "source": [x, y], "points": [...], "button": ..., "aligned": bool}` is the Clone Stamp (§10, Clone stamp). `"source"` is the cell Ctrl+click sets (`CloneTool::setSource`), set before the step's stroke: a cell off the canvas stops the replay naming the step, where the editor's click there would set nothing, since a source the file names and the replay doesn't take would make a picture other than the one asked for. It holds for later clone steps, with the distance the first stroke after it sets, as the tool keeps both across strokes; nothing in a steps file forgets it (only opening a file or New does, and no step does either). A clone step with a source and no points only sets the source, as a Ctrl+click paints nothing. A stroke with no source, on its own step or an earlier clone step, refuses the file naming the step when it is read, rather than painting nothing as the editor's press does: a stroke that paints nothing is a mistake in the file. `"points"` are cells, `[x, y, pressure]` as any brush's, and the stroke is `CloneSource::beginStroke` from the first point and the core `paintStroke`, the brush tools' stroke, so size, tip, pressure, symmetry, the selection and the alpha lock limit it as in the editor, and the stroke sets the distance as the tool's release does (`CloneTool::finish`), even one that changes nothing. `"aligned"` is Tool Options' Aligned, on when no step has set it (the tool's default), and held for later steps; only a clone step takes it. Either button copies the same, so `"button"` is taken and changes nothing. The clone copies the active layer's own cells, so on a layered start the layers above and below, hidden or not, play no part: the Clone Stamp's Tool Options have no Sample merged, so a clone step refuses `"sampleMerged"` (#419) as an unknown key, and one an earlier fill or eyedropper step left on changes nothing it copies. In the window (`--screenshot`) the step chooses the tool, and the replay's source, distance and Aligned are put on it (`CloneTool::restore`) after the document, which forgets them, so the view marks the source and Tool Options shows Aligned as the replay has them. The pointer: the source is the step's first press, so a picture before a clone step with a source has the pointer there, and one after it at the last point, or at the source when there are none.
- **Text steps (#421, built):** `{"name": ..., "action": "text", "at": [x, y], "text": "...", "textOptions": {...}}` is the Text tool (§15): a click at the cell `"at"`, then `"text"` typed there, which makes a new live text layer as the tool's first keystroke does (`Document::addTextLayer`): `makeTextLayer` from the tool's settings with the text, named the first free `Text N`, put just above the active layer and made active. `"text"` is taken as typed, not NFC-composed, since the Text tool doesn't compose; `"\n"` is Enter and any other control character, C0 (U+0000–U+001F), DEL or C1 (U+0080–U+009F), is refused, as the tool drops them (`typedInText` in `core/text.h`, which the tool's typing and input method commits use too; no face draws a control character, and C1 is unwritable in a UTF-8 `.ans` and IRC text, §8; #428); 1-4096 characters (`TextSource::kMaxLength`). A text step only makes layers: a click on a text layer the tool would edit (the topmost visible, unlocked live one whose box holds the cell, `textLayerAt` in `core/text.h`, which the tool's click uses too) refuses the step naming that layer, rather than editing it, so a file's text always lands on a layer of its own. `"textOptions"` is the Text tool's Tool Options: `face` (a bundled face's name, or a TheDraw font by its file, #422 D1 below), `case`, `letterSpacing`, `lineSpacing`, `align`, `wrap`, `colour`, `bigStyle`, `bigColours` (1-16 colours), each optional and checked as the source's own limits check it, and set as the Tool Options controls set them (`steps::applyTextOptions`, after `TextOptions::apply`): a big style picked puts its own colours in (`bigTextDefaultColours`) unless the step gives `bigColours`, and no style clears them; under a big style the face is block, so `face` other than `block` and `colour` are refused there, since the dock turns those controls off, and `bigColours` without a big style is refused, as its field is off; only a text step takes it, and it holds for later text steps, as the tool keeps its settings. The order is the tool's: click, then the settings, then the typing, so a step's options set the new layer's settings and the defaults after it. A step with `"textOptions"` and no `"at"` is Tool Options with no text cursor waiting (`TextTool::setSettings`): the controls show the active layer's settings when it is a live text layer, locked or not, else the defaults, and they apply only when one of them changes. So settings that change what they show become the defaults, that layer's settings with the step's changes, and the layer itself takes them only when it isn't locked (one edit, `textEdit` and `applyEdit`, as `Document::setTextSource`). A step with no settings, or only the ones shown, changes nothing, and one with settings is noted so; this holds for the face and big colours too, though the face picker and the colours field can apply an unchanged value, since a steps file repeating a setting means no change. In the window (`--screenshot`) the replay's settings are put on the tool as its defaults (`TextTool::setSettings` with no document) after the document, and the Tool Options controls are shown again from them (`TextOptions::sync`), as is the status bar's "Face:" (#459: a step that only sets the face left it at the face before).
- **Text, reference and generator layers (#422, decided; paths, the load pass and D1 built):** the maintainer accepted the design note's recommendations (2026-11-08). Each step lands in its own PR, after the paths and load pass below.
  - **D1, TheDraw faces (built):** a text step's `textOptions.face` takes `{"tdf": <path>, "font": <name>, "index": 0-255}` (`index` optional) as well as a bundled face's name: a `.tdf` file named relative to the steps file, and the font's name in it. Without `index` the name must be the only font of that name in the file; with it, the font at `index` must have that name, as `FaceRef` checks (§15). The file is copied into the project's `fonts/<sha256>.tdf` as a fonts-folder font is, so a `--project` capture opens without the steps directory. Not a hash into the start's `fonts/` (a start without one could never use a TheDraw face, and nobody can write one by hand), and not a lookup in the fonts folder (the result would depend on the machine's preferences). Built so: the parser checks the object's keys and the path (`textOptions face tdf`, through `pathProblem`) and lists the file in `Step::files` with `kMaxTdfBytes`; the replay reads the font from the loaded bytes when the step is applied (`loadFontFile`, or the copy the project or an earlier step already holds, by its sha256), so a malformed file stops the replay naming the step and the `TdfError`, a name the file doesn't hold, a name two fonts share with no `index`, and an `index` past the fonts or naming another font each stop it naming the step and listing the fonts the file holds (`it holds 0 "BW Outline", 1 "BW Block", ...`), and a step that would leave the text layers using over `kMaxFontFiles` (16) font files, more than a project holds, stops it naming the step, whatever the replay writes; in each case nothing is written. The face then behaves as one picked in the face picker: it holds for later text steps (the replay keeps the file as the Text tool keeps a picked face's, `ToolSettings::fonts`), and a layer set in it, new or restyled, copies the file into the project (`Document::keepFont`); a locked layer or a cells layer takes only the defaults, and nothing is copied. A big style refuses it, as the face picker is off under one. In the window (`--screenshot`) the Text tool keeps the file (`TextTool::keepFont`), so the face picker lists it as kept for new text and shows it picked. The `tdf-text` golden in `tests/data/replay/` and the tour's `46-tdf-text` use the repo's test fonts in `tests/data/tdf/`.
  - **D2, styles:** `applyTextStyle`'s `"style"` is a bundled Library preset's slug (`"chrome"`, the file stem under `library/text-styles/`) or `{"file": <path>}`, a `.style.json` relative to the steps file, read strictly (an unknown key is refused). There are no inline styles.
  - **D3, editing text:** `editText` with `"text"` replaces the active live text layer's whole text in one `textEdit`, under the text step's rules for `"text"`. There is no caret model, and no `text` key on `layerProperties`.
  - **D4, generators:** no generator steps for now. Replay never runs a generator: a start `.bwp`'s generator layers still show the cells they last saved, as the composite shows them. A follow-up issue comes if tutorials need one.
  - **D5, reference pictures:** `openReferenceImage` takes PNG and JPEG only, kept byte for byte (never re-encoded), so a `--project` capture's `refs/` entry equals the file on every platform. Any other format is refused, asking for a PNG.
  - **Text layers in the captures:** a capture's `.ans` is the flattened composite (`project::toAnsi`), so a live text layer's cells show in it as the canvas shows them, a styled one's too, since the style is drawn into the layer's cells.
  - **Paths (built):** every path a steps file names, `start.file` and each file a later step reads, is relative to the steps file's directory with `/` between directories (`steps::pathProblem`, resolved by `bwcli::replay::resolve`). `..` may leave the directory, so tutorials can share a fonts folder. An empty path, a NUL, a `\` (Windows' separator and the start of a UNC path) and an absolute path (a leading `/`, or a drive letter and `:`) are refused, naming the key: `start: "file" "/x/piece.ans" is absolute; a steps file names its files relative to its own directory`. Before #422, `dir / abs` gave `abs`, so an absolute start was taken silently; no steps file in the repo used one.
  - **The load pass (built):** each step lists the files it reads and the most bytes each may hold (`Step::files`), and `bwcli::replay::load` reads them into `Script::files` after the start and before `run()`, in both `blockwright-cli replay` and `--screenshot`, so applying a step does no I/O and a missing file writes nothing. It refuses naming the step and the path as resolved: `step 3 (title): <dir>/fonts/x.tdf: no such file`, `: is a directory`, `: not a regular file`, the read's own error (`: can't be read: Permission denied`), and `: larger than N bytes, the most it may be`. The file is read through its opened handle (`library::readRegularFile`), so a FIFO or device swapped in after the checks is refused rather than blocking or flooding the read; a start file is told from a zip project by its first four bytes, read through such a handle, and any other file is read the same way, held to `project::kMaxEntryBytes`, which `fstat` refuses before a byte is read. A zip project is then read again by `project::read`, by path, as the app's Open reads it; that read checks its own handle in turn (#448, §6). A path two steps write the same way is read once and held to the smaller limit. A text step's TheDraw face is the first action to name a file (`kMaxTdfBytes`); each later limit comes with its action (`project::kMaxEntryBytes` for a picture).
- Later: an MCP server over the CLI (Icy Draw ships one).

As built (#15, #106; `core/lint_rules.h`, `cli/lint.h`): `cli lint <in>` runs `lint::lintCanvas`, which the Lint dock runs too, so the two agree. It reads the whole canvas: the ladder check (§10) under each `--ramp` (repeatable, a name or palette indices, refused as `shade` refuses them; default every named ramp), with fallback specks on. A shade of two colours that any ramp linted has as neighbours is no fault, whichever ramps don't: the shading rule is "dither only between ramp neighbours", and the lint can't know which ramp the artist shaded on, so ░ of LightRed on Red is clean under the default though skin, read alone, would flag it. Under the default (no `--ramp`; the Lint dock's "All named ramps") a shade of a pair hand shading uses is no fault either (`lint::craftPair`): the measured ladders of hand shading are not all named ramps (warm skin is k n R w, brown base, where the named skin ramp is k r n R Y), and it has "a saturated hero colour dithers straight into black for folds (Y▓k, not via brown)" and "a one-hue ladder over black". So every colour but white may step straight into black, and the measured ladders' other steps (pale skin K w W, warm skin n R w, cool cloth m B c w, W▓B, Y▓W, m░r, K▓m, C░c, Y░r, hair Y░n, and nature.md's storm water K g, G▒K and crest r▒K) count as neighbours. White on black stays a fault: the notes draw it as strokes, not a ladder. A `--ramp` named on the command line (or one ramp picked in the dock) asks whether the piece is shaded on that ramp, so the craft pairs don't apply there. Its message names the glyph and both colours (`cell 0,0: ░ of 12 on 4, colours no ramp linted has as neighbours`). The other ladder findings stand under the ramp they came from: the named ramps put the colours they share in the same order (a test holds them to it), so where two ramps can both read a cell they agree on it; pixel noise; and the craft rules a program can judge (`checkRules`): `lone-pixel` (a one-pixel glint alone on the form it lights, a lighter rung of that colour on a named ramp), `gap` (a one-cell break in a line one cell thick, not where it breaks again 3–6 cells on with the line on both sides of that break, as a dashed line does; past the line's end is no break, so a line of two cells either side of its gap is judged; nor along the canvas's edge, where the cell across the line is off the canvas and there is no telling the line is one cell thick, as a column of dither down the edge isn't), `merge` (a glyph in its own background colour), `dotted-diagonal` (four or more pixels joined only at their corners), `flat` (only in `--box`, as the detail's flat share is read for `--box <subject>`: the cells of 4×2-cell windows of one look, not black, at every offset, joined side by side into areas of 48 cells or more, so the box's position doesn't change what is found; with no box it isn't judged, since a ground or backdrop may be flat by intent and flat fills are fine for graphic styles), `trailing-black` (black at a row's end where the row above or below reaches the edge: IRC shows the client's background there), `blank-edge-rows` and `no-cp437` (a glyph an `.ans` saves as `?`: one with no CP437 code, or one whose CP437 byte `.ans` reads as a control code, which the message names; the glyph follows its code point, and a control, bidi or invisible character (`unprintableCodePoint`, §8) is named by its code point alone, as `U+202E` or `U+FE0F`, never echoed raw, in the text, the JSON and the Lint dock). `no-cp437` is the ANSI target's (§6); under the IRC target, where those glyphs are fine, `ans-loss` runs instead: one finding for each kind of loss an `.ans` save would have, at the first cell it covers, counting the cells (glyphs saved as `?`, colours saved as the nearest of the 16, and cells with bold, italic or underline, which an `.ans` leaves out). `wasted-format` (#350 slice 3a; a warning under either target, since the bits are in the cells whichever target the document has) is formatting that draws nothing and still costs its toggles: an attribute without which the cell draws the same pixels at 8 and 9 px, read from the same rows the renderer draws (`formatRows`, `core/glyph_rows.h`), so the two can't disagree (#360). Bold on a blank (a space, NUL or no-break space), `▀` or `█` and underline on `█` are; italic on `█` isn't, since it shears the block off its background, and an underlined blank draws its line. Only a glyph the VGA font has is judged. The target is the project's, or ANSI for a flat file, and `--target ansi|irc` overrides it. `ans-loss` is a note (`lint::noteKind`, the one note so far; the maintainer's ruling on #352): it is printed, `note:` before its message in the text and the Lint dock (in italics there) and `"severity":"note"` in the JSON, but a note alone doesn't make a report unclean (`LintReport::clean`), so `lint` exits 0 and the summary says `clean, 1 note (1 ans-loss)`. The ladder rules judge an RGB cell by the nearest of the 16 (#350); rungs and shades read indexed cells only, so in practice that is a fallback speck at an RGB cell, and under the IRC target its message ends `(an RGB cell, judged by the nearest of the 16)`. Only a colour the cell shows counts, by `quantisedColourCount`'s rule (`showsQuantisedColour`: a foreground on a glyph that draws one, a background on a glyph that isn't a full block, and not exactly one of the 16), so an RGB foreground on a space or background under `█` adds nothing. A finding of one kind at the same cells under two ramps is reported once, under the first. iCE comes from the file (SAUCE). `--box` keeps the findings with a cell in the box (a dotted diagonal by the cells its pixels are in, not the rectangle between its ends) (noise, a whole-canvas score, always passes) and refuses a box off the canvas, as `shade` does. Text output is a line per finding, `x,y kind ramp message` (`-` for no ramp; a note's message starts `note: `), then the summary (`lint::summary`: the findings by kind, then the notes by kind); `--json` prints `{"findings":[{kind,severity,ramp,x,y,x2,y2,message}],"noise":N,"ice":bool}`, `severity` `"warning"` or `"note"`. It exits 4 when there are findings other than notes, 0 when clean. Each of the 317 real pieces (`tests/data/golden` and funkpower's) lints in under 150 ms under every ramp, the process included (cocktober, 120 rows: about 20 ms of lint). Known gap: the craft rule "figures that touch and fuse" needs to know which cells are which figure, which a flat canvas doesn't; `merge` covers only a glyph that can't be seen, and the rest waits for layers (§5).

## 10. Advanced brushes (shading and detail)

The core of what an editor adds: the shading and detail moves of hand-made block art, done at the cursor. Each brush is a core-library function with tests, wrapped by a GUI tool.

**Shading**
- **Ramp shade brush:** lighten/darken one step along the view's ramp per pass (the ramp picked in Tool Options, one for the view and shared by every ramp tool; a layer carries none), using the ladder A → B░A → B▒A → B▓A → B. Never mixes colours that aren't ramp neighbours.
- **Form shading brush** (`brushes/form.h`): paint over a masked shape and it shades as a dome, cylinder or flat plane lit from the scene light vector, with rim light and specular as toggles.
- **Soft brush / falloff** (`brushes/soft.h`): size and hardness; the falloff becomes banded rungs 1–3 cells wide, not noise.
- **Ramp gradient tool:** linear and radial, cell-banded or 4×4 Bayer (the Bayer's cells exempt from the speck rule, below), along a ramp. As built: the ramp is the view's, as for the brushes, not the layer's; a Pixels layer is refused (its rungs are shades, which a pixel layer can't hold), the press is declined and the status bar says why, and a text layer is painted as a cells layer.
- **Blend/smudge** (`brushes/smudge.h`): pulls ramp values along the stroke and re-quantises to the ladder.
- **Despeckle brush** (`brushes/despeckle.h`): majority filter that removes isolated cells and stray shade fallbacks (the lint's speckle).

As built (phase 2b, part A):
- **Scene light:** the Brush dock's light direction (`BrushSettings::lightDirection`, a `Compass` point), north-west (top left) by default, the one light the rim-light brush also uses. It is a direction on the canvas, a row counting as two columns, raised towards the viewer (z 0.6) for the dome and cylinder. Until layers and a project file exist it is per session, not saved; the project format should keep it with the document so every form in a piece is lit alike.
- **Form:** `formRungs` gives each cell of a shape its rung. Each cell's depth into the shape is its exact distance to the nearest cell off it (`edgeDistance`, a row counting as two columns, a linear-time distance transform). A dome is a ball whose surface meets the shape's edge, its normal from that depth; a cylinder is round across the shape's shorter side on screen and straight along the longer, and a light whose part across its axis is under 0.3 of it is tilted to the diagonal it leans to across the axis (west of a pillar, north of a log when it leans to neither), so a light along the axis doesn't lay the whole form in one rung; a plane tilts towards the light. A dome or a cylinder with the glint on gets it as a spot on the lit side: the top rung on the most lit cell, a cylinder's ties going to the middle of its length, and its more lit neighbour in the row, where every cell it lifts above the plane and each of that cell's neighbours is on the shape at the plane's rung, so the glint never reaches the fall, the shadow or the edge; with no room for it there is none, and a plane has none. The form follows the craft rule for a round form: a broad solid lit plane, the whole fall of the ramp crowded at the turn, the shadow side down to black. Each cell's arc is its distance from the brightest point over the surface (the angle between its normal and the light's whole direction, across the canvas and out of it, times the form's radius; on a plane its distance along the light), so a north-west light falls diagonally and a north one from the top, not in columns or rows. The turn is a right angle from the light (on a plane the middle of its span). A try lays the cells nearer the light than the fall's start as the lit plane, solid (the top rung, or with a glint the highest solid rung below it, the glint coming up to the top through a rung a cell round it), then every rung below the plane down to the first above the darkest colour, each `band` columns of arc wide, and past the fall the darkest colour, black on a ramp that starts with it. A cylinder too thin for the ladder to come down to black from its plane has its shadow edge (the cells on its silhouette across the axis facing away from the light) black all the same, which the lint reads as ink ending the gradient; not with a rim, which lights that edge, and not on a ramp that doesn't start with black. Its fall stays spread evenly over the rows it has: the 30×10 log lit from the north-west is b c b a 9 8 7 6 5 then black down every column, detail 2.00, every row along the axis one rung. The ladder (neighbours at most a rung apart), the rim (a rung more on edge cells facing away from the light) and the lint's specks voted out follow. The tries: bands of 1, 1.25, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3, 4 and 5 columns, the fall at the turn or 15% of the span either side of it, then starting just past the light's lit share (below) and 5, 10 and 20 points past it; with the glint (its falloff to the plane none or a column a rung, so it is two cells of the top rung ringed once by the ladder: a 2–5 cell spot at every size, where a wider falloff made a 13–15 column bull's-eye on a large dome, which reads as a disc), then without. **Bounds:** a try is in bounds where its black (the darkest rung) is at most the light's shadow (the cells turned from it) plus ten points and its lit plane (the plane's rung and above) at least the light's lit share (`formLight`: the cells it lights as brightly as the plane's rung, the rungs from black to the top taken as the light from none to full). A try out of bounds is never laid while one in bounds is clean. **The bar** is the detail measure: `lint::detail` (distinct looks over 4×2 windows, a test checking it against measured figures) at a mean of 3.6 looks a window or more and under 15% flat windows, each read as the mean over the 8 alignments of the windows on the form's box (the reading at the box's corner, the usual one for a piece, is reported beside it); besides that the ladder lint clean (every rule but the glyph count and noise, cells off the form counting as no neighbour), the lit plane laid, no rung of the fall wider than four cells along the gradient on the turn, at least two shade glyphs, and no seam (a cell lighter than both its neighbours in the row three rows running). The first try in bounds that clears the bar is the form. When none does, the clean try in bounds that comes nearest is laid (narrow rungs, two glyphs, no seam, the plane, the glint, under 15% flat, then detail), then the clean try that comes nearest; when no try is clean, the brush lays nothing. A form gets tries until they have covered about two million cells, so a select-all of 160×400 lays its first dab in under 0.2 s. **The bar is out of reach on most forms under the bounds**, and the brush says so by laying the nearest try in bounds. Before the bounds the brush cleared more by laying black far past the shadow (a 36-across disc 61% black against a 24% shadow). A survey of 384 forms (discs 12–64 across, two ovals, ten cylinders, three L shapes as domes and cylinders; all eight lights; glint on and off; steel) reads, before → after: forms over the shadow plus ten points 159 → 0; clearing the bar at the corner 138 → 35 and over the 8 alignments 103 → 49 (48 clearing the whole bar); by group, over the 8 alignments, discs 56 → 29 of 96, ovals 6 → 0 of 32, cylinders 23 → 16 of 160, L domes 16 → 2 of 48, L cylinders 2 → 2 of 48; median detail 3.26 → 2.62 and flat 4.2% → 9.6%. Discs 12 and 20 across clear (14 of 16 lights with each glint setting); from 28 across none does: the lit plane a light lights as brightly as the rung below the top is a third of a dome, one look a window, so a 36-across disc reads 2.8–3.0 with 11–12% flat from the north-west (black 33% against a 24% shadow) and a 64-across one 1.9 with 34% flat. The fix is a smaller form or a longer ramp. **Large domes miss the detail bar:** the glint as a spot breaks up less of the lit plane than the rings did, so glinted domes from about 28 across read lower still (a 36-across disc lit from the north 2.34 with 23% flat). Over discs 12–64 across in fours, every light, dome and cylinder, glint on and off (448 forms), round 6 leaves 56 clearing the bar (all domes, none over 24 across), as before, with the median detail 2.36 → 2.33 and flat 11.5% → 13%. With iCE off the form's lightest rung is the last one it can write (no bright background) running unbroken up from the darkest, so the ladder never jumps a rung it can't write.
- **Painting the form:** the brush paints the selection's shape (the cells the stroke may paint), or with nothing selected the cells the stroke has covered so far, each cell at the strength of its strongest dab; strength moves a cell that share of the ramp's rungs towards its tone. With nothing selected a stamp that grows the shape works the form out near it, over the covered cells within two dab widths and six columns of its footprint across and a dab's height and three rows up and down (every solve runs on the box of its shape and a ring of cells, not the canvas), and a stamp that grows or strengthens the shape lays again the covered cells within a dab's width and two columns of its footprint and half its height and a row: all the cells the form was worked out over when those hold every cell the image has covered, so a small shape is the whole form as before. A cell further off keeps what the stroke left it, so a long stroke is shaded as a run of forms along it, and a stamp's work is bounded by its size: a test holds a size-5 stamp's most work over a wave to within a quarter between the middle and the end of a 160-column stroke and the end of a 400-column one (32,535, 34,550 and 38,726 cells; laying the whole form again took 673,961, 1,106,897 and 2,891,174), and with a selection its most work after the first dab the same 48 cells on 160×100 and 400×160 (16,879 and 66,514 when the selection's shape was built and compared, and every covered cell laid, at every dab). What the dab actually changes is checked, not the form: the stroke's lint guard (`StrokeLint`, below) reads what its paints would add to the canvas as the stroke began, and while they would add a finding the changed cells within a cell of each new one (all of them when none is that near, as for one glyph and noise) are taken back to what the stroke had left them, so a part dab, a lone selected cell, a one-cell dab or a step that meets an edge far along its row adds nothing, and a dab that can lay nothing leaves the stroke as it was rather than taking back what earlier dabs laid (#723: a size-14 square drag on a flat field went from 149 changed cells to 0 mid-drag, and from 113 to 17; a test now holds every dab to at least half the most the stroke had changed). That costs order independence: replaying a stroke's dabs paints what playing them once does, but another order can paint otherwise (190 of 600 random strokes on small canvases, and 172, 175 and 192 of 400 under mirror X, Y and both; none adds a finding in either order). It works out the selection's form at a stroke's first dab, keyed on the shape's cells and the lightest rung, so a new selection or a cell gone transparent under the lock is a new form. Timed on the dev machine (RelWithDebInfo; the hidden `shading brush timings` test), mean / 95th percentile / worst, the first dab included: a wave of 60 unjoined size-8 dabs takes 18 / 48 / 59 ms a dab on 160×100 and 11 / 24 / 33 ms on 400×160; random walks of 40 joined size-9 dabs at half strength (a dab a line of up to five stamps, each a solve) 52 / 183 / 410 ms and 54 / 186 / 410 ms, about 80 ms a stamp. Laying the whole form again, a size-9 stamp at the end of a wave across 400×160 worked through 5.6 million cells. A dab whose footprint misses every paintable cell does no form work. Under symmetry without a selection each image is shaded as its own form, from the cells that image has covered, so a symmetric stroke shades as its images would one by one.
- **Soft:** the ramp shade brush with a falloff (`softFalloff`) measured against the tip's own footprint, and over a stroke against the stroke's own shape: the middle moves its strength's share of four rungs, and from the tip's edge in the cells move a rung, two, and so on, each band a column in from the ends of its row and a row in from the ends of its column, so a band is a cell wide wherever it runs. Each band's corner cell drops to the band outside it, so no cell is both in a band's run across the top and in its run down the side: the lint measures a step's width by the longest run the other way of any of its cells, and a stroke whose dabs drop a row puts a step at the end of every band across the top. The outermost band leaves its corners out, except where the shape runs three cells or fewer one way (no step there is wide enough to matter), and a two-by-two tip, all corners, keeps them, so a small tip or a thin stroke still moves every cell. No cell moves further than all four neighbours (a speck). Hardness is the share of the bands the falloff doesn't use; a hard tip (1) moves every cell of one dab its full strength. Cleaning each tip doesn't keep a stroke clean (overlapping dabs at a bend, a pressure change), so the stroke is shaded again after every dab: each cell wants the depth of the strongest dab that covered it, and moves no further than its band in the stroke's shape so far (the bands above, over the union of the dabs; the canvas edge and cells the stroke may not paint are no edge). Hardness 1 means no falloff inside one dab, but neighbouring cells across a stroke still never differ by more than one rung, so a pressure change ramps. After any dab the canvas adds no lint finding to the stroke's start, on any start and however far along a row or column the finding falls, one glyph and noise included: each solve's candidate is judged by the stroke's guard (`StrokeLint`, below) against the canvas the dabs it kept have left, and since that canvas adds nothing to the start, what the candidate adds is everything new. A finding is the start's by the lint's rule (`LadderIndex`, below, the maintainer's ruling on PR #59): by kind and place, whatever its message says; a wide step when the start had one the same way, on the same line, of the same rung, sharing a cell with it; one glyph when the start shaded with one glyph only; noise when it is within the limit or no higher than the start's. So a dab that would shade a field of one solid rung with a single glyph gives way (a field of a solid rung uses no shading glyph), and so does one that would lift the noise over the limit (shaded cells leave the pixel count, so the lonely pixels left weigh more). Where the guard, reading the tones as they stand, finds a skip, a wide step or a speck the stroke made, the lighter press gives way, each finding by its own cells (the cell or run that moved more; a wide step by its whole run, or where none of the run moved by the dab's cells along its line), lowered a rung at a time; where those can't move, the settling cells nearest it do. One glyph and noise, read over the whole canvas, give way by the whole dab. **Lint-clean wins over painting:** a dab whose cells can't settle without a new finding gives way entirely, every cell where the last dab left it (a size-1 dab on a flat field would be a speck; two cells on a gradient a rung a column skip their neighbours whichever way they go). The brush gives way cell by cell from the whole footprint rather than searching for the largest clean part of it, so it can leave a dab blank that a smaller change would take (a size-5 round dab on a gradient a rung every two rows does; on flat fields a test finds no dab left blank that could have painted). Each cell's tone is counted in rungs from its value when the stroke began, so nothing compounds. A dab's solve is bounded by the dab: it shades again only the cells within 9 of the box of its whole footprint, settles the 4 beyond, and reads bands and the ramp with 13 more of context, so the solve's cost doesn't grow with the stroke or the canvas. Each image of a mirrored dab gets a grid of its own (images whose regions touch share one), so a mirror costs its images and not the box between them. The canvas before the dab and the one being shaded are kept for the stroke and only the cells a solve changes are written, so no dab copies the canvas. The work count (`cellsVisited`) counts every cell read or written: the grids' copies, the band and ramp passes, each solve's passes, each rung read, and once a stroke the guard's start (three passes over the canvas). Tests hold it: a size-41 dab's most work a stamp is the same on 160×100 and 160×1000 (within 2%) and under 170 times its window (the dab and 26 cells each side; a dab that gives way takes a pass over the window a rung, and it gives way more rungs now the guard reads one glyph, noise and wide steps sliding along their line: 159 windows at most under round 7, where round 6 took under 100; a dab that lays nothing is solved a second time, below, and 134 at most since round 9); a mirrored dab within 10% from 160×100 to 1600×100 (X) and 160×200 to 1600×2000 (X and Y); and a wave of 60 size-8 dabs growing at most linearly with W + H over 80×40, 160×100 and 400×160. The guard's judge costs O(W + H) a changed cell (the index's update along the rows and columns through it, and the lines the start had a wide step on read whole), once a step of the give-way loop, so that part grows with the canvas's sides, not its area; taking the guard at the stroke's first dab is a whole-canvas pass. **A stroke never takes paint off:** under Lighten no cell is left darker than it has been during the stroke, and under Darken none lighter; where a solve would leave a cell short of its best, the cell is held there and the solve runs again. A dab that wants nothing deeper than the stroke already wants is skipped, so going back over a stroke at the same or a lighter press changes nothing (tests: Codex's two round-6 repros, and 120 random strokes that scrub back and forth). A dab that lays nothing, and only such a dab, is solved once more, round the dab even when the whole stroke is re-shaded, with the wants of the stroke's cells it hasn't moved, outside its own footprints, set aside, so an earlier dab that laid nothing holds no later one back; they are put back after, so dabs that are each a speck alone still make a line together (#727: on a fire dome with iCE off, a size-3 Darken dab at (29,12) lays nothing and one at (31,13) alone darkens row 13 to 444433344; the first then the second laid nothing, and now paints what the second does alone). That costs order independence: whichever of two overlapping dabs lands first keeps the paint it laid, so dabs within 4 cells of each other can give different tones in different orders (on a row 1 1 1 3, dabs A then B give 1123 and B then A 1114). Tests play 300 strokes of such dabs in one order and the reverse and 300 in shuffled orders (pressure to strength, sizes up to 41): no dab adds a finding, and fewer than one stroke in five differs (48 and 51 of 300 on the seeds the tests use; 43 and 43 in round 8, which solved nearly every dab twice; 49 and 51 before a dab that laid nothing stopped holding later ones back, 46 and 52 under round 6's guard). Under a mirror (X, Y, or both) a dab's images are shaded in one solve, each finding gives way by its own cells whatever the others did, and a cell that gives way takes its images with it, so a stroke on a canvas symmetric about the mirror stays symmetric after every dab (a test runs 300 random strokes, the axis on a cell and between two); the bands relax until no cell moves, so a shape that turns back on itself bands as its mirror does. The per-stroke state is kept only for the cells the stroke has touched. Timed on the dev machine (RelWithDebInfo; the hidden `soft brush timings` test), mean / 95th percentile / worst: a wave of 60 size-8 round dabs takes 0.29 / 0.63 / 2.3 ms a dab over a gradient on 160×100 and 0.25 / 0.61 / 2.0 ms over plateaus; on 400×160 0.34 / 0.32 / 8.4 ms and 0.33 / 0.39 / 8.1 ms, the worst being the first dab, which takes the guard. Joined strokes of 40 size-9 dabs at half strength in a random walk take 0.65 / 2.5 / 8.4 ms a dab on 160×100 and 0.75 / 3.5 / 11.0 ms on 400×160. With a dab that lays nothing solved twice (round 9), Darken at hardness 0 in the hidden `shading brush timings` test, the first dab included: the wave 0.31 / 0.46 / 3.0 ms on 160×100 and 0.37 / 0.30 / 10.9 ms on 400×160, the walks 0.42 / 2.0 / 7.0 ms and 0.54 / 1.1 / 9.5 ms (round 8, which solved nearly every dab twice, took 0.54 / 1.3 / 3.0, 0.55 / 0.74 / 11.4, 0.71 / 2.5 / 8.5 and 0.92 / 2.3 / 11.2 ms). On the same machine round 6's guard, one `addedFindings` a step of the give-way loop, took 4.7 / 10.9 / 14.7 and 10.5 / 16.5 / 16.7 ms for the gradient wave and 12.3 / 53.6 / 123 and 34.5 / 137 / 364 ms for the walk. A one-off `addedFindingsOnce`, which takes an index of the whole canvas, costs 2.0 / 2.3 / 2.7 ms on 160×100 and 9.0 / 11.0 / 11.7 ms on 400×160, about what a stroke's first dab pays. Shading the whole stroke again gives the same tones but for rare cells at the edge of the cells the near solve settles, which it can move where the whole solve, trying the cells further out too, holds them back (a test compares 400 random strokes on 40×24 and 80 on 160×60; over six seeds 2 of 2,400 strokes differ, both before and since round 9 though not the same two, and add nothing; the test pins the two at this head by stroke, cell and rung: seed 2026102304 stroke 297, one cell near 17 and whole 18, and seed 2026102309 stroke 205, four cells a rung apart; a hidden test runs all six seeds). Random strokes on 160×60 (150 over gradients, 150 over plateaus, 150 over flat fields) add no finding at any dab; round 4 let 2 of the gradient strokes and 17 of the plateau ones add one. Giving way entirely paints less: 74 of the 150 plateau strokes changed something under round 6's guard, where 76 did before; under round 7's, which judges by place and run rather than message, 84 do, and 85 since round 8, which solves a dab that laid nothing again (85 at rounds 8, 9 and 10 alike). Single dabs and dragged strokes are lint-clean at every size from 1 to 41, both tips, every hardness and strength (a test sweeps them), and so are 2,000 seeded strokes of two to six points that bend and cross themselves, under constant and changing pressure to size and to strength; 3,000 more, on flat fields, gradients and a form dome, five ramps, iCE on and off, under a selection or the alpha lock, add no finding at any dab. With iCE off a cell moves only as far as the rungs it can write run unbroken from its own, and is left when there is none.
- **Despeckle:** each cell under the brush that the lint calls a speck (`speckFix`, the same rule) takes the rung most of its neighbours share (the nearest on a tie, then the darker), copied from a neighbour that has it (below, above, left, right; keeping the cell's own transparent flag), and the dab goes over its cells again until none is a speck. Cells are read as the stroke has left them, by rows from the top and left to right, turned over (and the neighbours with them) for a flipped tip. Under a mirror the dab's images are cleaned together: a cell and its images are judged from the same cells and set together, the images read as the mirror of the dab, so a stroke on a symmetric canvas stays symmetric (a test runs 400 random strokes under X, Y and both, the axis on a cell and between two), and images that don't meet clean as they would one at a time. Cells that aren't specks, black, the top rung, a cell of the gradient's dither (`lint::ditherExempt`, the speckle rule below) and cells off the ramp are left alone; with iCE off a bright background reads as off the ladder. A fix (a cell with its images) that would add a finding the stroke's start didn't have, by the stroke's guard (`StrokeLint`, below), gives way to the speck's next rung among its neighbours' (the most shared, then the nearest, then the darker), but never to the far end of theirs (the highest for a dark speck, the lowest for a light one) when only one neighbour holds it, and is left undone when every one would: a speck's new rung can make a skip or a wide step, or leave the shading on one glyph. (#727: a rung-4 speck on the steel sphere between 7s above and left and 6s below and right took the tie's 6, which made a wide step, and stayed; it now takes 7. #742: a rung-19 speck on sunset with 17 below, 3 above, 7 left and 0 right, whose guard refuses 17, 7 and 3, took the 0, a hole in a near-white cell; it now stays. #746 and #747: round 9 dropped the near end too, the gentlest fix there is, and left specks a neighbour's rung cleared cleanly; it is tried again. A hidden test, `despeckle speck corpus counts`, gives a size-1 dab to each of the 115,346 specks the lint finds on 4,000 random fields of every ramp, iCE on: 89,591 are cleaned, against 91,537 with no rung left out and 79,979 under round 9's rule. Every speck the far-end rule leaves, 1,946, is one only that far rung would clear, and none is left that another neighbour's rung would clear (a test checks this on 200 fields).) Timed on the dev machine (RelWithDebInfo; the hidden `shading brush timings` test, the form's runs above), mean / 95th percentile / worst, the first dab, which takes the guard, included: a wave of 60 unjoined size-8 dabs over a gradient takes 0.04 / 0.01 / 2.2 ms a dab on 160×100 and 0.20 / 0.01 / 11.3 ms on 400×160; random walks of 40 joined size-9 dabs 0.06 / 0.04 / 2.0 ms and 0.26 / 0.04 / 10.1 ms.
- **The lint at a change** (`lint::addedFindings`): the findings at cells a canvas has that another of the same size, differing only at given cells, doesn't, exactly what linting both whole canvases and comparing finds (a test checks it on random canvases and changes). It reads only the change's reach (`changeReach`): the rows and columns through the changed cells and the cells next to them, each to its ends, plus the rows and columns the width rule reads across them (a step's width reads its rung's run the other way, and a column's step isn't judged where a row reported its cell). `findingsAt` gives the findings from given rows, columns and cells. The brushes no longer use it: each keeps a `LadderIndex` a stroke (below).
- **The stroke's lint guard** (`brushes/guard.h`, `StrokeLint`): the soft, form, despeckle and smudge brushes each take one at a stroke's first dab, from the canvas as the stroke began, and keep it in the stroke's brush state, so it lives and dies with the stroke and is never carried over an undo, a cancel or an edit between strokes. It holds one `LadderIndex` and the canvas as the dabs it kept left it. `judge(after, changed)` returns what `after`, differing from that canvas at `changed`, adds to the stroke's start by the lint's rule; `keep()` takes it, and a judge not kept is put back at the next. Every change a brush writes to the stroke has been judged and kept, so the preview and the committed edit are the canvas the guard holds, and add nothing to the start. The index's `apply` compares with the canvas it holds, not the start, so wide steps need one more read: one that shares cells with a kept one isn't added to the kept canvas, yet may have slid off the start's along its line a dab at a time, so on lines the start had a wide step on the guard reads the line's wide steps whole (`LadderIndex::wideRungs`, O(W) or O(H)) and matches them against the start's runs. A judge costs O(W + H) a changed cell. Tests: two repros round 6's guards painted (one glyph on a solid field, noise lifted over the limit); 240 random strokes of the three brushes, iCE on and off, under no mirror or X, Y or both, read against the stroke's start over the whole canvas after every dab, add nothing (round 6's guards let 18 soft, 2 form and 2 despeckle strokes of them add a finding); and `wideRungs` matches `checkLadder` line by line.
- **Ramp gradient** (`brushes/gradient.h`, the core function): `rampGradient` fills the cells a stroke could paint (the selection, the alpha lock) with a linear gradient from a start cell to an end cell, or a radial one from a centre out to an end cell, between two rungs of the ramp, as one edit, cell-banded (`GradientBanding::Cells`) or with a 4×4 Bayer dither along its band edges (`GradientBanding::Bayer`, #160). A cell's place is its projection on the start-to-end line, or its distance from the centre, a row counting as two columns (so a radial gradient is round on screen). Every rung between the ends is laid in order, each ░▒▓ rung two columns wide along the gradient (two cells across a row, a row down a column), and the solid rungs share the rest of the span equally as plateaus, so a long gradient terraces as hand shading does (solid plateaus, the turn squeezed to 1–3 cells); with no solid rung between the ends the two end rungs take the rest as planes. A radial gradient's plateaus take whole bands, so every ring ends on an even radius: a ring ending between two near the centre (1.5, 3.5 or 5.5 columns out) is a rung four cells wide where it cuts a row. A span too short for the ladder at two columns a rung runs the ladder on past the end cell rather than skip a rung. With iCE off the ladder stops at the last rung short of one that needs a bright background, as the ramp shade brush does. Where the ladder's shades would all show one glyph (steel's 3 to 5: each shade written the way round that keeps its background dark, both ▓), one more rung goes on past the end, or else before the start. A cell left a speck (the first ring of a radial gradient centred a column off the canvas clips to the one cell beside it) takes its nearest neighbour's rung. Places are worked out in double, so ends anywhere an int reaches don't overflow (the tests run clean under UBSan). In a selection or under the alpha lock the fill is fitted to the cells it doesn't paint, as the lint reads them: a field further along the ramp beside the plane of the last rung (or the first) carries the ladder on to the next solid or the field's rung, whichever is nearer; a jump into a field that would be a skip pulls the fill toward the field's rung, a rung a cell in from the edge, found again on the result until no more are needed, and a corner pulled part of the way from one edge is pulled the rest from the other; and a speck against the cells round it takes its nearest neighbour's rung. An area too narrow for the climb is all climb. In a selection the layout and settling passes cover only the box its cells take (`Mask::bounds`), every cell beyond it read as the field: some six visits a cell of the box (`gradientCellsVisited`); with none they are whole-canvas passes.
