- Samples install as directories (#207, 2026-11-02): each is a directory project copied as it is to `<datadir>/blockwright/samples/`, not a `.bwp` packed at build time as #207 first suggested, because packing needs `blockwright-cli` to run on the build host and a cross build (the Windows MSI) has no runnable CLI there. The app opens either form, so a `.bwp` sample dropped in the folder still lists.
- macOS (#20, 2026-10-31): a `Blockwright.app` bundle (`BLOCKWRIGHT_MACOS_BUNDLE`) with the CLI beside the editor in `Contents/MacOS` and the Library, the samples (`Contents/Resources/blockwright/samples`, found from `Contents/MacOS` as on Linux), licences and notices in `Contents/Resources`; macOS 12.0 is the oldest supported (Qt 6.8's). It builds against Qt's own 6.8 build from aqtinstall, with `qtimageformats`, not Homebrew's Qt, and links static libzip and zstd from `scripts/macos-deps`, so nothing in the app is built for a newer macOS than it claims.
- The dmg (#20): `scripts/package-macos` runs `macdeployqt`, adds the notices for what it bundled (#280), signs ad hoc and makes the disk image with dmgbuild, with an icon and a drag-to-Applications background drawn by `packaging/macos/make-art.py`. It is signed ad hoc and not notarized until funkpower has a Developer ID, so Gatekeeper blocks the first run of a downloaded copy, the app's and the bundled CLI's alike, until the quarantine flag is removed from the bundle (`xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine`); `packaging/README.md` and the cask's caveat say so.
- The dmg's notices (#280): after `macdeployqt` and before signing, so the signature seals them, `packaging/macos/third-party` adds to `Contents/Resources/doc` (beside the install's `LICENSE`, `NOTICE` and font and icon notices) `THIRD-PARTY.txt` from `packaging/macos/`, filled in by `packaging/qt-third-party` with the bundled Qt's version, source link, frameworks and plugin directories and the libzip and zstd versions `scripts/macos-deps` recorded; the LGPL v3; libzip's and zstd's licences, which `scripts/macos-deps` keeps in `<prefix>/licenses`; and in `qt-sbom/` the SBOM of each Qt module the bundle carries a framework or plugin of. A Qt framework or plugin it can't place in a module, or any missing version, SBOM or licence, fails the build, adding nothing. `macos_third_party` checks it on Linux against fake Qt prefixes and bundles, including that no run leaves its temporary directory behind; `qt_third_party` checks that `packaging/qt-third-party` writes values literally, refuses an `@` in one and fails on any placeholder left over.
- Homebrew (#20): a cask in funkpower's own tap (`funkpower/homebrew-tap`), generated with each dmg; the official homebrew-cask takes only notarized apps.
- Linux source releases (#20, 2026-10-31): `scripts/release` makes the release tarball with `git archive` of the tag (tracked files only, the same bytes from the same commit), and the tarball carries its commit in `packaging/tarball-commit` (`export-subst`) so a build of it never asks git. Distribution builds have no network: `BW_FETCH_CATCH2=OFF` uses the installed Catch2 3 or stops, and `BUILD_TESTING=OFF` needs none. A Linux install carries a desktop entry, hicolor icons scaled from the macOS icon, and AppStream metadata with the id `online.pumpin.funkpower.blockwright`. The AUR packages are `blockwright` (the release tarball; `scripts/release` fills in its version and checksum) and `blockwright-git`.
- Debian, Ubuntu and Fedora (#20, 2026-10-31): `packaging/debian/` (debhelper compat 13, `dh` with CMake) and `packaging/rpm/blockwright.spec` build the release tarball and run the tests offline against the distribution's Catch2 3. The layout tests measure in the bundled Noto Sans (`third_party/noto-sans`, moved there from `tests/data/fonts` by #295), registered under a family of its own (`Bw Layout`), so no build root's Noto Sans can shadow it whatever revision it is; the packages need no font build dependency for them. They need Qt 6.7, the minimum (#263): Debian 13, Debian testing, Ubuntu 26.04, Ubuntu 26.10 and Fedora 45 are built; Debian 12 and Ubuntu 24.04 (Qt 6.4.2) are not packaged natively and get the AppImage. The RPM's `%license` carries every licence and `NOTICE`, so an install without docs (`tsflags=nodocs`) keeps them. Catch2 before 3.8 discovers tests through a CMake list, so no test name may hold an unmatched square bracket (`catch_names`). Both binaries have manual pages (`packaging/linux/*.1`). No apt or dnf repository yet: the packages are release assets.
- AppImage (#20, 2026-10-31): `scripts/package-appimage` bundles Qt and its libraries with linuxdeploy and its Qt plugin (tagged releases pinned by sha256), built on Debian 12 so it runs from glibc 2.36 up, with Qt's own 6.8.3 build from aqtinstall (Debian 12's Qt 6.4.2 is older than the Qt 6.7 minimum, #263). One AppImage carries both programs: it runs the editor, and `blockwright-cli` under a `blockwright-cli` symlink or with `--cli` first (`packaging/linux/AppRun`). It bundles Qt's Wayland shell-integration, decoration and graphics-integration plugins (linuxdeploy's `waylandcompositor` module), and its check starts the editor under a headless weston. Licence notices go in `usr/share/doc/blockwright/`, as the MSI's do: Qt's LGPL and SBOMs, ICU's licence, and the Debian copyright file of every other bundled library, each listed in `bundled-libraries.txt`.
- Flatpak (#20, 2026-11-15): `packaging/flatpak/online.pumpin.funkpower.blockwright.yml` on the KDE runtime 6.11 (Qt 6.11, the newest `org.kde.Platform` 6.x on Flathub), with zstd built static (the runtime's has no CMake config, which `find_package(zstd CONFIG)` needs) and libzip as modules pinned by sha256, Blockwright last with `BUILD_TESTING=OFF`; flatpak-builder records each module's licence in `share/licenses/<app id>/`. `scripts/package-flatpak` builds the commit exported with `git archive` (so `--version` has it, as a release tarball does) into a single-file bundle, `blockwright-<version>-x86_64.flatpak`, whose runtime comes from Flathub. finish-args: `wayland`, `fallback-x11`, `ipc`, `dri` and `--filesystem=home`, not portals: a portal grants only the file picked, and projects, reference images, generator scripts and the scenes an imported project names by absolute path sit beside each other, and the CLI takes paths; no network. The user Library and settings persist in `~/.var/app/<app id>/` (GenericDataLocation there), apart from a native install's. In a Flatpak the GUI's desktop file name is `FLATPAK_ID`, the name of the exported entry, rather than the name it runs as. Generators run inside the sandbox with the runtime's Python. Not published to Flathub yet; `flatpak_manifest` lints the manifest statically in ctest, and the build itself is a release step.
- FreeBSD (#288, 2026-11-04): a port in `packaging/freebsd/` of the release tarball, built with the base clang against packaged Qt 6, as on Linux (the `UNIX AND NOT APPLE` install block applies unchanged), and a CI job on FreeBSD 14. Timing limits in the tests get headroom for a slower machine rather than per-platform values. `MAINTAINER` stays unset until the port is submitted. OpenBSD and NetBSD are out of scope: `posix_spawn_file_actions_addchdir_np` needs a fallback on OpenBSD, and neither has a runner.
- Offscreen tests on a Mac (#20) model a Mac's screen, not the offscreen platform's: 72 dpi (the base Qt's styles scale from on macOS), the 13 pt system font, and popups that never become the focus window, as Cocoa never makes them key.
- Message box titles on a Mac (#20): macOS shows a message box no window title (`QMessageBox::setWindowTitle` sets none), and most boxes' text leans on theirs ("Open Scene" over "Could not open %1"). The app's boxes go through `app/message_box.h`, which on macOS makes the title the box's text, its bold heading, and the text its informative text, as Apple's alert guidelines have it; elsewhere it calls Qt's own static functions, so Linux and Windows boxes are unchanged. The unsaved-changes prompt, titled only "Blockwright", and the generator trust question, which carries its own informative text, are left as Qt makes them.
- UTF-8 `.ans` (#302, 2026-11-03): read by detection, not by asking (§8): a byte order mark, or all high bytes well-formed UTF-8 with at least one three- or four-byte sequence; otherwise CP437, so no CP437 file reads differently. A bad byte in a file forced to UTF-8 is one U+FFFD. SAUCE stays CP437. Files Blockwright writes for itself (bundled stamps, generator output) are always CP437 (snapshots since #336 follow the project's encoding, and a user-library stamp CP437 can't hold is UTF-8). Writing (part 2): `convert` and `shade` take `--output-encoding`, not the read option `--encoding`, which applies to every input of eight commands and has an `auto` that means nothing for output; CP437 stays the default, so no existing command's output changes. SAUCE stays CP437, as its specification defines its text fields, and every reader of SAUCE expects that. A byte order mark only when the bytes would not say UTF-8 on their own: most viewers of UTF-8 art don't want one, but without it ASCII-only or é-only output would read back as CP437. The app saves in the encoding the document was opened in, with a UTF-8 filter in Save As to change it. App (part 3, done; #302 closed): an encoding choice at Open, as filters, since a file detection reads wrongly needs a way in; Reopen with Encoding beside it, because a platform dialog may show filters with the same pattern as one; the encoding in the status bar; C1 controls unwritable in UTF-8, as in the IRC export, because `C2 9B` is a CSI to a terminal that honours C1; a project keeps its `.ans` encoding in an optional manifest key, not a new version, as it changes nothing a project shows. Wide and combining characters stay one cell per code point; UTF-8 generator output and lint's echo of control characters are left for later.
- IRC target (#350, 2026-11-04, slice 1): a document is drawn for ANSI or IRC (§6), a preference, not art. Under IRC the palette has mIRC's 99, stored as exact RGB so a cell needs no new colour kind; an RGB colour is never folded by iCE for IRC; lint judges an RGB cell by the nearest of the 16 and swaps `no-cp437` for `ans-loss`. An `.ans` (or XBIN or BIN) that quantises a colour counts it as a loss under either target, which #350 calls a fix: before, a 24-bit colour from the eyedropper was saved as the nearest without a word. Strikethrough, reverse, monospace and `^D` are not in v1; attribute bit 4 is kept for strikethrough. Opening an IRC `.txt` is a later slice. Slice 4, a `.txt` from the canvas for Publish and Send, went with them (§9).
- Bold, italic and underline (#350 slice 3a, 2026-11-04): attribute bits 1–3 (bit 4 kept for strikethrough), project format 5.0, the encoder's `^B ^] ^_`, `decodeLine` and the sender port, the renderer, `wasted-format`, `formattingDropped`, and the palette dock's B/I/U toggles written by the glyph brush and picked by the eyedropper (§4's palette dock, §6, §8, §9's lint). Choices made, smallest sound option each: the encoder puts toggles after a colour code and closes none at a line's end; it writes formatting that draws nothing rather than dropping it (the lint reports it), so a cell exports as it holds; `fit` leaves a formatted cell alone rather than merging it; the search's slack is 9 only on rows with formatting; `wasted-format` is a warning under either target; `.ans`, XBIN and BIN count formatting as lost under either target; the reserved strikethrough bit is refused even in 5.0; only the glyph brush writes the pen's formatting (the tools that write colours, not glyphs, leave it), only under IRC and not on a Pixels layer; the pen's formatting isn't kept between runs. Slice 3b (2026-11-05, after #334 slice 8): typing writes the pen's formatting under the same rule as the glyph brush, only under IRC and not on a Pixels layer; `typeCluster` and `replaceText` write whatever `Pen::attrs` holds, and the editor passes none outside that rule, so the pen keeps its formatting across a switch to ANSI and back.
- Unicode glyphs under IRC (#350 slice 2, 2026-11-05): one typing policy (`TypingPolicy`, `typingPolicy(Target, TextEncoding)` in `target.h`), Unicode under the IRC target or a UTF-8 `.ans` and CP437 only for ANSI with CP437, which `isTypeable`, `typeGlyph` and #334 slice 8's `TypingOptions` (so `typeCluster`, `typeMarks` and `replaceText`) take; four Unicode F-key sets after Moebius's 16, offered only under Unicode (§4, Keyboard mode); the refusal names the target and encoding (`★ has no CP437 code; not typed (target: ANSI, CP437)`). Choices made: the Unicode policy types what slice 8 types in a UTF-8 document, wide pairs (中 under IRC with a CP437 `.ans`) and clusters included, and refuses with slice 8's reasons, `… in IRC text` for `… in a UTF-8 .ans` under the IRC target; `TypingOptions` holds the policy, not an encoding, since an IRC document typing Unicode may still be saved as a CP437 `.ans`. A Unicode set kept from an IRC document reads as set 6 in an ANSI/CP437 one without being forgotten. ★ and ☆ are 16 columns in Unifont though the width table counts them one cell; they draw squeezed into it, as any whole cell with a wide bitmap does. Live text layers (§15) and the glyph brush's glyph are unchanged: the brush takes whatever F-key glyph the set offers. Replay's `keyboard` step takes the policy too (#366's review): it had validated with the CP437-only `isTypeable(char32_t)` code point by code point, so an IRC or UTF-8 document refused there what the editor types; it now splits each line into clusters and types them with `typeCluster` under `typingPolicy(target, ansEncoding)`, and its refusal names the code points and the target and encoding.
- #360's review (2026-11-04): the encoder's ways to draw a cell (`ways()`, `solidColour`) make no substitution for a cell in any formatting, since italic shears a block off its background and bold widens a half block, not only underline; the shade tools (Shade, Ramp Shade, Soft, Smudge, Form Shade) keep a cell's formatting; an underlined blank draws, by one predicate (`attr::showsOnBlank`) that paste, the encoder's trim and the trim lint share; `wasted-format` compares the rows the renderer draws (`formatRows`, `core/glyph_rows.h`) with and without each bit at widths 8 and 9, so bold on ▀ is reported and italic on █ is not; a project before 5.0 opens with every attribute bit but blink cleared and a status-bar count, where it was refused (the old `0xA0` round-trip fixture is one such file), while 5.0 and later still refuse an unknown bit.
- Wide and combining characters (#334, 2026-11-03, slices 1–8 of 8 built, §16): a cell holds a cluster (a base code point and the zero-width ones after it), and a wide one takes a lead cell and a continuation cell, stored as such so a later width table can't move a saved piece's columns; a lead is a cell whose right neighbour is a continuation, so a wide glyph alone in one cell (every one in a project saved before this) is an ordinary one-cell glyph that nothing erases. Width is glibc's `wcwidth` rules per code point, from Unicode 18.0 with four named differences, not grapheme clusters and not Qt's tables: it is the cell grid of the terminals most IRC clients that keep art's columns run in, at the cost of drawing emoji sequences wider than grapheme-joining terminals do and of columns that proportional-font clients don't keep, which lint notes. Every CP437 code point is narrow. `Cell` stays 20 bytes, with clusters interned in a bounded process-wide pool and never written by id; `.cells` version 2 and project version 4.0 only for a layer that holds a pair or a cluster. It serves reading UTF-8 art and writing UTF-8 `.ans` and IRC text.
- UTF-8 through snapshots and stamps (#336, 2026-11-04, slices 1–3 of 4 built, slice 4 dropped; slice 3, for Publish and Send, went with them, §9): Slice 1: snapshots follow the project's `ansEncoding` (`takeSnapshot`, Take Snapshot, `cli snapshot --output-encoding`), since nothing outside Blockwright reads them and a CP437 round of a UTF-8 piece loses its wide glyphs; a CP437 project's snapshots are the bytes they were. Slice 2, UTF-8 stamps in the user library, follows the maintainer's ruling on #336's questions (#336 comment 9457) and is the next entry.
- UTF-8 stamps in the user library (#336 slice 2, 2026-11-04; the maintainer's ruling on #336's three questions): a stamp is written as UTF-8 only when CP437 can't hold its cells, and then always with a byte order mark; it is read as UTF-8 only with the mark; the bundled library stays CP437; an older build may list such a stamp as a problem but must not misread it. The mark alone doesn't guarantee that last (an older build's CP437 read can cost the same `ircBytes` when the widest row is ASCII), so a UTF-8 stamp's sidecar also carries `"encoding": "utf-8"`, a key older builds refuse; the mark and the key must agree (§4, Library implementation notes). A cluster CP437 composes to a byte also makes a stamp UTF-8, so a cluster round-trips.
- Resizable Library stamps (#345 slice 2, 2026-11-04; no maintainer ruling on the issue, so each choice is the smallest that holds and is open to change): the slice is one sidecar key, `"insets"`, its length saying frame (4) or band (2), rather than a second file or a `kind` field; older builds refuse the key, which is what keeps them from placing a frame they can't resize. The middle is always drawn and the stamp's blank cells stay transparent through transparent paste, so a frame stamp has no separate fill setting. Repeats are anchored at the top-left, as `drawFrame`'s are, so the far end may cut a pattern part-way. A stamp holding a wide character can't be resizable. The handles are pixel squares on the float's outline in the Select tool (a `ToolEvent` now carries the pointer's view position); a float lifted off the canvas or pasted is never resizable, only a placed stamp. A flip or rotation turns the saved stamp and slice with the float, and a re-cut after it starts from them. The sidecar read gains a 1 MiB limit through `readRegularFile(path, limit)`, the same change #358 makes, so the two merge without a conflict in it.
- Glyph set 21, the sparkles (#399, 2026-11-07; the maintainer's ruling: a new F-key set 21 that holds ✦ and the other missing ornaments, the existing sets and keys unchanged): F1–F10 are ✦ ✧ ✶ ✷ ✸ ✹ ⋆ ✱ ✺ ❖, the list #399 proposed with ✺ for ✳. No set held any of them. Each is one code point, narrow by the width table on every receiver the table knows (`widthVaries` false, `joinMask` 0), from Unicode 1.1 (⋆ 1.1 too), and none has the Emoji property or East Asian width Ambiguous: ✳ ✴ ❇ are Emoji=Yes, which a client with a colour emoji font may draw as a two-column emoji, and ✽ is Ambiguous, which a CJK-locale terminal draws two columns wide, so all four are left out. None has a CP437 code, so the set is Unicode-only like 17–20: hidden by the CP437 policy, offered in IRC and UTF-8 documents, bindable while hidden with `offeredWhen` (#400). Unifont draws all but ⋆ 16 pixels wide, so the editor squeezes them into a cell as it does ★☆; IRC clients draw them in their own font in one column. The slots are all filled: the theme had ten good glyphs, so the blank slot set 7 uses was not needed. It has no default key: Ctrl+Shift+F1–F10 are sets 1–10's, and Quick tool keys (§11) leave the rest unbound; it is reached from the Glyphs menu, the glyph bar's list, or by stepping, which goes 20 → 21 → 1 under the Unicode policy and never reaches it under CP437. As the last set it changes no stored index (`glyphSet/index` 0–19 mean what they did, 20 is now set 21 and is kept under CP437 as 16–19 are) and no action name, so kept shortcuts load as they were.
- Measuring IRC lines as the ascii script sends them (#364, 2026-11-05; no maintainer ruling on the issue, so each choice is the one closest to this plan): the row meter's bar and number, fit, the IRC view, Export IRC Text's refusal and `export --irc`'s refusal all use `irc::txtSentBytes`, so a line the encoder writes in 402 bytes that the script sends as 483 is over everywhere. Choices: fit keeps "text is never changed" (§8), so the issue's ask that fit bring the 80-star row within 407 sent bytes is not met: that row comes back unchanged and is still reported over, and recolouring text to fit is left for a ruling; the meter keeps the written size (`IrcMeter::bytes`) beside the sent one (`IrcMeter::sentBytes`) and its tooltip names both; `export --irc` prints both columns and the summary gives the longest of each; the IRC view always re-encodes a line as `compact` does and, where Pythons cluster a line differently, shows the fewest cells any of them keeps, the counterpart of `txtSentBytes` taking the largest size; `irc::overBudget` (the written size) is kept for callers that ask about the bytes as written.
- Canvas Size (#397 slice 1, 2026-11-06; the maintainer ruled that it comes first, with Crop to Selection and Trim later reusing it, §5): a cells or pixels layer that covers the canvas exactly follows it to the new size, and the cells past the new edges are cut. Every other layer keeps all its cells and moves by the anchor's shift. Keeping the canvas layer's cut cells off the canvas would lose nothing either, but it would leave the document unable to save as its own `.ans` (`flatHoldsAll`) and make the new cells the ground, not the layer's blank, while the step's undo already brings the cut cells back. The anchor starts at top-left, and the odd column or row at the centre goes right or down. The step keeps the stack from before, so undo is exact.
- Canvas Size in a steps file (#420, 2026-11-07; the issue's spec, the rest the smallest choice that holds): `canvasSize` takes `"size": [w, h]` and an optional `"anchor"` by the dialog's names, top-left when left out as the dialog opens. It takes no Tool Options: `"size"` would otherwise be read as the brush's, and a resize has no tool to set them for. The selection logic and the cell width moved from `Document` to the core (`resizedSelection`, `canvasCellWidth`) so the step and the dialog run one path.
- Hidden generator layers (#440, 2026-11-11; the maintainer's ruling, option 2): `run-generators` without `--layer` skips hidden generator layers and says so (`layer N (name): hidden, not run`, on stdout with the other per-layer lines); a hidden one runs only when `--layer` names it. A hidden layer shows in no composite, render or export, so running it changes no output, while its program still runs with the user's rights (§7 Trust) and a failure in it would stop the whole run and write nothing. The app needs no change: it runs only the layer its generator form names.
- Micron export (#475, 2026-11-13; the issue's spec, the rest the smallest choice that holds): trailing blanks are always kept (they show a background the viewer's may not match, and centring counts them); no `#!bg=`/`#!fg=` page header is written; a blink cell without iCE is written with its dark background, as the editor shows it. Round 1 of its review: an italic █ writes its background, by the renderer's `formatRows` (#360's rule that italic shears a block off its background); `export --micron` of a project flattens it without `toAnsi`'s iCE fold, which turned an RGB background into the nearest of the 16 where the app kept it. Round 2: `export --micron` of a flat file opens it with `openAsEditor`, as the app and `cli shade` do, so an iCE-off file's explicit bright background (SGR 100-107, 24-bit) is written bright, not darkened by the parsed iCE-off flag; a project keeps its own iCE setting.
- Publish to Library and Send to WeeChat removed (2026-11-14; the maintainer's ruling): they fitted one person's own setup, a particular ascii-script library and WeeChat host, not a general editor. The File menu, `app/publish.*`, `core/pipeline.*`, their settings, the IRC Budget dialog's note about them and their tests went (§9). The `efnet-weechat` preset (407), the IRC View's cuts as the ascii script sends a line, Export IRC Text, `cli export --irc` and the Library stamps stay. Settings left in a config file are ignored, not migrated.

## 15. Text and text styles

Lettering is hand work in every piece so far: TheDraw faces screened by hand for letters that misread at 1×, recoloured by ramp, given shadows and outlines one at a time. ascii.py already has the common looks as styles (`block`, `3d`, `shadow`, `outline`, `mini`, `rainbow`) and a recolour ramp. The editor does this as Photoshop and GIMP do: a Text tool that makes live text layers, and a stack of effects on the layer, saved as presets.

**Text tool** (toolbox and Tools menu, Ctrl+Shift+X; no single key, and the test that no two actions share a shortcut covers it). A click makes a `text` layer (§5) at the cell and puts a text cursor in it; typing, Backspace, arrows and Enter edit the text, Esc leaves it. The view scrolls to keep the text cursor in sight as it moves, as it does the cell cursor (#222), and shows the top of one taller than the view; a change that leaves it where it was, or ends the edit, doesn't scroll, and neither does an Alt+drag (§5) carrying it with the letters, nor the move landing where the drag showed it. Clicking an existing text layer edits it. Tool Options (§11) holds the face, case (as typed or capitals), letter and line spacing, alignment and a wrap width in columns. The text stays text until the layer is baked, so changing the face or the style redraws it.

**Faces**, one interface whatever the source. A face turns text into two things: a letter mask (which half-block pixels are ink) and, for TheDraw fonts, the glyphs' own source cells (character, foreground, background), which the mask alone can't hold (a TDF letter can contain ░▒▓ and line-drawing cells). For a source cell the mask is the whole cell: both its pixels are ink when the cell isn't blank, where blank is what transparent paste skips (a space or NUL on black without blink, §4); a space on any other background is ink.

| Source | What | Where it comes from |
|---|---|---|
| Pixel faces | `block`: the IBM PC BIOS 8×8 font at one pixel a font pixel, every ASCII character; `vga`: the bundled IBM VGA 8×16 the same way; `mini`: 4 rows of half-block pixels, 1–5 columns a letter (most 3–4) plus 1 of spacing | `block` and `vga` from VileR's Ultimate Oldschool PC Font Pack (CC BY-SA 4.0, already bundled and credited, §14), with the slashed 0 as a credited modification; `mini` drawn for ascii.py by funkpower. Bundled |
| TheDraw fonts (`.tdf`) | Outline, block and colour fonts, read only (§8) | A fonts folder set in Preferences (`~/tdfiglet/fonts` by default when it exists; Clear keeps no folder). Not bundled until each font's licence is checked. The tests use small TDF fonts drawn by funkpower for them |
| FIGlet (`.flf`) | Plain-character faces | Later |

- **Face picker:** each face rendered in the current style, with search and favourites. For the text typed, it marks letters a face can't draw and confusable pairs. Its list asks for three faces' height and takes whatever room Tool Options has beyond that (#236).
- **Confusable rule:** two characters of a face are confusable when their one-ink 1× masks, aligned left and on the baseline and padded to the wider, differ in at most 2 pixels, or are identical apart from a one-pixel shift. The check runs on the face's own mask, before styling; the sets screened are the known confusables (`ZB8S5O0I1`, `Z2zMWKAH`, `l23456_`). Bundled faces carry the result as data, user TDF fonts are screened when first loaded.

As built (#18, faces; `core/face.h`, `core/pc_fonts.h`):
- **Interface:** a `Face` gives a glyph per character: an `InkMask` (half-block pixels, `height()` pixel rows, two a cell, the bottom row the baseline row) and, for a face built from cells, its source cells, whose mask is `maskOfCells` (both pixels of each cell that isn't `isBlank`). `layoutText` lays text out from column 0 with the face's own letter spacing, lines a face height apart, `\n` ending a line, wrapped at a width in columns as the fixtures say; it gives the mask, the source cells when a glyph has any (holes between glyphs), the lines and the characters the face can't draw. Case, extra letter and line spacing, alignment and margins belong to the Text tool, on top of this; the face layer has none of them.
- **Wrapping:** the spaces between two words at a wrap are dropped; leading spaces and spaces ending a paragraph are laid out like any character and are no place to wrap; a line holds at least one character.
- **Missing characters** are laid out blank at the width of the face's space and listed by index, for the picker's marks. The bundled faces draw printable ASCII, U+0020–U+007E, and nothing else: `block` and `vga` too, though their fonts hold all 256 CP437 glyphs, so the three faces mark the same characters missing.
- **`block`** is `Bm437_IBM_BIOS.FON` from the pack, 8 columns and 4 cell rows a character, no spacing. The BIOS font's own 0 is already slashed, so the slashed 0 is the font's and no glyph is modified; `NOTICE` says so. **`vga`** reads the table the renderer draws with, 8 columns and 8 cell rows, and keeps its dotted 0 (the issue asks for the slashed 0 on `block`). Both tables live in `core/src/` and `render/` takes the VGA one from `ibmVga8x16()`, so there is one copy and core doesn't depend on render.
- **`mini`** is ascii.py's `MINI_FONT` (1.28.0, funkpower's), glyph for glyph: 4 cell rows (8 pixel rows), capitals and digits on pixel rows 0–4, x-height letters on 1–4, descenders on 5–6, and row 7 blank as the gap between lines. Letters are 1–5 columns (`i` and `l` one, `j` and `r` two, most 3–4) plus 1 of spacing, the space 2 columns, and it covers printable ASCII. Lettering set in the editor's `mini` matches what ascii.py's `mini` style puts on IRC.
- **Confusables:** the shift in the rule is one pixel left, right, up or down (a diagonal step is two shifts and doesn't count), and masks align on their bottom rows. Screened over the three sets, `block` and `vga` have no confusable pair and ship an empty list; `mini` ships S/5, O/0, Z/2 and K/H (its 0 is slashed, but the slash is two pixels off the O). `test_face.cpp` screens each face again and fails if the shipped list differs. No two sets share two characters, so a pair is never reported twice and screening doesn't dedupe; a test pins that. `confusablesIn` gives a face's pairs that a typed text holds both characters of.
- **Fixtures:** `tests/data/text/words.txt`, the four words and the sentence, one a line. At a wrap width of 80 the sentence is three lines in `block` and `vga` and two in `mini` (`the quick brown fox`, `jumps`); every word is one line.

As built (#18, TheDraw fonts; `core/tdf.h`):
- **Format:** Roy/SAC's "TheDraw Fonts File (.TDF) Specifications" (2014), checked against the 1,202 published `.tdf` files tdfiglet and retrofont collect (3,725 fonts: 7 outline, 164 block, 3,554 colour), which all read with no glyph left out (a check run on the dev machine, not a test). Where the fonts and the spec differ the fonts win: nothing follows the last font but, at most, SAUCE; the letter spacing is a count of columns (9 fonts have 0), not the spec's 1–40 scale; the width and height bytes are often wrong, so the rows are read as they are and a row wider than the width widens the glyph; colour fonts are read in character/attribute pairs, so an attribute of `00` or `0D` ends no glyph or row and a `&` is drawn with its attribute as tdfiglet draws it (TheDraw stores it so), but some colour fonts store the descender marker `&` alone before `0D` or `00`, as outline and block fonts do: when the pair reading has no end, breaks the limits or runs past the start of the next glyph's data (or the end of the font's), the glyph is read again with such a `&` alone (of the 95 published colour glyphs with a `&`, 50 are pairs and 45 have it alone, and the reading kept ends where the next glyph's data starts in all 95). `core/src/tdf.cpp` cites the spec and lists these.
- **Outline fonts** are drawn in TheDraw's 10th outline style (`A` ═, `B` ─, `C` │, `D` ║, `E` ╒, `F` ╗, `G` ╓, `H` ┐, `I` ╚, `J` ╛, `K` └, `L` ╜, `M` ╡, `N` ╟), the one Roy/SAC's table gives; `O`–`R` are a blank space cell inside the letter, `@` a hole. All 19 styles draw `A`–`N` as a line and `O`–`R` blank, so the letter mask is the same whichever style the Text tool later offers. Outline and block fonts carry no colour (TheDraw draws them in the current one), so their cells are grey on black; colour cells keep the font's foreground, background (bits 4–6) and blink, blink as `attr::Blink`, apart from the background, as a file read with iCE off.
- **Face:** `tdfFace(font)` is as tall as the font's tallest glyph, descender rows included, every glyph standing at the top with holes below, so the bottom row is the baseline row of the tallest glyph. It draws `!`–`~` where the font has them and a blank space; anything else is missing, and there is no case fallback (case is the Text tool's). Each glyph's source cells are kept, so a later "█ only" rule reads each cell's character. TheDraw fonts have no space: the face's is half the average width of the letters it has, rounded up. **Open:** whether the Text tool wants a fixed space instead.
- **Broken input:** a broken file or font header is a `TdfError` naming the byte offset: no signature, no font, a header cut short, no `55 AA 00 FF`, a name over 12, a type not 0–2, glyph data past the end of the file, a glyph offset past its font's data, anything after a font that isn't another font, `00` or SAUCE's `1A`, or over the limits below. A glyph past the format's limits (over 30 wide or 0 wide, a height byte over 12, over 12 rows, no end) is left out, listed in the font's `rejected` with the reason, and missing in the face. `readTdfFile` refuses a file over 4 MiB (the largest published one is 241 KiB). A file's size doesn't bound its memory, since glyphs can share data (a 4 MiB file could make 625 million cells), so `readTdf` also fails a file of over 256 fonts or whose glyphs come to over 2^20 cells (`kMaxTdfFonts`, `kMaxTdfCells`), each glyph counted, shared or not, as wide as it is and as tall as its font's tallest glyph (what the face pads it to), and checked before the glyph's cells are made. So a file's faces hold at most 2^20 source cells and twice as many mask pixels, besides each font's space. The published files have at most 34 fonts and 132,441 cells as stored (`tdfonts-3.tdf`), 159,786 padded (`colosall.tdf`), and the largest font 15,552.
- **Screening** runs when the face is made. Of the test fonts the outline and colour ones have no confusable pair; the block one, two master rows a cell row, has B/5, 8/0, Z/2 and W/H.
- **Test fonts** (`tests/data/tdf/`): `make_test_fonts.py` draws one master alphabet (`A`–`Z`, `a`–`z`, `0`–`9`, `_`) on a grid 4 pixels wide (5 for M, W, m, w) and 6 high, and makes `bw-outline.tdf` (each pixel 2×2 cells, traced in outline codes, 12 rows with descenders), `bw-block.tdf` (half blocks, 3 rows) and `bw-colour.tdf` (shaded by row down the sea ladder: cyan █, then ▓ ▒ ░ in cyan on blue, then a space on blue, 6 rows; clean under the lint unstyled), every glyph at most 8 columns, spacing 1, and `bw-all.tdf` holding all three. The script's output is the same on every run. The four words are 76, 69, 58 and 70 columns in each.

**Text styles** are a stack of effects on a layer, drawn by the core compositor (§5) and redone whenever the text, face or style changes. Each effect has an on/off switch and its own settings. They are drawn in a fixed order, as Photoshop draws layer effects, whatever order the dialog lists them in, and each reads a defined mask:

| Order | Effect | Reads | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Plate | the text's box | a band of one colour behind the text, a set number of cells round it, for headers and the warning-sign look (recolouring) |
| 2 | Glow | the letter mask grown by the outline | a halo walking the ramp's shading ladder outwards from the colour next to the letters (its brightness) down to the ramp's darkest colour (black for every bundled ramp), one ladder rung a step, so each shade joins ramp neighbours (§10); a counter or gap the halo's edge can't reach without crossing the first ring holds the first ring's colour; drawn on the plate, or on black where there is none |
| 3 | Shadow | the letter mask grown by the outline | a drop shadow (direction, distance in pixels, colour) or a 3D extrusion |
| 4 | Outline | the letter mask | a one-pixel half-block ring, or a ring of cells, in a colour |
| 5 | Fill | the letter mask; source cells | flat colour; a ramp across the letters by row or column that steps through the ladder (solid, then the ░▒▓ rungs, then the next solid), so neighbouring colours are always ramp neighbours, with the ░▒▓ rungs on full ink cells only and a ▀/▄ fringe taking the solid ramp colour nearest its position; one ramp colour per letter (rainbow), with each letter's colour kept off its neighbours' by the outline or a blank column. A row gradient steps by pixel row (two to a cell), a column gradient by cell column. On TDF source cells Fill keeps each cell's character and maps its colours onto the ramp by rank, dark to light, as ascii.py's `recolour` does: black stays black, and the other colours take consecutive ramp steps ending at the top step the Fill reaches, lower where that would merge a cell's two colours (with iCE off a background shows only a dark step), and never one colour over itself where the source had two (#184). A rows or columns gradient on source cells that show one colour besides black (Block big text, a one-colour TheDraw font) runs by position instead, since one colour has one rank and the rank mapping would put every cell on the top step. Each cell keeps its character and its colour takes the solid nearest the cell's rung (a ▀/▄ half in a row gradient, the solid nearest its pixel). Along each run of solid cells the gradient crosses, a step only one cell long takes the next step's colour, or the step before at the run's end, so a step's edge never cuts a lone cell off a stroke (#311). A rainbow on such a face keeps the top step |
| 6 | Shade | the letter mask, full cells only (a TDF source cell only when it is █) | fills full ink cells with the ramp's ladder (§10 `stepRung`); a cell only half ink (a ▀/▄ fringe) takes the step of the cell its ink faces (a ▀ the cell above, a ▄ the cell below) when Shade stepped it: the solid nearest that cell's rung, on a ▒ rung the lighter when the fringe lies on the cell's lit side. Side-by-side fringes of one half, one colour and one letter are an edge, cut at a fringe facing ink Shade left alone or another letter's ink: a fringe on the edge facing background takes the nearest faced fringe's colour, the lighter on a tie, so no end of the edge is left a lone pixel. An edge facing no stepped cell, and a fringe that cuts one, keep the Fill colour. A letter is the character whose glyph drew the pixel (`StyleInput::letters`, from `TextLayout::glyphs`); a layer or big text, which doesn't know its letters, takes each line's runs of inked columns. The nearest faced fringe is found in two sweeps along the edge, so a row costs its width |
| 7 | Bevel | the letter mask, full cells only (a TDF source cell only when it is █) | lights the stroke edges facing the light one rung, by the rim-light rule (§10), with Tool Options' light direction; fringes and colours off the ramp are left as they are |

- **iCE off:** Fill and Glow write each rung as `stepRung` would with iCE off (§10): where a rung needs a bright background they stop at the last rung that keeps the background dark, as the ramp shade brush does, so they never write a rung the ladder lint would read as a skip. A preset whose look needs a bright-to-bright interval (two bright ramp neighbours, such as LightRed to Yellow on fire) says so (`"ice": "required"`). Save as Preset… sets that from `styleNeedsIce`: a switched-on effect can write a ░▒▓ between two bright ramp colours (#310). With iCE off such a preset is shown disabled in the Library and the Layer Style dialog, and its fixtures run with iCE on only. Every other bundled preset runs its fixtures with iCE on and off.
- **Effects stack like layers:** within a styled layer each effect is drawn over the ones before it by the compositor's rules (§5), so where two effects share a cell the later one wins, and with iCE off it keeps its colour and the half under goes dark, as the upper layer does.
- **Effects don't feed each other's masks,** except that Glow and Shadow are grown by the Outline when it is on, so a shadow falls from the outlined letter. The ring stays out of a face's source cells, which the letters cover (a TheDraw glyph's blank cells, an open notch or a counter): a ring there would show nothing and cast a shadow from nothing (#184).
- **On other layers:** the same stack can be applied to a cells or pixels layer. On a pixels layer each ink half-block pixel is one mask pixel; on a cells layer each non-blank cell (as transparent paste reads blank, §4) is a full-cell mask pixel.
- **Lint:** the styled result is art like any other, so the ladder lint judges it. The Layer Style dialog shows its findings live, and a bundled preset ships only if its fixtures pass (below), the 407-byte line limit included: a preset too dense for it at 80 columns (a heavy Chrome or Gold 3D) is thinned until it fits or isn't shipped.
- **Presets** are saved stacks: Gold 3D, Shadow and Outline (ascii.py's), Chrome, Fire, Ice, Neon, Rainbow, Warning sign and Masthead to start, then Sunset (#184) (all eleven ship, thinned: see As built below). They name ramps, not colour numbers, where they can. They are files in the Library (§12): `<slug>.style.json` holding `{"kind": "text-style", "name", "category", "tags", "ice" (optional, `"required"`), "effects": [...]}`, recognised by the `.style.json` suffix and the `kind` field, so the Library scan never takes one for a stamp sidecar. `library list` shows them with their kind, and `library check` validates them (known effects and settings, ramps that exist) and renders each one's thumbnail sample.
- **Layer Style dialog** (Layer > Layer Style…, or double-clicking a layer's effects in the Layers dock): the effect list with switches on the left, the selected effect's settings on the right, a live 1× preview, and Save as Preset… (to the user Library). The dialog also shows the IRC bytes of the widest line as WeeChat's ascii script sends it (`irc::txtSentBytes`, as the row meter measures it, §3) against the project's budget, with that line's size as written beside it, the lint findings and the confusable warning.
- **Undo:** a run of typing (until the cursor is moved, another command runs or the text tool is left) is one undo step, as in a text editor; changing a Tool Options setting, the face or the style stack is one step each. Each stores the layer's properties before and after (not cell deltas, which can't bring back the source text); undoing one restores the text and settings and the exact cells it replaced, and redo draws the new source.
- **Bake** turns the layer's styled result into plain cells and clears its style, for text, cells and pixels layers alike; it is one undo step, and undoing it gives back the live layer with its text, face and stack.
- **Project file (§6):** a text layer's `layers/<id>.json` keeps its text, Tool Options settings, face and style stack. A pixel face is named. A TDF font is named and copied into the project (`fonts/<sha256>.tdf`), so the project opens the same on any machine and with the font file gone; the copy is what the layer uses. A styled cells or pixels layer keeps its stack beside its cells.

**Fixtures** (`tests/data/text/`, funkpower's own):
- **Words:** `ZB8S5O0I1`, `Z2zMWKAH`, `l23456_`, `funkpower`, each on one line, and the sentence `the quick brown fox jumps` with a wrap width of 80 columns. Words wrap at spaces; a word longer than the wrap width breaks at the character that would cross it. Text starts at the layer's column 0 plus its left margin, with the face's own letter spacing. Each line plus its margin on each side must fit 80 columns with nothing clipped, where a side's margin is the outline's width plus the largest of the glow's width, the shadow's distance or the 3D extrusion's depth on that side, plus the plate's padding.
- **Faces:** each bundled pixel face (`block` and `vga` are 8 columns a character, so the 9-character words fit with up to 4 columns of margin a side), and three TDF fonts drawn by funkpower for the tests (outline, block, colour; the colour one with ░▒▓ cells in its letters), each at most 8 columns a glyph and covering `A`–`Z`, `a`–`z`, `0`–`9` and `_`.
- **Per preset, per word, per face, iCE on and off (iCE on only for a preset marked `"ice": "required"`):** the ladder lint passes and every line is at most 407 bytes.
- **Round trips:** save and reopen give the same text, settings, face, stack and visible cells; after reopening, changing the style redraws from the text. Editing the text, then undo and redo, restore the text and the rendering each time. Bake, then undo, gives back the live layer. A preset saved and reloaded from the user Library has the same stack.

As built (#18, live text layers; `core/text_source.h`, `core/text.h`):
- **Source:** a live text layer's `LayerProperties::text` holds a `TextSource`: the text (UTF-32), the face, case (as typed, or capitals: a–z only), extra letter spacing in columns and line spacing in whole cell rows (0–64 each; cell rows so a TDF face's source cells stay on cells), alignment (left, centre with an odd leftover column on the right, right), a wrap width in columns (0–65535, 0 no wrap) and a colour (a palette index, 7 by default), which is what the letters show until Fill (text styles) colours them. The face is a `FaceRef`, a kind and the kind's own fields: `pixel`, named for a bundled face, or `tdf`, a TheDraw font named by its file's sha256, its index in the file and its name (below). The text is at most 4096 characters. A source whose face this build lacks, a setting out of range, or a box larger than a layer the project file holds (65,535 a side, `kMaxLayerCells`) can't be drawn (`textSourceProblem`): it is refused when given to a layer, read or written. The box is measured (`measureText`: `layoutText`'s line breaking without the mask, in 64 bits) before anything is drawn, and `renderText` checks it too, so a short source with a huge box (thousands of wide letters 64 columns apart over thousands of lines) is refused without allocating its mask.
- **Drawing** (`renderText`, through the `Face` interface): `layoutText` with the face's letter spacing plus the source's, then each line placed in the text's box by the alignment, where the box is the wrap width wide, or the widest line when wider or not wrapping. The layer is the box's size at its offset. A cell with both pixels ink is █, one ▀ or ▄, in the colour on black; a cell with none is transparent; a face's source cell wins where it has one. Missing characters are blank, as `layoutText` lays them out. The compositor and `.cells` are unchanged: a live text layer composites its drawn cells as any text layer does.
- **Editing:** a live text layer isn't painted on (`layerPaintable` is false, and the app's `editable()`); it changes only through its properties. `applyLayerProperties` gives a layer new properties and redraws a live one, refusing (before touching the layer) a source on a layer that isn't a text layer or one that can't be drawn. `bake()` turns a text or generator layer into a cells layer and drops the source.
- **Cells and source:** a live text layer's cells are redrawn only when its source changes. A change that leaves the source as it is (rename, lock, alpha lock, visibility, mode, offset) keeps the cells as they are, and so does opening a project: the cells are read as saved, not redrawn, even when the source in an edited file would draw others. That is what a 1.0 reader shows, and §6 lets a minor version add only what an older reader can drop without changing what the project shows, so a 1.1 reader shows the same; the source takes over at its next change.
- **Undo:** `LayerPropsEdit` is a step's properties before and after; `textEdit` (a new text, setting or face), `bakeEdit` and `propsEdit` build one, `applyEdit` applies or reverts it, and the app's layer-properties command holds one, so the Layers dock's changes and a source change are the same step. Every step that changes the source (giving a layer its first one, a new text, setting or face, Bake or dropping it) keeps the cells it replaced: undo puts them back as they were instead of redrawing the source before, so it restores the exact cells whatever drew them, and redo draws the source after. A step leaving the source as it is keeps no cells and touches none. `applyEdit` says whether it replaced the cells, and the app recomposites the layer's cells when it did, whether or not its bounds changed. A keystroke's edit carries its typing run (non-zero); `mergeEdits` folds one into the edit before it when the run and layer are the same, it starts where that one ends and both leave the layer live, which is what the Text tool's undo command will do in `mergeWith`.
- **Project file:** `layers/<id>.json` has `"source": {"text", "face": {"kind": "pixel", "name"}, "case" ("as-typed", "capitals"), "letterSpacing", "lineSpacing", "align" ("left", "centre", "right"), "wrapWidth", "colour"}` on a live text layer only, and the format is 1.1. A 1.0 reader drops the key and opens the layer as cells read as text, showing the same cells; a 1.1 reader opens a text layer without one the same way. The cells are read as saved, not redrawn, like a generator's cached output (see Cells and source). A source on another kind of layer, an unknown face kind, face, case or alignment, or a value out of range is refused by the reader and the writer.
- **Tests:** `test_text.cpp` draws a test face exactly (half blocks, transparency, source cells, capitals, spacing, alignment, wrap) and every fixture word in each bundled face against its letter mask, and takes each fixture word in each bundled face through save and reopen (zip and directory), a source edit and its undo, and Bake and its undo, saving after each. `test_app_layers.cpp` checks the app side: a live layer takes no typing, a bad source is refused, and undo gives back each source and the cells before the first. Both check a layer opened with cells its source doesn't draw: a change leaving the source keeps them, undo of any step gives them back exactly, and the composite follows. A source with an oversized box is refused by every path under a 64 MiB cap on any one allocation.
- **Left for later:** text styles and presets.

As built (#18, TheDraw faces in text layers; `core/font_store.h`, `app/preferences.h`):
- **Face kind:** a `tdf` `FaceRef` names a font file by the sha256 of its bytes, the font by its index in the file (0–255) and its name. `FontStore` holds font files by hash (`FontFile`: the bytes, the font names, and a screened `tdfFace` for each font in file order, all made the first time one is asked for, so opening a project makes no faces for a copy no layer uses); `FontSources` looks a face up in the project's copies first, then the fonts folder, then files a caller holds (the Text tool's kept font), the first holding the file deciding. The face is the file's font at that index only when its name matches. `findFace`, `textSourceProblem`, `makeTextLayer`, `applyLayerProperties` and `applyEdit` take the sources. A source whose font no source holds can't be drawn: `textSourceProblem` names the font, its index and `fonts/<sha256>.tdf`, and says whether the file is missing or holds no such font. Under the cells-and-source rule above, a layer whose font is gone keeps its saved cells: a change leaving the source keeps them, undo puts back the cells it kept, and a change of source is refused before the layer is touched.
- **Project file:** 1.2 (§6). A TheDraw source is written as `"tdfSource": {…, "face": {"kind": "tdf", "file", "index", "name"}}` and its file as `fonts/<sha256>.tdf`, once per distinct file. `Project::fonts` holds the copies read; the writer refuses a TheDraw source whose file `Project::fonts` lacks, and a kept file at a copy's path. A project opens from its copies alone, with no fonts folder and the font file gone.
- **Fonts folder:** Edit > Preferences… sets it (kept in the settings under `fonts/folder`). With no setting, `~/tdfiglet/fonts` is used if it exists, resolving `~` with `QDir::homePath()`; otherwise there is no folder. Clear stores an explicit empty setting, so the default never comes back after clearing. OK preserves an unset setting unless the folder is changed or Browse or Clear is used. The dialog labels the default when in use. The dialog shows the folder, Browse… and Clear, a count of the fonts and of the files not read, and a list of each font with its file and each file it couldn't read with the reason; this Preferences list is the warning for skipped broken files required by #177. `scanFontsFolder` reads every `.tdf` file under the folder (subfolders too, the extension in any case, sorted by path) as `readTdfFile` reads it, records every font variant's name and type without constructing or screening faces, skips a second copy of a file it already has, and lists a file it can't read as rejected with the `TdfError` text, never throwing; no folder, or one that isn't there, is an empty catalogue. The catalogue's `entries()` are `FaceEntry` metadata (`FaceRef`, display name "NAME (file.tdf)" (the relative path replaces the file name only when labels collide, and two fonts of one name in one file add their place in it, "NAME (file.tdf #2)"), type and an owning file reference), what a face picker lists. The folder is scanned when the app starts and whenever Preferences is accepted, changed or not (fonts added to the folder are seen), on a QtConcurrent worker; Preferences scans its list in the background too. A newer request cancels the older scan between files and ignores its result even if it finishes last. Publication and `fontsFolderChanged` run on the GUI thread; the picker and Preferences show "Scanning fonts…" until completion. Each worker owns its FontStore until publication, and never touches it afterwards: the GUI constructs and screens faces lazily for visible previews, marks or an actual use. FontFile's `call_once` protects face construction; FontStore mutation itself is confined to its owning thread.
- **App:** when a text layer takes a source in a font from the folder, the file is copied into the document's fonts (`ProjectExtras::fonts`) as part of the change, so its undo and redo, and the save, don't depend on the folder afterwards. A copy no layer uses is kept for a redo but isn't content: a document of one cells layer is still held whole by a flat `.ans` (`flatHoldsAll`).
- **CLI:** reads a project's TheDraw text layers through its copies (as their saved cells, like every reader) and refuses a tampered copy; a command that writes the project back keeps them.
- **Tests:** `test_app_font_collection.cpp` (scratch-home default present/absent, unchanged Preferences OK retaining the implicit default, explicit Clear even when no default exists, worker execution and GUI publication, out-of-order rescan completion, startup scanning state and all variants, file/type search and type filter, 1,500 synthetic files with 4,500 fonts listing and filtering with zero faces/previews and painting only visible rows; type-ahead and display-text enumeration building no more faces than first paint); `test_font_store.cpp` (store, lookup order, the catalogue of a scratch folder with nested, upper-case, duplicate, broken and cut files, cancellation during discovery and reading of 1,000 synthetic files, colliding labels disambiguated by relative paths and same-named fonts in one file by their place in it, problems and the kept cells without the font; the project: round trip in zip and directory, one copy per file and only those used, a tampered, garbage, oversized or missing copy, a face not in its file, more copies than the limit, a project opened and edited after the folder is deleted, what a 1.1 reader sees, the keys mixed up, and the writer's refusals, faces made only when asked for, an over-4 MiB file refused when loaded and when written; `test_project.cpp` refuses an over-4 MiB copy and a 17th copy in a zip or a directory before reading it; `test_tdf.cpp` counts padded cells); `test_app_fonts.cpp` (the setting, the dialog's list and Clear, OK scanning an unchanged folder again, a copy left by an undone change not counting against a flat save, Edit > Preferences reaching the document and the next window, a folder font copied into the project and edited after save and reopen with the folder gone, and the CLI's `convert`, `export --irc` and `snapshot` on a TheDraw project and its refusal of a tampered copy).
- **Left for later:** listing the project's own fonts in Preferences. Each window still scans and holds its own catalogue, as before; sharing it is deferred. Bundling TheDraw fonts remains blocked until each font's licence is checked.

As built (#18, the Text tool; `app/text_tool.h`, `app/text_options.h`, `app/face_picker.h`):
- **Tool:** Text (Ctrl+Shift+X) is in the Tools menu and has a toolbox button of its own. A click on a text layer that is visible, unlocked and live makes it the active layer and puts the caret at the character nearest the click; a click anywhere else puts the caret at the cell, and the first character typed there (a key, Enter or an input-method commit) makes a new text layer at the cell (`Document::addTextLayer`, one step "New Text Layer", named "Text 1", "Text 2" and so on above the active layer) in the tool's current settings and edits it, so a stray click leaves no layer and no undo step. While the caret waits there, Tool Options shows and sets the settings the new layer will start with, not the active layer's, and the status bar and caret follow them. Typing (key presses and input-method commits, whose replacement offsets, in UTF-16 units from the caret, replace or delete characters before or after it), Enter (a newline), Backspace and Delete edit the source; Left, Right, Home, End, Up and Down move the caret by the laid-out lines; a move to another character, or to the other line where a wrap joins two, starts a new run. An input-method replacement that leaves the text as it was moves the caret, adds no step, and starts a new run when the caret moves. Where a wrap drops no space the end of one line is the start of the next: the caret keeps which line it is on there, so End stays at the end of its line and Home at the start of its own. Escape, another tool or another active layer ends the edit, and so do a new document and any change that leaves the layer no longer live, visible and unlocked (lock, hide, Bake, undo past it), checked again on every keystroke; any other change starts a new run and keeps the caret inside the text. A layer the tool made and left empty again when the edit ends (typing typed and deleted, or undone) is dropped together with every step after its own that only changed it, with nothing left to redo (`Document::dropNewTextLayer`). Escape, another tool or a click elsewhere drop it at once; another layer made active (`Document::setActiveLayer`) drops it from `activeLayerAboutToChange`, before the new layer is active, so nothing can come between; and every other document command drops it from `Document::commandAboutToPush`, which each command that pushes a step emits first, before it reads the document and outside any command, so the command's step never lands after the empty layer's: a new layer (it goes above the layer that was active before the edit), Delete, Raise or Lower of the empty layer (refused, the layer gone), hiding, locking or renaming a layer, Bake Text, a text source set from elsewhere, a format or SAUCE change, the IRC budget and a snapshot. The Layers dock rebuilds its rows after the handler of its own row's signal returns when such a drop changes the layers under it. With no edit open, an input-method commit or a key the edit acts on (one that carries printable text, Enter, Backspace, Delete, or Left, Right, Home, End, Up or Down without Alt) on an active live text layer starts one at the end of its text; any other key (a modifier or a lock pressed alone, a dead key, Compose, a function key) starts none, and in an open edit it changes nothing. The canvas shows a dashed box round the layer's letters and a caret bar while editing, and the status bar the face. On a styled layer the letters sit inside the style's margins (Live text, below), so the box, the caret, the input method's rectangle and the caret a click puts all go by where the letters are drawn, not by the layer's top-left, however the layer got its style. While an Alt+drag (§5) previews the layer at another offset they follow the letters there (`CanvasView::shownOffset`, signalled by `layerShown`), and go back with the letters on Esc or land with them on release (#221). The input method is told the caret's cells as its cursor rectangle (`Tool::caretRect`, a column one line of the face high), at the end of the active live text layer while no edit is open, and asked again whenever the caret moves, the tool changes or another layer is made active; with any other tool it is the cell cursor.
- **Typing runs:** each keystroke is a `textEdit` carrying the tool's run; the app's layer-properties command merges a keystroke into the step before it by `mergeEdits`, so a run is one undo step named "Typing". Moving the caret, a click, a Tool Options change, ending the edit, or the undo stack moving any other way (undo, redo, another command, a save) starts a new run.
- **Tool Options:** the face picker, case (as typed, capitals), letter and line spacing (0–64), alignment (left, centre, right), wrap width (0, shown as None, to 65535 columns) and colour (the 16 palette colours). They show the active live text layer's source, or the settings a new layer starts with when the active layer isn't one, and follow undo. A change sets both the tool's settings and the active layer's source, one step each named for the setting.
- **Face picker:** a model/view list of face entries (`FaceEntry`: a `FaceRef`, a display name, type and a lazily resolved face): the bundled `block`, `vga` and `mini`, then the fonts folder's TheDraw fonts ("NAME (file.tdf)", the catalogue's `entries()`), then the project's copies of fonts the folder doesn't have ("NAME (in the project)"), so a project's fonts can be picked with no folder. The Text tool keeps the file of the TheDraw font new text starts in (`TextTool::keptFonts`), so a font picked before the first keystroke still makes the layer, and is copied into the project, after the fonts folder is cleared or changed or a new document drops the copy it came from; while neither holds it the picker lists it as "NAME (kept for new text)". The tool keeps the file and each listed entry shares it: both a new layer (`Document::addTextLayer`) and a face picked for an existing layer (`Document::setTextSource`) look it up there and copy it into the project, and only a face a layer uses reaches the project. When another face is picked the tool drops the file in a queued call, after the picker's own signal, and tells Tool Options first (`TextTool::keptChanged`), which lists the faces again then; until that the entry stays and can be picked again, so the file remains available for as long as its entry is listed. Tool Options lists them again when the fonts folder is set (`Document::fontsFolderChanged`) or the project's fonts change, holding the catalogue and copies so their faces last; the same entries keep the list as it is. Picking a TheDraw font sets the layer's face as any face, one step, and copies the file into the project (`Document::setTextSource`); at a waiting caret it is the face the new layer starts in. Each entry shows a preview of "Abc" drawn in the face, requested only by the delegate as a row is painted and cached by its `FaceRef` (a TheDraw font's by its file's hash) in a 256-entry LRU cache. Row layout, listing, filtering, type-ahead and display-text queries construct no faces or previews. The text role contains only the name and favourite mark; the delegate requests missing/confusable marks only for painted rows, and tooltips request them on demand. The QListView uses a QAbstractListModel and a uniform two-line delegate size, so scrolling does not measure thousands of rows or allocate an item/widget per entry. A search field filters the display names (including file names) and the font type (`pixel`, `outline`, `block`, `colour`), case-insensitively; a type selector on its own row below the search field can narrow the list too. The ★ button keeps the current face as a favourite in the settings (`text/favouriteFaces`, through `appSettings()`, a TheDraw font by its file's hash, index and name); favourites are listed first and marked ★. For the current text (in capitals when the case says so) each entry is marked with the characters the face lacks (each distinct character looked up in the face, nothing laid out) and with its confusable pairs in the text (`confusablesIn`), for example "confusable S5 O0" on `mini`; the tooltip says the same in words.
- **Bake Text** (Layer menu, no shortcut) is enabled when the active layer is a live text layer and runs `Document::bakeTextLayer`: the core `bakeEdit`, one undo step, which undo turns back into the live layer with its source. (Now Layer > Bake Layer, which bakes styled cells and pixels layers too; below.)
- **Tests:** `test_app_text.cpp` (offscreen): the shortcut, menu entry, toolbox button and options row, and no shortcut clash; a click making a layer and Escape dropping an empty one; an empty layer dropped with its typed-and-deleted or undone typing on Escape, another tool or another active layer, with no redo left; typing, Enter and Backspace as one step, an input-method commit in the same run, undo of the run and of the layer; undo and redo ending the run and keeping the caret in the text; locking or hiding the layer ending the edit; input-method replacements and deletions in UTF-16 offsets; End and Home at a wrap; a new document with the same layer ids ending the edit; caret moves starting a new run; clicking into an existing layer; every Tool Options control setting the source as one step that undo reverts, and the controls following undo; the picker listing the bundled faces with previews, filtering, keeping a favourite across pickers and in the settings, and the missing and confusable marks, also through the dock in capitals, and the missing marks for a long many-line text with no allocation over 4 MiB; Bake as one step ending the edit, and its undo; an unchanged input-method replacement after typing starting a new run; a Tool Options change at a waiting caret showing in the status bar; the empty made layer dropped before each command from elsewhere (New Cells and New Pixels Layer, Delete, Raise, Lower, hiding from the dock, rename, Bake, SAUCE, the IRC budget) with only that command's step left, and a layer holding text left as it is; a fonts-folder TheDraw font (`tests/data/tdf/bw-block.tdf` in a scratch folder) listed with a preview, found by the search, marked, kept as a favourite, picked as one step that redraws the layer in it and copies the file, with undo and redo, typing in it, the project's copy listed and drawing once the folder is cleared, and picked at a waiting caret for a new layer; previews requested on demand and cached; an input-method commit with no edit open editing the active text layer from its end; the input method's cursor rectangle following the caret at a waiting caret, through typing and Home, and at the active layer's end, and back to the cell cursor when another layer is made active, with an update each time; a key that edits nothing (30 presses: Ctrl, Shift, Alt and Meta with and without their own flag, AltGr and Mode_switch with and without `GroupSwitchModifier`, Kana Lock, Caps Lock, Num Lock, Scroll Lock, Super and Hyper left and right, Hangul, Zenkaku/Hankaku, Eisu, a dead key, Compose, F5, Print, Insert, Alt+Left and Alt+Home) starting no edit (a Ctrl+Backspace after it deleting nothing) and leaving an open one's text, caret and run alone, while a printable key and Backspace still start one at the end.
- **Left for later:** the Layer Style dialog (a text layer's style is in the core, #73, but nothing in the app sets it yet), and style presets. (The dialog came with #73, below.)

As built (#73, text styles in core; `core/text_style.h`, `core/style_draw.h`):
- **Model:** `LayerProperties::style` holds a `TextStyle`, a list of up to 7 effects, at most one of each kind, each with a switch and its settings: Plate (colour, padding 0–16 cells), Glow (ramp, width 1–16 cells, brightness 1–16 rungs, each at most the ramp's top rung), Shadow (drop or extrude, one of eight directions, distance 1–16 pixels, colour), Outline (pixels or cells, colour), Fill (flat colour; rows, columns or rainbow on a ramp), Shade (ramp, light direction) and Bevel (ramp, light direction). The list keeps the order it is given in, and the file keeps it; drawing doesn't depend on it. `textStyleProblem` says why a stack can't be drawn (switched-off effects are checked too, so switching one on can't make a stack undrawable); `layerStyleProblem` adds where a style may go: a cells or pixels layer, or a text layer with a source, nothing else.
- **Drawing** (`drawStyle`): each switched-on effect is a layer of its own, composited in the table's order over nothing by `composite` with the drawing's iCE, so the later effect wins a shared cell and, with iCE off, keeps its colour while the half under goes dark (§5). The letters themselves are the Fill layer, or with Fill off the ink pixels and source cells as they are. What each effect reads is in `style_draw.h`. The canvas grows by `styleMargins`: the outline's cell, plus the largest of the glow's width and the shadow's reach on that side (its distance in columns across, rounded up to cells down), plus the plate's padding.
- **Readings this build settles:** a Fill row gradient is one ladder rung a cell row (a cell holds one rung), its ▀/▄ fringes half a rung lighter for a top pixel and darker for a bottom one, so the pixel rows still step; a column gradient one rung a cell column. Both run light (top, left) to dark and stop at rung 4, a line with fewer rows than rungs taking the middle of the ladder. Rainbow's letters are runs of ink columns, so letters that touch share a colour. Glow's ring next to the letters is rung `brightness` and each ring out a rung darker, down to rung 1 (a halo wider than its brightness keeps rung 1 out to its edge); the cells with ink take rung `brightness` too, so a fringe's empty half shows the glow, as `halvesUnder` puts a pixel over a shade. A diagonal shadow moves its distance along both axes. Shade with a diagonal light steps half the sum of both axes' distances from the lit side, never below rung 4. Bevel judges the letters as Fill and Shade left them, on black. Flat Fill on a source cell sets its foreground only; the ramp modes map its colours by rank (as amended by #184, below).
- **iCE off:** Glow and Fill walk each rung from the one before (starting from the ramp's darkest) a rung at a time through `stepRung` with iCE off, and stop short of the first rung that needs a bright background: no rung is skipped, so on fire, whose LightRed–Yellow step needs one, a glow or fill tops out at LightRed. Shade and Bevel step with `stepRung` too, so no effect writes a bright background; tests hold that for every ramp effect on fire, and hold neighbouring glow rings and fill rows and columns to a rung apart.
- **Live text:** a live text layer's style is drawn into its cells when its source or style changes, with the iCE of the step that changes it (the app's `Document::setLayerProperties` passes the document's), and redo draws it with the same. The document checks the style with `layerStyleProblem` before it pushes the step, so a style the layer can't hold or draw, or one whose margins grow a valid text box past a layer's size, is refused with the layer and the undo history as they were; the layer grows right and down by the margins and its offset stays, so the letters move by (left, top) within it. A change of the document's iCE setting redraws it in the same step (`iceEdit`, below).
- **Cells and pixels layers:** the compositor draws their style (`styledAtComposite`) when it has an effect switched on; an empty stack, or one with every effect off, composites as no style. The layer shows `styledCells`, its cells with the style drawn on them, clipped to the layer's bounds, with the composite's iCE, settled against its cells as an edit is so no drawn cell breaks a wide pair (§16); its own cells stay as painted. A cells layer's styled cells composite by its mode; a pixel layer's in the normal mode (it ignores its own) and never over a text cell (§5). A pixels layer's set pixels are the ink, a cells layer's non-blank cells source cells; the whole layer is one line. An edit anywhere in such a layer dirties the whole layer (`dirtyRect`). `compositeRect` composites a rect styling each layer once, and the app's document recomposites, previews an edit on a styled layer and repaints a style change or an iCE toggle through it; `compositeCell` keeps the last 8 styled layers per thread, each keyed by its properties and its cells' `Canvas::revision()` (a number naming the cells as they are, new after any write through the mutable `at()` or `resize`, kept by a copy), so a cell at a time over overlapping styled layers styles each once and checks its entry in constant time, comparing no canvas. Known limit: a styled pixel layer's ▀/▄ cell is drawn on black, so its empty half no longer shows the layers below.
- **Undo and Bake:** `styleEdit` builds the step (a `LayerPropsEdit`), applied and checked as any other; a live text layer keeps the cells it replaced, as a source change does. `bake()` and `bakeEdit` of a cells or pixels layer whose style the compositor draws keep `styledCells` as its cells, both ways in the step, so Bake leaves the composite as it was, except that a baked pixel layer is a cells layer and covers text cells under it; a baked pixel layer takes the normal mode it composited in, whatever its own, and undo gives its mode back; one whose style has nothing switched on only drops the style. A live text layer bakes as before, its styled cells kept.
- **Project file:** `layers/<id>.json` has `"style": {"effects": [...]}`, each effect `{"kind", "enabled", ...}` with its settings by name (`"plate"`: `colour`, `padding`; `"glow"`: `ramp`, `width`, `brightness` (1.8, below; a file without it reads it as the width); `"shadow"`: `form` (`drop`, `extrude`), `direction` (`n` … `nw`), `distance`, `colour`; `"outline"`: `form` (`pixels`, `cells`), `colour`; `"fill"`: `mode` (`flat`, `rows`, `columns`, `rainbow`), `colour`, `ramp`; `"shade"` and `"bevel"`: `ramp`, `light`). A live text layer's style is 1.3: a 1.2 reader drops it and shows the same cells. A cells or pixels layer's is 2.0 (§6), even with nothing switched on, and a 1.x file with one is refused; a Glow in it is 3.0, whatever its brightness, and a 2.x file whose Glow there has brightness ≠ width is refused, while one without the key or with the width's opens. A flat save (`.ans`) of a document whose one layer has a style is an export: the project stays the document's file. A reader checks the list's length before reading it, each value's type and range, every name, and the stack as `layerStyleProblem` does; a writer refuses the same before writing.
- **Tests** (`test_text_style.cpp`): each effect's mask on `funk` in `block`, `mini` and `vga`, against masks the test works out from the plain letters; Outline, Fill and Shade on the `bw-colour` and `bw-block` TheDraw test fonts (the source cells kept, recoloured by rank, shaded only where █); the same picture whatever order the stack is listed in, the later effect winning, switched-off effects drawing nothing; iCE on and off; Shade's fringes on flat and ramp Fills, at a letter's end, at ink Shade left alone and along a row 65,535 pixels wide; the ladder lint clean for a framed stack (Plate, Outline, Shadow, rows Fill on steel) in the three faces and a carved one (flat Fill, Shade, Bevel) in `block`, iCE on and off; style steps' undo and redo; the compositor, `compositeCell` and `recomposite` on styled cells and pixels layers, a styled pixel layer over text in every mode, a cell at a time over two overlapping styled layers held to styling each once (`canvasCellsCopied`), and a warm cell-at-a-time scan of an 80×2000 styled layer held to comparing no canvas (`canvasCellsCompared`), with `Canvas::revision()` kept by a copy or a move and new after a write; Bake of a styled pixel layer in CharsOnly and ColoursOnly through `bake()` and `bakeEdit`, the picture kept and undo giving the mode back; a live style set through the app's document with iCE off, drawn and redone with it off; the app's document refusing an outline that grows a text box past a layer's size, an undrawable style on a cells layer and a style on a generator layer, with the layer, the canvas and the undo stack unchanged; the app's canvas against a fresh composite through painting, the preview, a style change, an iCE toggle, undo and redo; Bake and its undo for all three kinds; save and reopen (zip and directory) with the same composite, 1.3 and 2.0; and every refusal, read and written. A ramp Fill under Shade or Bevel stacks two gradients, two rungs a cell, which the lint flags: a preset has to pick one.
- **Left for later:** the Layer Style dialog and the rest of the app (restyling a live text layer on an iCE toggle, the Bake command). Library text-style entries and the bundled presets came next (below), then the dialog and Bake (below); restyling on an iCE toggle came last (below).

As built (#73, Library text styles and the bundled presets; `core/library.h`, `library/text-styles/`, `tests/test_text_presets.cpp`):
- **Entry:** `parseTextStyleEntry` and `textStyleEntryJson` read and write `<slug>.style.json`; the writer's key order is fixed (`kind`, `name`, `category`, `tags`, `ice` when required, `effects`) and each effect is one line, `kind` and `enabled` first and then its settings by name, so a preset written back is the same bytes. The effects are the project file's (`core/src/style_json.h` is shared by both), and an entry is checked as `textStyleProblem` checks a stack, plus what a sidecar refuses: an unknown key at the top or in an effect, a key given twice in any object, at any depth and however it is escaped (the project reader's `repeatedKey`, shared), an empty name or category, a tag as a stamp's tag rule refuses, `ice` other than `"required"`, no effects. A file with no `kind` is told what a `.style.json` holds before anything else, so a stamp sidecar renamed to one gets that message, not an unknown key. A Fill's ramp must exist even when the Fill is flat (or switched off): the entry keeps it as the ramp the Fill offers when switched to a gradient. A project file still opens a flat Fill whose ramp this build lacks, as `textStyleProblem` doesn't check it there, since it draws nothing. The scan takes each `.style.json` (ASCII case folded) as a text style, never as a sidecar; a stamp named `<x>.style.ans` is a problem, since its sidecar would read as a text style.
