A style's thumbnail sample is `Aa` in `block` (`textStyleSample`); `library check` draws it with iCE on and, unless the style needs iCE, off, and a sample it can't draw or a row over 407 bytes is a problem. Its summary is `N stamps, M text styles, P problems`, each in the singular for 1.
- **Presets:** eight ship, none needing iCE: Gold 3D (brown extrusion one pixel south-east, dark grey outline, flat brown on wood), Shadow (dark grey drop shadow south-west, dark grey outline, flat cyan on sea), Outline (white outline, flat cyan on sea), Chrome (light cyan outline, flat grey on steel), Ice (cyan outline, flat light blue on ice), Neon (light magenta outline, flat light green on toxic), Warning sign (a brown plate, flat black on wood) and Masthead (a blue plate, black drop shadow south-east, flat grey on steel). Each names the ramp its Fill would use as a gradient; the outline, shadow and plate are one colour each.
- **The matrix:** each preset on each of the 5 fixture words in 6 faces (`block`, `vga`, `mini` and the `bw-outline`, `bw-block` and `bw-colour` test fonts), iCE on and off: 480 drawings. Each must draw every character of its word (no character the face lacks), have letter ink, and show it: the drawing must not look the same as the style drawn round no letters, so a plate alone or black letters on black fail. Each is held to the lint's ladder findings (non-neighbour shade, wide rung, rung skip, speckle, fallback speck, one glyph) under its default, as the Lint dock and `lint` run it: every named ramp, the craft pairs, the drawing's iCE. Noise and the craft rules are reports, not faults: plain flat text in `mini` with no style already scores noise up to about 0.2. Each line is held to 407 bytes, each line with its margins to 80 columns, each drawn row to column 80, and the drawing to its margins: drawn with 8 cells to spare a side it draws nothing past them and the same within. A preset read from its file is the same stack and writes back to the same bytes, and a copy in another library scans as the same entry; a text layer in each preset, in each face, word and iCE setting of the matrix, saves and reopens (zip and directory) with the same iCE setting, text, face, stack and cells; after reopening, the next preset's style redraws it from the text and undo gives the cells back; a text edit, its undo and its redo give the new and old text and drawing; Bake keeps the drawing and drops the text and style, and its undo gives back the live layer. A baseline row holds the three TheDraw test fonts unstyled to the same faults (30 drawings), so a preset's finding there is the preset's; the pixel faces aren't held to it, as `block`, `mini` and `vga` with no style already speck (steel, on their grey). Gold 3D is held to its brown extrusion, and in the three TheDraw test fonts each extrusion pixel that shows to a letter's ink pixel or a shown outline pixel one step north-west of it (#184). With `BW_PRESET_SHEETS` set to a directory, `[.sheets]` writes a comparison sheet per face and iCE setting: each word unstyled and then in each preset.
- **Thinned:** the §15 looks that use Glow, Shade, Bevel or a ramp Fill don't pass in every face, so each preset was thinned to flat colours that do. Shade steps a full cell a rung darker; a ▀/▄ fringe in the Fill colour beside the shading was a fallback speck (`vga`), so Chrome and Ice lost their shade. Fringes now take the step of the cell they face (#184) and no face shows a fallback speck, but a shaded Chrome or Ice still fails: `one-glyph` in `mini` and `bw-block` with a light from the north, and speckle in `bw-block` and `bw-colour`, whose half-block source cells Shade leaves alone, so neither gets its shade back yet. Gold 3D didn't pass with its bevel and passes without it. Its extrusion shipped dark grey because a brown one specked as `skin` in the `bw-block` and `bw-colour` faces: the outline's ring under a glyph's blank cells (the `M`'s open notch) still cast the extrusion, which showed under the notch as a lone brown cell between black. With the ring kept out of source cells (above) the extrusion is brown, as decided in #184. A Glow of width 1 is ░ alone, which is a one-glyph finding; width 2 skips against a solid Fill on the same ramp and specked inside letter counters (until #184 Q4, below), and with an outline it is too wide for the TDF faces' margins, so Neon is an outline. No ramp Fill passes in `block`, `mini` or `vga`. A ramp Fill maps a TDF source cell's colours by rank; `bw-colour` has three (black, blue, cyan), which skipped a step on the five- and six-colour ramps (sunset, ice, toxic), so those failed there until #184 mapped the ranks onto consecutive steps (below). Some flat colours fail too: grey under a white, dark grey or black outline, and brown under a red, light red or yellow one, speck in `mini`.
- **Left out (until #216):** Fire and Rainbow. Fire needs a red-to-yellow look: a ramp Fill fails in the pixel faces as above, and a flat red passes the TheDraw test fonts but specks in `mini`, `block` and `vga`, with a bright outline or with none; Glow and Shade fail as above. Rainbow is a ramp Fill and fails in the pixel faces on every ramp, and on `bw-colour` on sunset.
- **Left for later:** the app side: the Layer Style dialog, Save as Preset… to the user Library, applying a Library text style, the app Bake command and restyling a live text layer on an iCE toggle. (The dialog, Save as Preset… and Bake came next, then applying a Library style and restyling on an iCE toggle, below.) Fire, Rainbow and the fuller looks wait for effects that pass: a Shade that passes in `mini` and on a TDF font's own half-block cells, a Glow ring that keeps two glyphs, a ramp Fill that leaves a TDF font's own shading cells to their ladder.

As built (#73, the Library dock's text styles; `app/library_dock.h`, `tests/test_app_library_styles.cpp`): the dock lists the scan's text styles after its stamps, in one category list and one search (`library::matches` takes a text style by name and tags as it takes a stamp). Each thumbnail is `textStyleSample` ("Aa" in the block face, the sample `library check` draws) with iCE colours, as `library list` sizes it, rendered at 1× and put through `stampIcon` like a stamp: the samples (16–19 by 4–7 cells, 128–152 by 64–112 px) are bigger than the 96 × 64 box, so each is averaged down to fit. Thumbnails share the stamps' cache, keyed by the entry's JSON, so a rescan re-renders only a style that changed. The tooltip gives the name, "Text style", category, tags, whether it needs iCE colours, and which library and file it is in. The window tells the dock the document's iCE setting whenever its View > iCE Colours check is synced (on startup, on the toggle and its undo, and on opening a document or project), and with iCE off a style that needs iCE is shown disabled, its tooltip saying iCE colours are off; its thumbnail stays the iCE-on look it needs; a malformed `.style.json` is one of the "files skipped" with the rest. Activating a text style emits `textStyleActivated`, and dragging one carries it as a text style, not a stamp; what the window does with them is below.

As built (#73, the Layer Style dialog and Bake; `app/layer_style_dialog.h`, `tests/test_app_layer_style.cpp`):
- **Dialog:** Layer > Layer Style… (enabled on a cells, pixels or live text layer) or a double-click on the Layers dock's Style column opens it, window-modal. The left list has one row of each effect in EffectKind's order, the order they draw in, each with its switch; the right side has the selected effect's settings, in the core's ranges (plate padding 0–16, glow width 1–16 and brightness 1–16, shadow distance 1–16, colours by palette swatch, ramps by name, light and direction by compass point). Changing an effect's settings switches it on. The style the rows give (`styleFromRows`) keeps the layer's own effects in their order with the rows' switches and settings, then appends each other row that is on or has settings other than the default; a layer that had no style and gets nothing keeps none.
- **Preview:** each change draws the layer alone with the style at (0, 0) the way the compositor shows it (`applyLayerProperties` on a copy, then `composite` over a blank ground), at 1×, in a scroll area whose size hint stays near 36 × 24 lines of text, so a layer taller or wider than that (80 × 200 cells is 640 × 3200 px) scrolls and the dialog still fits the screen. The picture and the bytes of its widest IRC line (`irc::encodeCanvas`, against the project's budget, under it) take the cells as the canvas draws them and the IRC meter measures them (`iceCell` with the document's iCE setting). The lint under them reads the composite as stored, through the Lint dock's own default (`dockLint`: every named ramp, the craft pairs, the document's iCE setting). That is the styled layer previewed alone (at the origin, visible, in Normal mode) through the same lint function and options as the Lint dock, so for that layer by itself it reports what the dock would; it is not a lint of the whole document, whose layer order, blending and hidden layers the dock takes into account. Its findings are listed, and, for a text layer, the pairs of characters its face draws alike (`confusablesIn` on the text in its case, `casedText`, as it is drawn). A style the layer can't hold or draw (`layerStyleProblem`, a glow wider than its ramp's rungs among them) is named, and OK stays off. The layer itself isn't touched until OK.
- **Undo:** OK sets the style through `Document::setLayerProperties`, one step "Layer Style"; Cancel, or OK with nothing changed, pushes none.
- **Save as Preset…** asks for a name, category and tags (the Save to Library form without its CP437 rule) and writes the style to the user library with `library::writeTextStyle`: a new `<slug>.style.json`, `<slug>-2.style.json`, … at the lowest slot nothing sits on in any case (`textStylePathFor`), never over another file, the entry read back before anything is written; the temporary is moved into place with an exclusive rename (`renameat2` `RENAME_NOREPLACE` on Linux, `renamex_np` `RENAME_EXCL` on macOS, a hard link where neither is supported, its temporary name removed after it and tried again as the save ends if that fails, `MoveFileExW` without replace on Windows), so a file another editor makes at the path meanwhile is left alone and the save fails with "already exists"; the Library dock rescans.
- **Bake:** Layer > Bake Layer runs `Document::bakeLayer`, enabled on a live text layer ("Bake Text"), a generator layer ("Bake Generator", #438: a cells layer of the cells its last run left, with its name, offset, visibility, mode and locks) or a cells or pixels layer with a style ("Bake Layer Style"): the core `bakeEdit` with the document's iCE setting, one step, the canvas showing what it showed. Undo gives back the layer, style and mode as they were, a generator with its params and form. A generator run asked for before the bake (a parameter step's, or Run) does nothing if the layer is baked when its turn comes: one still queued then doesn't start, and one in flight is dropped when it lands, as for a step a newer step on the layer overtook; undoing the bake gives back the generator with the step's params and the cells it baked, until it runs again. A bake undone before the run's turn comes leaves the run to start and land as any other.
- **Layers dock:** a Style column shows "fx" for a layer with a style, its switched-on effects in the tooltip. It has no editor of its own, so a double-click there opens the dialog rather than renaming.
- **Tests** (offscreen): the rows and the style they give; OK as one step and its undo and redo, Cancel and an unchanged OK as none, the dialog reopening on the layer's style; the settings widgets both ways; the preview against the composite, its IRC bytes and lint against the encoder and lint run directly; a style the layer can't draw turning OK off; a live text layer restyled and undone, and its confusables; Save as Preset writing a file the library scans, a second save going to `-2`, a bad tag writing nothing; Bake of styled cells and pixels layers with iCE on and off, and its undo and redo; the Style column, its tooltip and a real double-click. `test_library.cpp` checks the preset slots and the write.
- **Left for later:** applying a Library text style to a layer (the dock's `textStyleActivated`, a preset picker in the dialog) and showing an iCE-only preset disabled in the dialog; restyling a live text layer when iCE is toggled; Bake of a generator layer from the menu. (Fire and Rainbow came next, then applying a Library style, the dialog's presets and restyling on an iCE toggle, below; Bake of a generator layer came with #438, above.)

As built (#73, the Fire and Rainbow presets; `library/text-styles/`, `tests/test_text_presets.cpp`):
- **Why now:** #216 redrew `bw-colour.tdf` ladder-clean unstyled. The ramp Fills that failed only on its old shading now pass, with no effect, lint rule or fixture changed, so the two left-out looks ship in a form that passes the whole matrix (now 10 presets, 600 drawings), iCE on and off.
- **Fire:** a dark grey outline and a column Fill on wood (yellow, brown, red from left to right). Fill rows on the fire ramp reads better but fails one drawing (`mini` / `l23456_`, a one-glyph finding: the letters are too short for a second rung); columns on fire and skin fail a wide rung in `vga`; flat red passes but reads as red, not fire.
- **Rainbow:** a dark grey outline and a rainbow Fill on the fire ramp (red, light red, yellow, white a letter). Rainbow passes on fire, skin, wood, grass, violet and sea; on sunset, ice and toxic, the ramps with more hues, it still fails on `bw-colour` (a ▓ of two colours no ramp holds as neighbours), because a ramp Fill mapped the font's three source colours by rank onto a longer ramp and skipped rungs (#184, the rank mapping). A sunset Rainbow waits for that decision. (It shipped as Sunset, #184 Q6c, below.)
- **Tests:** the preset count, the matrix and the round trips take the two files as they take the others (10 × 2 × 6 × 5); the Library dock test lists ten thumbnails; `library check` on the bundled library reports 10 text styles.

As built (#73, applying Library text styles, the dialog's presets and restyling on an iCE toggle; `app/main_window_library.cpp`, `app/layer_style_dialog.h`, `core/text.h`, `tests/test_app_style_apply.cpp`):
- **Double-click:** a text style double-clicked in the Library dock goes on the active layer when that is a live text, cells or pixels layer (the layers `layerStyleProblem` lets hold one, and the ones the Layer Style dialog is enabled on), through `Document::setLayerProperties`: one step, "Text Style <name>". The preset's effects replace the layer's whole stack; nothing is merged, as choosing it in the dialog replaces the rows. A baked text, generator or reference layer, the style the layer has already, or a style that needs iCE while it is off pushes nothing, and the status bar says why.
- **Drop:** a dragged text style carries its key as `application/x-blockwright-text-style`, not the stamp type, and the canvas takes either. Dropped on a cell, it makes a new live text layer there holding "Text" in the Text tool's current face and settings, with the style drawn on: one step, "New Text Layer" (`Document::addTextLayer` takes the style, so the layer is made and styled in one push). The layer's top-left is the drop cell and the letters sit in it by the style's margins (Live text, above). The Text tool becomes active, editing the layer with the caret after the word; a layer emptied and left is dropped, as any the tool makes.
- **iCE:** both gestures refuse a style that needs iCE while the document's iCE is off ("<name> needs iCE colours, which are off (View > iCE Colours)"); the dock shows it disabled already, so neither normally reaches it. The Layer Style dialog has a Preset box ("Choose…", then the Library's text styles in the dock's order) above the effect list: choosing one loads its effects into the rows in its order, for OK to apply as the one "Layer Style" step, and the box shows "Choose…" again. While iCE is off a preset that needs it is disabled there, its tooltip "Needs iCE colours, which are off: turn on View > iCE Colours to use it"; the others say whether they need iCE. The box is filled as the dialog opens, and the dialog is window-modal, so the setting doesn't change under it.
- **iCE toggle:** a change of the document's iCE setting (`Document::setFormat`, which View > iCE Colours uses) redraws each live text layer with a style in the same step. The core's `iceEdit` gives a `LayerPropsEdit` with the properties unchanged and both the cells before and those drawn with the new setting, so undo and redo put the exact cells back rather than drawing again; a layer whose drawing doesn't change, or whose font can't be drawn now, is left as it is. Styled cells and pixels layers already followed it through the compositor, and plain text doesn't depend on it. Typing on a styled text layer (`setTextSource`) now draws with the document's iCE; it drew with iCE on whatever the setting.
- **Tests** (offscreen, `test_app_style_apply.cpp`): a double-click on a live text layer (one step, the cells `renderStyledText` draws, the same style again no step, another replacing it, undo and redo) and on cells and pixels layers (their cells kept, the composite changed and back); refusals on baked text and generator layers and of an iCE-only style with iCE off, which applies once iCE is on; a real drop of a dragged style on the canvas (the new layer's source, style, cells and place, one step, the Text tool editing it, undo and redo) and of a key the Library doesn't have; the caret after a dropped word and a click on its first letter where the style drew them; a typed layer styled mid-edit from the Library and the Layer Style dialog (Neon and Shadow), its caret moved with the letters and each click along them putting the caret it put before; a dropped layer emptied with Backspace and left by Escape or another tool (gone, the layer before active again, the undo stack back to where it was, nothing to redo); the Preset box with iCE off and on (the iCE-only preset disabled with its tooltip and refused, presets loaded through the box and `usePreset`, OK one step); an iCE toggle with a live text layer on a fire rows Fill (redrawn, a plain text layer untouched, undo and redo exact), then typing with iCE off and toggling back. `test_app_library_styles.cpp` checks that a style drags as a text style.

As built (#184 Q2, a ramp Fill's rank mapping on TheDraw faces; `core/src/text_style.cpp`, `tests/test_text_style.cpp`, `tests/test_text_presets.cpp`):
- **Rule:** a ramp Fill (rows, columns, rainbow) on TDF source cells keeps black as the ramp's darkest (black on every named ramp, as ascii.py's `recolour` keeps it) and gives the other colours the cells use, ranked dark to light by luma, consecutive ramp steps ending at the top step a Fill reaches: the ramp's top with iCE on, the step `walkRungs` stops at with it off (LightRed on fire, LightMagenta on sunset). Colours a rank apart are ramp neighbours, so no ▓ or ░ of two source colours skips a step. A face with more colours than the ramp has steps past black gives its lowest ranks the first step past black. A cell keeps its two colours apart and in rank order: with iCE off a background shows the dark step at or below its own, so the top window can merge them (a ▓ blue on cyan under steel came out grey on grey), and the window moves down a step at a time to the highest one that keeps every cell (steel: dark grey on grey). Where no window does (fire, ice, grass, toxic and violet have one dark step past black with iCE off, so blue on cyan can't take two consecutive steps there), the steps, each at most that top and rising with rank, are the ones that keep the most cells apart, then the most in order (each counted by the cells it is on, not the pairs of colours, so a pair on 20 cells outweighs one on 2), then put the fewest colours on black, then leave the fewest gaps, then reach highest (fire: black on red). A cell no choice keeps apart and in order (more colours than steps, each pair on a cell) is moved a step apart in its source's order: its foreground goes to the step past its background, up for a lighter foreground and down for a darker; where the foreground is black or the background is at the ramp's end, the background moves instead, to the nearest step past the foreground the other way that it can show. Black never moves (sea with iCE on: black on blue stays black on blue, grey on cyan goes light cyan on cyan, not cyan on light cyan). It replaces the spread from the ramp's bottom, which put `bw-colour`'s blue and cyan two steps apart on sunset, ice and toxic. Flat Fill is unchanged.
- **Look:** the TDF letters now sit at the ramp's top. Fire (columns on wood) goes from red and brown to yellow over brown on `bw-colour`, and from brown to yellow on `bw-block`; Rainbow (fire) on `bw-colour` goes from red with a yellow top to white over yellow with iCE on and light red over red with it off, and on `bw-block` from light red to white. The pixel faces draw as before.
- **Tests:** the mapping on `bw-colour` "FUNK" for rows, columns and rainbow on fire, sunset, ice, toxic, steel and sea with iCE on, fire and sunset with it off, black kept, and `bw-block`'s one colour taking the top (since #311, under a rainbow only; rows and columns run by position); and every named ramp × rows, columns, rainbow × the three TDF test fonts × the 5 fixture words, iCE on and off (900 drawings), linting clean and no source cell of two colours drawn one colour over itself. On the old mapping 90 of them failed, all sunset, ice and toxic on `bw-colour`. A one-glyph colour face, a ▓ blue on cyan, keeps its two colours under every named ramp, iCE on and off, in rows, columns and rainbow (steel, sunset and wood a step lower with iCE off, fire and ice black on their dark step); and one of eight colours with every pair on a cell keeps each cell's two apart, in their source order and with black kept, on sea, fire and steel, iCE on and off, with black on blue and grey on cyan checked exactly on sea. A face of five colours with every pair on a cell and 20 more blue on red takes blue, red, magenta and green to sea's steps 1, 2, 2, 3 (2 cells merged before they are moved apart), not 1, 1, 2, 3 (22).
- **Unblocked:** a sunset Rainbow (dark grey outline, rainbow Fill on sunset) now passes all 60 drawings of the preset matrix; it failed 10, all on `bw-colour`, before. Shipping it is #184 Q6c (below).

As built (#184 Q6, Fire and Rainbow, and the Sunset preset; `library/text-styles/sunset.style.json`, `tests/test_text_presets.cpp`):
- **Decision:** (a), then (c). (a) accepts Fire and Rainbow as #219 shipped them (above) and changes nothing. (c) adds a sunset Rainbow once Q2's mapping is in, as a preset of its own beside Rainbow on fire.
- **Sunset:** a dark grey outline and a rainbow Fill on the sunset ramp, which is already named, so no ramp is added. In the pixel faces each letter takes the next of blue, magenta, light magenta, light red and yellow, dusk to sun, with iCE on and off alike (a rainbow Fill colours ink pixels' foreground only). On the TheDraw faces the letters sit at the ramp's top (Q2): yellow on `bw-outline` and `bw-block` and yellow over light red on `bw-colour` with iCE on; with it off `walkRungs` stops at light magenta, so light magenta, and light magenta over magenta on `bw-colour`. It passes all 60 of its drawings, iCE on and off; it needs Q2, without which it failed 10, all on `bw-colour`.
- **Tests:** the preset list, the matrix and the round trips take the file as they take the others (11 × 2 × 6 × 5, 660 drawings); the Library dock test lists eleven thumbnails; `library check` on the bundled library reports 11 text styles.

As built (#184 Q4, Glow in counters and a Glow brightness; `core/src/text_style.cpp`, `core/src/project.cpp`, `app/layer_style_dialog.cpp`, `tests/test_text_style.cpp`, `tests/test_library.cpp`, `tests/test_app_layer_style.cpp`):
- **Decision:** (a) keep the inner ring inside counters, plus (b) separate brightness from width if (a) still reads weak. Both shipped.
- **(a) Rule:** the rings are the Chebyshev distance from the grown letters, as before. A cell two or more rings out is drawn as the halo's edge only where it joins the canvas outside the letters: a 4-connected flood from cells outside the halo and halo cells on the canvas edge, through cells two or more rings out. A cell the flood doesn't reach (a counter, the gap between two letters, a notch) holds the first ring's rung, so a counter narrower than the halo is one colour instead of a darker pocket inside a lighter ring. Source cells still hide the glow (above), so `bw-block` and `bw-colour`, whose counters are black source cells, draw as before.
- **(b) Rule:** `GlowEffect::brightness` is the rung of the ring next to the letters, apart from `width`, how many rings the halo has. Ring d (1 next to the letters) takes rung max(1, brightness − d + 1). With iCE off the walk stops where it did, so a brightness past that rung draws as that rung. `textStyleProblem` holds brightness to 1–16 and the ramp's top rung, as width. The dialog has a Brightness (rung) box under Width; Save as Preset… writes it and a Library preset reads it. A layer file or preset without the key reads brightness = width, which is how a Glow drew before, so older documents and presets draw the same; the key is always written. On live text only a Glow with brightness ≠ width bumps the version, to 1.8 or 2.4 (§6), since the saved cells already show the glow and an older reader that drops the key shows the same cells. On a cells or pixels layer the compositor draws the glow as the file opens, and the counter rule (a) changes what it draws even at brightness = width, so any Glow there is 3.0 and a 2.x reader refuses the file instead of drawing the ring at the width's rung or a darker pocket in a counter (Codex review on #231, rounds 1 and 2). 2.x files from before 3.0 with such a Glow still open and draw their counters filled.
- **Why (b):** with (a) alone, a same-ramp Glow next to a ramp Fill still fails because width set brightness. On toxic with a flat light green Fill (rung 8), width 1 is rung 1 alone (░, a one-glyph finding on every face) and width 2 skips from rung 2 to the letters' 8 (rung-skip on 40 drawings of `block`, 50 of `bw-colour`, 16 of `bw-block`, 63 of `vga`, 13 of `mini`, over the fixture words and iCE). Over the preset matrix (6 faces × 5 words × iCE, 60 drawings), toxic Glow + flat light green Fill: width 1 brightness 1 fails 60, brightness 4 fails 14 (speckles that brightness 1 has too; no one-glyph or rung-skip); width 2 brightness 2 fails 50 (all rung-skip), brightness 7 fails 34 with no rung-skip, brightness 15 fails 7. With a rows Fill: width 2 brightness 2 fails 50, brightness 15 fails 23; width 1 brightness 4 fails 31. Contrast (luma, 0.299/0.587/0.114): a toxic width-2 ring at brightness 2 (▒ green on black) is 50 against light green letters at 185, a murky band; at brightness 7 (▓ light green on green) it is 164, a glow that still reads apart from light green, light cyan (204) and white (255) letters; at 12 it is light cyan, the bottom row of a rows Fill, and the halo swallows the letters.
- **Limits:** a brightness at or above the letters' own rung merges the halo into them; keep it a rung or more below. A rows Fill spans several rungs (toxic 12–16), so no one brightness sits a rung under every row. On `block`, a one-cell hole (Z, S, 5) holds the first ring inside rung-8 letters at rung 7, which the lint reads as a speck. The bundled presets carry no Glow, so none changes.
- **Tests:** "a counter narrower than the halo holds the inner ring" (every Glow width 1–3, iCE on and off, the pixel faces and the three TDF test fonts over the fixture words; `block`, `vga`, `mini` and `bw-outline.tdf` must show such a counter) and "no ring is a speck inside a lighter one" (the fixture words) failed on the old drawing, 36 of 535 and 48 of 244 assertions (84 of 779 for `[glow]`); the rung of each ring by width, brightness and iCE on `block`; a toxic Glow at width 1 brightness 4 and width 2 brightness 7 beside a flat light green Fill on every face, word and iCE, with no rung-skip or one-glyph finding and every visible first ring at its brightness; brightness 0 and over the ramp refused by `textStyleProblem` and the reader; the 1.8, 2.4 and 3.0 versions, a switched-off Glow counting, a 2.x file with one on a cells layer refused, 4.0 refused and 3.0 read; "an older reader refuses a file whose Glow it would draw differently, or shows the same cells" (a transparent 7×7 cells layer with a white █ at (3, 3) and Glow steel width 2 brightness 6, and 1.8 and 2.4 files with the Glow on live text, read through the 2.4 reader's version gate with the key dropped) failed on the 2.4 writer, 5 of its assertions (7 of 35 515 for `[glow],[version]`); "a Glow round a counter on a cells layer saves as 3.0, which an older reader refuses" (white █ round the square (1,1)–(5,5) on a transparent 7×7 cells layer, Glow steel width 2 brightness 2, zip and directory saves, read by the 04dac94 reader simulated in the test: its version gate, the key dropped, and its Glow redrawn with each ring by distance alone, the rungs read off a dot whose rings all reach the edge; it checks the simulation differs from this build only at the counter cell (3, 3)) failed on the round-2 writer, which saved 2.0, 7 of its 331 assertions (8 of 35 787 for `[glow]` with the 2.4 → 3.0 change for a pixels layer's Glow at brightness = width); 2.x files with a cells layer's Glow and the key absent or at the width opening; equal brightness staying 1.3 with the key written, a file without the key reading brightness = width and drawing the same cells, and the zip and directory round trips; a preset's round trip and an old preset without the key; the dialog's Brightness box.

As built (#310 and #311, the iCE marking on Save as Preset and a gradient by position on one-colour faces; `core/include/blockwright/text_style.h`, `core/src/text_style.cpp`, `app/layer_style_dialog.cpp`, `tests/test_text_style.cpp`, `tests/test_text_presets.cpp`, `tests/test_samples.cpp`, `tests/test_app_layer_style.cpp`):
- **#310 rule:** `styleNeedsIce(style)` is the one test of whether a stack needs iCE. It reads the rungs each switched-on effect can reach on any text from the effect's ramp. A rung needs iCE when it is a ░▒▓ between two bright ramp colours. Glow reaches rung 1 to its brightness. A rows or columns Fill reaches rung 4 to the ramp's top, and a flat or rainbow Fill writes solids only. Shade reaches from the colour Fill leaves down to rung 4 (any rung when Fill is off). Bevel reaches one rung above any colour Fill and Shade leave. Save as Preset… writes `"ice": "required"` when it says so. Each bundled preset's marking is checked against it: the bundled presets need none (Fire's columns on wood have no two bright neighbours, a rainbow writes solids). A ramp Fill's rank mapping of a TheDraw face's colours depends on the face, not the stack, so it doesn't count.
- **#311 rule:** see Fill (row 5, above). Before, `recolourSources` put a face's one colour on the ramp's top step, and the gradient pass skipped source cells, so Block big text, whose cells are all source cells, came out flat white under fire. A face of two or more colours keeps the rank mapping (#184 Q2).
- **Look:** Block big text under a rows or columns Fill steps down the ramp, as a pixel face's gradient does (fire's rows with iCE off stay at light red, as a pixel face's do). `bw-block` and `bw-outline` take the gradient too: under the Fire preset (columns on wood), `bw-block` goes yellow, brown, red left to right where it was yellow throughout.
- **Tests:** `styleNeedsIce` on each effect's reach (fire, sunset and ice gradients need iCE; steel, wood and sea don't; Glow by brightness; Shade and Bevel from the Fill colour; an effect switched off and an unknown ramp count for nothing). Save as Preset… marks a fire rows Fill and leaves a steel one and an outline unmarked. Block big text "FORGE" under rows and columns on fire, steel and sunset, iCE on and off: each cell keeps its character, the steps fall light to dark with none skipped between neighbours, more than one step shows (except fire rows with iCE off), it lints clean and every line fits the budget. `bw-block` "FUNK" under fire takes light red to white in rows and red to white in columns, white under a rainbow. The 900-drawing TDF ramp Fill matrix and the preset matrix pass unchanged. Without the run rule, 17 drawings of the TDF matrix failed, all specks on `bw-block` columns where a step was one cell long. Drawing its full cells as the ░▒▓ ladder, as a pixel face's are, failed 69 (specks and one-glyph findings on a face three rows tall), which is why source cells keep their character.

As built (#177b, whole-cell big text; `core/big_text.h`, `app/text_options.h`):
- **Decision:** five built-in Text Options modes on the `block` face: Big shadow, Big 3D, Big outline, Big block and Big rainbow (#177c). These are source settings, separate from the Library's eight effect-stack presets. The stack cannot express the reference's one whole cell per font pixel, its one-column/one-row diagonal shadow, or its per-character colour list. Each mode stays editable as text and can take an ordinary effect stack afterwards. Picking a big mode sets the face to `block`; picking None restores native-size text. No TDF font is bundled.
- **Font:** the reference's 8×8 font is not the BIOS variant used by the native `block` face: 62 printable glyphs differ, including 0, 2, A and Z. The big-face adapter doubles each pixel row and applies XOR differences to those glyphs of the existing BIOS face, keeping one font table. The native `block`, `vga` and `mini` faces stay as they were; `mini`'s half-pixels are checked against the reference too.
- **Shapes:** Shadow copies ink one whole cell left; 3D copies it one cell down and right (a drop, not an extrusion); Outline grows it by its eight neighbouring cells; Block adds nothing. Letter ink wins over the edge. These are ascii.py 1.32.1's shapes. A solid foreground █ on black shows each reference background-colour pixel identically with iCE on and off, without a bright background or blink. Clear cells stay transparent. Clear rows are trimmed per line and clear columns around naturally sized text; a wrap/alignment box keeps its width. Explicit newlines, leading/trailing spaces, case, alignment and spacing remain editor settings; wrapped lines have the reference's one-row gap in addition to line spacing. The wrap width includes two cells of effect room, so a wrapped 80-column box is not clipped by a border or shadow. A word wider than the box breaks at a character, as native text does. Line hit testing follows each line's occupied box, keeping gap rows on the preceding line; the cursor keeps the font origin after trimming, which can sit above a low glyph's box.
- **Colours and thinning:** Big colours accepts mIRC numbers 0–15 or names, separated by commas, or the reference palette names (fire, ice, neon, sea, forest, candy, steel, rainbow). The first colour is the face, the second the shadow/border; extra colours are kept, as the reference accepts them. An empty stored list uses the mode's defaults. To pass the fixture ladder lint without altering the reference shapes, Shadow ships `7,0` instead of `7,6`, 3D `8,0` instead of `8,5`, Outline the unchanged `0,12`, and Block `0` instead of `4`. White edges and a white Block face avoid isolated non-highlight pixels in the font. Explicit original colours still reproduce the reference and may produce lint findings; user colours have the same freedom as other text colours.
- **Rainbow, thinned (#177c):** Big rainbow keeps the reference's shapes (Block's: the ink alone) and its walk: each character, spaces and missing glyphs included, takes the next of the colours (default `4,7,8,9,11,12,13`, the reference's), and each line, wrapped or after a newline, starts the walk again. The reference walk fails the ladder lint with iCE on and off (`ZB8S5O0I1`, `420`, `a0Zl!?` and `BLAZE 420` leave lone pixels, such as the dot in the reference 0, in a colour with no neighbour of its own), and the lint is unchanged, so the mode is thinned: after colouring, a pixel with no edge-neighbour of its own colour is drawn white, the top rung of every ramp, which the lint takes as a highlight. Every other pixel keeps the reference colour, so the walk still shows on every letter's strokes; only those specks change. This is separate from the Library's Rainbow preset, a ramp Fill (below).
- **Storage:** `TextSource` holds `bigStyle` and `bigColours`. A live big layer uses `bigSource` rather than `source`, with any effect stack inside that object as `style`, and writes project version 1.4 (or 2.0 when another layer requires that major); Big rainbow uses `bigRainbowSource` the same way and writes 1.6 (or 2.2). An older reader skips the new source key and its effects together and displays the saved cells instead of editing the big letters at native size. The source keys are mutually exclusive, and unknown modes, non-block faces, malformed or oversized colour lists and oversized boxes are refused. Source/style changes, undo, redo, project reopen and Bake use the existing text-layer machinery.
- **Proof:** `test_big_text.cpp` compares 86 drawings to cells captured from the reference renderer, including `420`, `BLAZE`, all five fixture texts, lowercase/punctuation and explicit colours, Rainbow's ten put through the thinning rule; all 95 printable glyphs match the reference adapter oracle. The four bundled modes on nine texts and Rainbow on its ten are 92 lint/budget drawings (iCE on/off), with both `checkLadder` and the default all-ramps lint, ≤80 columns including effect room, and ≤407 IRC bytes per row. Big rainbow also passes that lint for every printable glyph in each of the seven colour slots and for every pair of printable glyphs side by side, and its walk is checked across spaces, a missing glyph, letter spacing and a new line. The same 86 cases on/off are 172 undo/redo, zip/directory project round trips and Bake checks. Offscreen Text Options tests cover picking modes, entering a palette, invalid colours, new-layer defaults, Big rainbow's default walk, the eight-cell cursor, multiline clicks above a low glyph, gap and below-layer clicks, wrapped clicks and vertical arrows in all four modes, native TDF line boundaries, undo and redo. Original colours and the unthinned Rainbow have regression checks retaining their lint failures; `bigRainbowSource` has key, version (1.6, 2.2) and older-reader checks.

As built (#178 part 1, reference layers; `core/layer.h`, `core/project.h`, `app/canvas_view.h`, `tests/test_reference_layers.cpp`, `tests/test_app_reference_layers.cpp`):
- **Model:** a reference layer has no cells (0 × 0) and its picture in `LayerProperties::reference` (`ReferenceImage`: the bytes, shared, so copies of the stack don't copy them; x, y, width and height in canvas pixels; opacity 0–100). `props.x`/`props.y` stay 0; visibility is the layer's own. `Document::addReferenceLayer` adds one on top of the stack as an undo step, refusing what the writer would (`project::layerReferenceProblem`).
- **Format:** 1.5 (§6; 1.4 was taken by big text first): `refs/<sha256>.png|jpg`, one file a picture, checked on read and on write. A reader checks each file once and the layers naming it share one copy of its bytes. On write, reference bytes replace an opaque kept file at the same path, once per picture. A project with no picture still writes 1.3, 1.4 or 2.0, so the files of a project without one don't change.
- **View:** each visible one drawn over the art, bottom first, from a cached copy scaled to its view size; the copies share 2^24 px. Both import and project display check header dimensions before decoding: JPEG uses native scaled decoding; handlers without it (PNG and BMP included) are refused above 2^26 px, otherwise decoded in full then shrunk to at most 2^24 px. Only PNG, JPEG and BMP rely on their raster headers alone. An allowlist of formats detected from the bytes (`referenceDecode`: GIF, WebP, TIFF, ICO, PBM, PGM, PPM, XBM, XPM, each checked to honour the limit) also decodes under a 256 MiB Qt allocation cap (at least 4 bytes/px), including an ICO's embedded PNG whose dimensions differ from its directory; every other format is refused with a message naming it: PDF (decodes past the limit), SVG and SVGZ (decode embedded images of any format, PDF included, outside the allowlist), ICNS (reports no size), unrecognised bytes and later plugins. Higher-depth images can hit the byte cap below 2^26 px. Reference reads are serialized, preserve stricter caller limits and restore the previous process-wide limit on every exit; an environment override that prevents enforcing the cap causes refusal. Small ICOs with PNG or bitmap payloads still import. Unknown dimensions are refused. Qt's allocation limit can also refuse a decode. Retained pictures are at most 2^24 px each and kept only while a visible layer shows them (§4). Import reports refusal in the status bar; the view skips a refused project picture, caches the failure and reports it without allocating a scaled copy. It replaces the session overlay and its View > Reference Image menu.
- **Tests:** the core round trip of zip and directory (two pictures, one shown by two layers, one hidden), the versions, every refusal, what a 1.3 reader's save opens as, and that the composite, `.ans`, IRC and snapshots are the same with and without the layers; in the app, Open Reference Image (fit, aspect, undo, a BMP kept as a PNG, a file that won't decode, a save and reopen), drawing (place, size, opacity, stack order, hidden, at 2×), Reference Opacity and its undo, paint tools leaving the layer alone and Alt+drag refusing a hidden one, the decoded and scaled caches within the cap (thin layers included, and `fitPixels`), a PNG and a BMP over 2^24 px opened scaled at their own aspect, a reduced picture averaged, decoded layers sharing one picture's bytes, a kept-file collision replaced by reference bytes, PNG and BMP over 2^26 px refused before full decoding, JPEG over that ceiling decoded scaled under a 128 MiB Qt allocation limit, an oversized PNG embedded in a 16×16 ICO refused under raised or disabled caller limits, small PNG- and bitmap-backed ICO imports, caller-limit restoration on success and refusal (including concurrent reads), environment overrides checked in fresh processes, the format allowlist (PDF, unknown and suffix-only names refused), a PDF refused under a stricter caller limit and on import even with a .png suffix (skipped without the Qt PDF plugin), and refused import leaving undo and layers untouched with status feedback while project rendering skips and caches the failure.
- **Left for later (#178):** drawing a reference layer in its place in the stack (under the art layers above it) rather than over all the art. Done in part 3, below.

As built (#178 part 2, moving and resizing reference layers; `app/canvas_view.h`, `app/draw_tools.h`, `app/transform_options.h`, `tests/test_app_reference_transform.cpp`):
- **Gesture:** the #180 layer-move path (`CanvasView::beginLayerMove`/`moveLayerTo`/`endLayerMove`) takes a reference layer too, in canvas pixels (`canvasPixelAt`). A tool whose `Tool::movesLayer()` is true gets the gesture on a plain left drag; the Move/Transform tool is the one. Pressing on a corner handle (`referenceHandleAt`, 8 view px) with that tool resizes instead of moving: the opposite corner stays put, and without Shift both sides scale by the larger of the two ratios. The preview is the drag's frame (`referenceFrame`), drawn from the cached copy stretched so a drag doesn't rescale it; release commits one property command. Esc, a tool switch, another command or a reset drop it as they drop a move.
- **Tool:** `MoveTool` (`move`, Mo&ve/Transform, Ctrl+Shift+J, a toolbox button of its own) draws the active visible reference's frame and handles. Its Tool Options row is `TransformOptions`: X, Y, Width, Height, Keep aspect and Scale, synced from the document, each edit one "Move Layer" or "Resize Layer" step.
- **Tests:** Alt+drag moving a reference (preview, status, one step, composite unchanged, undo and redo, a zero drag adding nothing), Esc mid-drag, the Move tool moving an art layer by cells and a reference by pixels and refusing a locked one, handle hit tests, corner resizes with and without Shift (anchors, the 1 px floor, undo and redo, Esc, Shift pressed or let go mid-drag redrawing at once), Alt+drag on a corner with another tool moving, a stylus drag, the Tool Options fields (X, width with and without Keep aspect, Scale, undo and redo, following a drag, disabled with a note when locked; Keep aspect's width, height and scale steps there and back with no rounding drift, a stretched size keeping its own aspect, a free edit, undo and redo or another layer between kept edits taking the shape they leave; Scale's arrow steps up and down adding up through sizes that round the same, and a width, height, move, undo or another layer showing the size's own scale) and a moved and resized reference surviving a project save and reopen.

As built (#178 part 3, reference layers in stack order; `core/composite.h`, `app/canvas_view.h`, `tests/test_reference_layers.cpp`, `tests/test_app_reference_layers.cpp`):
- **Core:** `coverageAbove(stack, index, rect, options, shown, pending)` gives, for each cell of the rect, the halves (`Coverage::kTop`, `kBottom`) the visible layers above `index` show in the composite. It walks the stack bottom first as `compositeInto` does, tracking which cells are text: an opaque cell of a cells, text or generator layer covers both halves in any mode; a pixel layer the halves `layerPixels` gives, but not over a text cell; a styled layer by its styled cells (the compositor's per-thread cache of them); reference and hidden layers nothing. `ShownOffset` puts one layer at another offset, for a layer move's preview, which offsets the layer only while it recomposites. `ShownCells` puts a tool's cells in progress on the active layer (in canvas cells, as the layer composites them; a styled layer's are all its styled cells), for its preview.
- **View:** the tool's `paintCells` draws the composite as its edit will leave it (over the whole bounding box of the edit), then `paintReferenceLayers` draws every reference, each clipped to `uncoveredBy`: the uncovered runs of each half row of the cells it is painted over (a cell's top half the view rows `halfRowAt` reads as top), with the tool's `layerCells` (from `Document::previewEdit`, filled only while the stack shows a reference) on the active layer. So a stroke covers a reference under the active layer only in the cells and halves it draws, and an erase or a lifted selection uncovers it as it will; a paint works out coverage only for the cells it exposes.
- **Repaint:** a reference's visibility follows the art's coverage, which can change where the composite doesn't (a blank cell over the background's blank cell, the same glyph and colours). `layersChanged` (visibility, stack order, properties, a committed move) repaints the whole view, and a tool's `changed` does too, but `cellsChanged` carries only the cells whose composite changed. So while the stack shows a reference, `Document::layerCellsChanged` carries the cells a layer edit (its do, undo and redo) or a generator run touched and, for an offset preview (Alt+drag and its Esc), the cells within the old and new extents where the layer shows a different cell at the two offsets (`otherCells`), or the whole of those extents for a styled or missing layer, and the view repaints those too.
- **Tests:** the core covers each layer kind and mode, the half cells of a pixel layer, pixels over a text cell left uncovered until a glyph-writing cells layer makes it a cells cell, colours-only leaving the text, hidden and reference layers covering nothing, the rect clipped to the canvas, a shown offset, and a styled layer's outline covering cells its own cells don't; the app draws a reference under art and pixel halves above it and over the art under it, shows it where the art above is hidden, over everything when raised to the top and under again on undo, at 2×, two references at their own opacity with art between them, a line tool's cells in progress over a reference under the active layer and under one above it, a diagonal line's and a half-block stroke's cells in progress covering it only in the cells and halves they draw, an erase in progress (the glyph brush's right button) uncovering it only in the cells it erases, a floating selection uncovering it where it was lifted and covering it where it is, an art layer covering a reference as an Alt+drag moves it, uncovered again on Esc, and, from the paint events' regions (offscreen `grab()` repaints everything), an edit that leaves the composite as it was and its undo and redo repainting the cell, and an Alt+drag of blank cells repainting where it covers and where it left, and its Esc repainting both again; the core also covers a tool's cells in progress (`ShownCells`) covering, uncovering and the last change of a cell winning.

## 16. Wide and combining characters

Planned (#334, where the choices still to confirm are listed). Slices 1–8 are built: `Glyph`, the cluster pool, the width table and every writer's interim handling of a pair or a cluster, then the pair invariant in the core and the editor: the settle, the composite pass, selections, transforms, mirror and the eyedropper, then the `.ans`, XBIN and BIN writers and their loss reporting, then IRC: the encoder, the fit, the row meter, the IRC view, `decodeLine`, the sender's cut and IRC text's loss list, then `.cells` version 2 and project format 4.0, then the UTF-8 reader's pairs and clusters with its cluster-cap and full-pool reports, and lint's `width-varies`, `lone-mark` and `bidi-control`, then the renderer: Unifont, the system-Unifont option, the atlas, 9 px mode, squeezed wide glyphs and the cursor on a pair, then typing: the cursor, wide and combining clusters typed, Backspace and Delete, lone marks, the IME and the typing policy by encoding (Slices). A UTF-8 `.ans` now reads a wide glyph as a pair and a zero-width code point into the cluster before it; the renderer draws the pair across both cells and the cluster's marks over its base (Rendering). Typing makes either in the editor too (Cursor and typing). CP437, XBIN and BIN still read as §8 has it: one cell per code point.

A UTF-8 `.ans` or an IRC line can hold characters a terminal draws two columns wide (中, 한, ｗ, most emoji) and code points it draws on the character before (é as e + U+0301, the U+FE0F of ❤️, the joiner in 👨‍👩‍👧). Read one cell per code point, every column right of the first such character is out of place: a terminal draws 中 over two columns and the rest of the row moves right, and a mark gets a cell of its own, which the terminal never shows. This is for reading UTF-8 art made elsewhere in the columns a terminal gives it, editing it, and writing it back as UTF-8 `.ans` and IRC text that keep them; CP437 art, which is all narrow, is not touched. Snapshots follow the project's encoding, and a user-library stamp CP437 can't hold is saved as UTF-8 (#336), so both keep their pairs and clusters.

**The model.** A cell holds a *cluster*: one base code point plus any zero-width code points after it. A wide cluster takes two cells, a *lead* holding the cluster and a *continuation* to its right holding none. The continuation is a reserved glyph value, not a flag, and a lead is defined by it: **a lead is a cell whose right neighbour is a continuation**, and no other cell is one, whatever its width. So whether a cell is wide is stored in the canvas and never recomputed from a width table (a later Unicode version can't move a saved piece's columns). The pair invariant, kept by one core helper that every edit goes through (Editing): a continuation is never in column 0 or right of another continuation, and it has that lead's colours, attributes, transparency and marks (lost blink, dither, §10). A narrow cluster is one cell as today. Pairs are purely structural: the width table is consulted only where a pair is made (the UTF-8 reader, `decodeLine`, typing) and by the UTF-8 `.ans` writer's and IRC's check of a lead and the UTF-8 `.ans` writer's check of a lone cell (Writing), which change only what is written (a lead the table doesn't make two columns goes out as `?` and a space, a lone wide cell or lone mark as `?`), never the columns; never by the invariant, the settle or the `.cells` reader, so a lead whose cluster a later table calls narrow stays a lead and the columns a file was saved with are the columns it opens with.

A wide cluster in one cell with no continuation after it is a *lone wide* cell. Every wide code point in a project saved before this is one, and the UTF-8 reader made them until slice 6. It is an ordinary one-cell glyph to every rule here: the settle never spaces or converts it, edits, moves, pastes, flips and the composite keep it as they keep any glyph, every writer but the UTF-8 `.ans` writes it as today (its UTF-8 bytes in IRC text; `?` in CP437) and a UTF-8 `.ans` writes it `?`, listed (Writing), the IRC meter counts it one cell, the renderer draws it in one cell (Rendering), and lint `lone-mark` names it. Nothing lays it out again as a pair, since that would push every cell right of it on the row off the canvas: the UTF-8 reader would, from its bytes, which is why a UTF-8 `.ans` doesn't write them.

Tradeoff: one more cell kind, which every path that writes cells must respect (the helper does, and the pair tests cover each path), in exchange for columns that match the terminal and the IRC client; a side table per canvas or per row instead would keep `Cell` untouched but break the free copying of cells between canvases, edits, blocks and the clipboard that everything relies on.

**Storage.** `Cell::ch` becomes a `Glyph` (`core/` `glyph.h`), a 32-bit value: up to U+10FFFF a single code point (every cell today), 0x110000 the continuation, above it an id into a process-wide, append-only cluster pool (each entry its code points, at most 16, and its width). `Cell` stays 20 bytes (a `static_assert`). `Glyph` converts from `char32_t` implicitly but maps anything above U+10FFFF to U+FFFD, so no conversion makes a continuation or an id (`irc.cpp` expected such values in a cell, and `test_irc.cpp:86` put 0x110000 in one; that test now checks U+FFFD): the continuation and ids come only from named factories (`Glyph::continuation()`, the pool's `intern`). It converts to `char32_t` only through `codePoint()` (empty for a cluster or the continuation), so every site that took a cell's glyph as a code point fails to compile until it says what it does with a cluster: the compiler finds them, not a grep. The pool interns, so a cluster read a million times is one entry, and never frees. Its entries live in a fixed directory of 22 chunks, chunk k holding 1024·2^k entries (room for every id a `Glyph` has), each allocated when needed and never moved; a lookup takes no lock (the chunk pointers and the entry count are atomic, an id is checked against the count and an unknown one reads as U+FFFD), and the lock is only for adding. The cap of 65 536 entries applies only on the lossy paths, the ones that can keep a cluster's base and report its marks dropped: the UTF-8 reader, the IRC view's `decodeLine` and typing. Once the pool holds that many, a new cluster read or typed there keeps its base code point, drops its marks and is reported with a count (`12 clusters read as their base: too many distinct clusters`). Loading a project always interns its table, past the cap, since its own code-point limit (Project format) bounds it: whether a project opens never depends on what the session read before it, and only a pool out of ids or memory refuses one. Ids are process-local: they are never written, never sorted (`Glyph` has `==` and no `<`; a tie-break that sorted on the glyph, as the IRC fit's candidate order does, compares code point sequences instead), never hashed across processes, and no output follows the iteration order of a container keyed by `Glyph` (lint, loss lists and the `.cells` table go in cell order). The pool is shared by every test in a process, so no test asserts an id or the pool's size, only how the size changes across a call (`ClusterPool::size`). Timing tests (`[.perf]`, as `test_app_perf.cpp`): composite and `renderCanvas` of a 400×2000 canvas, the parse of a large UTF-8 `.ans` with and without clusters, and the pool's lookup, each against the same measure before the change.

**Width.** Per code point as glibc's `wcwidth` gives it, not as grapheme clusters (UAX #29). The grid is a terminal's: an IRC client that keeps art's columns mostly runs in a terminal and lays a line out by `wcwidth` (WeeChat by its host C library's, irssi by its own table or utf8proc's), so these are the columns the art is drawn and read in. Clients disagree, and lint notes where (Lint): WeeChat's widths are its host glibc's Unicode version, not this table's; irssi's built-in table is Unicode 5.0's, which counts most emoji 1; mIRC and HexChat draw in proportional or fallback fonts with no cell grid, so wide or combined art doesn't hold its columns there whatever the table says; kitty, WezTerm, foot and Ghostty in their grapheme modes widen VS16 and join ZWJ sequences. The rules are glibc's own (its `localedata/unicode-gen`, as glibc 2.44 builds its table for Unicode 17.0), first match wins:
- U+00AD is 1 and U+115F 2.
- 0: the Hangul jamo of syllable type V or T (in U+1160–11FF and U+D7B0–D7FF), general categories Mn and Me, and Default_Ignorable_Code_Point, which takes in U+200B, the joiners, the variation selectors, the bidi controls, the tags and the Hangul fillers U+3164 and U+FFA0. A zero-width code point joins a cluster (Reading).
- 2: East Asian Width W or F (which since Unicode 9 includes every Emoji_Presentation character).
- 1: everything else: Ambiguous, and the Cf characters that aren't default ignorable and draw (the prepended concatenation marks U+0600–0605, U+06DD, U+070F, U+0890–0891, U+08E2, U+110BD and U+110CD; U+FFF9–FFFB; U+13430–1343F). All 256 CP437 code points are 1, a test says so: ░▒▓, the box drawing and the blocks are Ambiguous, which some East Asian terminals draw wide, and the art depends on their being narrow.

Four differences from glibc, on purpose: U+0000, which a cell holds as a blank, is 1 (glibc: 0); U+3248–324F are 1, as UAX #11's Ambiguous is everywhere else (glibc's table gives them 2); an unassigned code point, which glibc calls unprintable (−1), is 2 in the ranges UAX #11 defaults to W and 1 elsewhere, the width a later version is likeliest to give it; and the C1 controls, U+2028 and U+2029, also −1 in glibc, are refused like controls: read into a cell of their own as C1 is today and never joined, written as `?` and listed in UTF-8 `.ans` (`utf8Writable` refuses them) and IRC, and not typed. A test pins every code point this paragraph and the list above name, and `scripts/gen-width 18.0.0 --check-libc 17.0.0` (the second version the one the host's C library builds its table from) compares the table with the host's `wcwidth` and expects these and the code points the two Unicode versions give differently, nothing else. So 👨‍👩‍👧 is three wide clusters (👨+ZWJ, 👩+ZWJ, 👧) over six cells, 👍🏽 two (the skin tone modifier is wide on its own), ❤️ one narrow cluster (VS16 doesn't widen), 🇬🇧 two narrow ones. The table is generated from Unicode 18.0's UCD (`UnicodeData.txt`, `EastAsianWidth.txt`, `DerivedCoreProperties.txt`, `HangulSyllableType.txt`, and `DerivedAge.txt` for lint) by a checked-in script (`scripts/gen-width`, the version its argument, so a bump is a regeneration and a test diff), its output (`core/src/width.inc`) checked in with the version in its header; Qt's own tables aren't used, since their Unicode version follows the Qt a distribution ships, and the cells a file reads as must not depend on the build.

**Reading.** In a UTF-8 `.ans` (§8) a wide cluster is read as a pair; with the cursor on the last column it wraps first, as xterm does, and on a one-column canvas it is U+FFFD. A zero-width code point joins the last cluster the parser wrote, which is the lead when that was a pair (a pair written at w−2..w−1 with its wrap pending included), as long as nothing has moved the cursor since (SGR doesn't); otherwise (at the start of the file, after a cursor move or a line break, or right after a C1 control, U+2028 or U+2029, which a cluster never holds) it takes a cell of its own, as today, and lint names it. A bare CR ends the cluster but doesn't move the cursor (§8's CR, as libansilove has it), so a zero-width code point after it takes the next cell as one of its own: `a` CR U+0301 `b` gives `a`, U+0301 and `b` in three cells. So 👨‍👩‍👧 read at column 0 gives leads at 0, 2 and 4 holding 👨+ZWJ, 👩+ZWJ and 👧, each with its continuation; a test says so. A cluster holds at most 16 code points: the zero-width code points past that are dropped, so the columns stay the terminal's, and the read reports them (`3 combining code points past 16 dropped at 4,0`), the one case where a file doesn't write back byte for byte. Otherwise the parser doesn't normalise: what it reads it writes back byte for byte. CP437 files, XBIN and BIN hold neither and read as today. The IRC view's `decodeLine` lays out clusters with the same rules, so the view shows the columns a client does; a mark after NUL, which an `.ans` reads as a space, takes a cell of its own there, as ascii.py's does (an IRC line never carries NUL). It interns a cluster once, when the cluster is finished, so no prefix of one enters the pool; past the pool's cap (which `DecodeOptions` can lower, for tests) a cluster keeps its base and drops its marks, counted. A wrap leaves the last column a blank filler cell, and on a one-column row the U+FFFD a wide code point becomes takes the marks after it.

**Editing.** `settleWide(canvas, edit)` runs before `settleMarks(canvas, edit)` in `Document::edit` (both on the layer's cells, once `fitLayerEdit` has fitted the edit to them, and the same in an edit's preview) and in `cli shade`, after every edit, over the changed cells and their neighbours. It goes by the continuations alone, never by width, so it never touches a lone wide cell:
- A pair the canvas held before the edit, which the edit wrote a glyph on one half of and not the other, breaks: the other half becomes a space in the pair's colours (as a terminal erases it).
- A continuation in column 0 or right of another continuation becomes a space in its own colours.
- A change of colour, attribute or mark alone on one half of a pair is made to both; when an edit writes both halves differently the lead's win.

A glyph written on one half breaks the pair whatever the edit does to the other half's colours, so a recoloured lead whose continuation gets `A` becomes a space, and so does a recoloured continuation whose lead gets `A`. A new pair written over an old one stays whole only when the edit writes its continuation too, in the new lead's values.

A path that writes pairs and would cut one at the canvas edge (a paste, move or stamp clipped at the right edge) writes that pair's lead as a space in its colours, as a selection copies a cut half, so no clip at the edge makes a new lone wide cell. A layer's edge and its alpha lock clip the same way: where `fitLayerEdit` keeps one half of a pair a move, paste, stamp or any other edit but typing writes (the other off the layer, or a clear cell the lock keeps clear), it writes the half it keeps as a space in its colours (`fitHalfPairs` in `layer.cpp`). A pair is judged by what the edit leaves, not by the cells it lists: a continuation written beside a lead the edit leaves out as unchanged (a pair moved or pasted onto its own glyph) is a pair with the layer's cell as its lead, and a paste, move, stamp or flip keeps a continuation it lands on unchanged beside a lead it changes, so the settle keeps that pair whole. Undo and redo replay the fitted edit, so they don't fit again. Mirror mode takes each cell the edit asks for with the pair it is a half of once the edit lands (`EditedCells`, `edit.h`: the settled edit's cells, and the canvas's where it leaves a half out as unchanged, so a pair typed over a lone wide glyph of its own is mirrored whole though only its continuation changes), puts the mirrored pair's lead at `mirrorColumn(x + 1)`, and returns the edit unsettled: its own changes and the images of those pairs and cells, not of the cells the settle adds, which hold no cell against an image either, so the settle runs once, after `fitLayerEdit`, and a pair the fitting refuses takes none of its cleanup with it; a pair that would overlap its own image (on an odd width, one covering or touching the middle column, such as 39–40 or 40–41 of 81) gets no mirrored copy and the status bar says so, and on an even width a pair across the axis (39–40 of 80) is its own image. Brushes need nothing of their own: a wide cell is not a half-block or spray cell to them, so plotting into either half writes that half and the settle spaces the other. Brushes, the glyph bar, the custom-glyph brush and the glyph picker take narrow glyphs only; the eyedropper on either half picks the pair's colours and its glyph only when narrow. Pixel layers can't hold a wide cell.

**Cursor and typing.** The cell cursor never rests on a continuation: arrows and clicks put it on the lead, which it draws two cells wide; `pastEnd` means on the last column or on a lead whose continuation is the last column. Typing a wide cluster writes the pair and moves two cells (onto the lead with `pastEnd` when the continuation is on the last column); on the last column it is refused in the status bar (an editor row isn't a stream that wraps). A lone zero-width code point typed (with no dead key or IME to compose it) joins the cluster that ends just left of the cursor on the same row, which is the lead when that cell is a continuation, or the cluster under the cursor with `pastEnd`: the one typing last wrote when the cursor followed it. The cluster is then NFC-normalised as typed text is today; at column 0, or as a 17th code point, it is refused. Backspace and Delete clear the whole pair. The IME's commit (`replaceText`) is split into clusters, its replacement range counted in clusters (Qt gives it in UTF-16 units from the cursor, which `clusterRange` converts against the cursor's row: each cluster counts its code points' UTF-16 units, a column off the row one, and a range starting or ending inside a cluster, in a surrogate pair or among its marks, takes all of it), and a wide one that would start on the last column is dropped and reported. What may be typed follows the typing policy (#350 slice 2: `typingPolicy`, Unicode under the IRC target or a UTF-8 `.ans` encoding, `Document::textEncoding`, §8): Unicode takes any cluster `utf8Writable` takes, wide or narrow, CP437 (an ANSI document saved as CP437) only CP437 glyphs as today, its refusal naming the cluster and the target and encoding (`中 has no CP437 code; not typed (target: ANSI, CP437)`, marks by code point). Controls, C1 controls and U+2028/2029 are never typed. Replay's `keyboard` step (§9) types under the same policy, so a steps file types what the editor would in that document.

Built (slice 8), with these choices where the plan left one open. The core does it (`edit.h`): `moveCursor`, `atRowEnd`, `isTypeable`, `splitClusters`, `typeCluster`, `typeMarks`, `replaceText`, `backspace` and `deleteCell`, each returning an `EditResult` that carries what it refused (`rejected`, which the app words by typing policy with `rejectedGlyphsMessage`) or a status `notice` in English. The cursor snaps against the active layer's cells. Text from a key goes through `replaceText` as an IME commit does, so a wide cluster typed on the last column is reported as dropped (`中 would start on the last column; dropped`) rather than with `typeCluster`'s `中 doesn't fit on the last column; not typed`; nothing is written either way. A commit of marks alone with no replacement range joins as a lone mark; a commit that starts with a mark followed by more is refused (`U+0301 starts text with more after it; not typed`), not joined. A narrow cluster typed over a lead overwrites it and makes the continuation a space. The typing edit is marked typed (`CellEdit::typed`) and writes that lead even when its cell is unchanged (a narrow cluster typed over its own base, a stored pair's `e`), and the settle takes a lead a typed edit writes as typed, not as a lead left with its glyph, which it would make a space; `toLayerEdit`, `fitLayerEdit` and mirror mode keep that change too. A pair kept whole needs both halves in the edit, so the typing edit writes the continuation beside a lead it writes wide or joins a mark to, though the continuation's cell is unchanged, and `toLayerEdit` and `fitLayerEdit` keep such a change where they drop other no-op ones (`dropNoOps` in `layer.cpp`), as `mirrorEdit` does for both images. A mark joined to a stored pair keeps it a pair whatever base NFC composes, since a pair's width is stored, not recomputed. A pair typed onto a layer is fitted whole or not at all: where alpha lock refuses one half, or the layer's edge clips it, `fitLayerEdit` drops the other too, so nothing is typed. Only a typed edit is rejected whole; every other path keeps the half that lands, as a space (above). Text typed past the end, a key's or a commit's, goes on the row's last cluster as the commit has left it, which is a pair's lead when a pair ends the row, so it is typing over the lead the cursor shows: a wide cluster replaces the pair and the cursor stays on its lead past the end, and a narrow one is written on the lead, the continuation becomes a space and the cursor moves onto it. That holds for a pair the same commit wrote, so `中a` committed on two columns leaves `a` and a space. A commit that writes and clears nothing, every cluster dropped, leaves the cursor as it was, past the end too. Past the pool's lossy cap (`ClusterPool::kLossyCap`, `TypingOptions::poolCap`) a new cluster is typed as its base code point, with `N clusters typed as their base: too many distinct clusters`. Under the Unicode policy the refusal says `U+2028 can't be held in a UTF-8 .ans; not typed (target: ANSI, UTF-8)` for a control (`… in IRC text` under the IRC target) and `… holds more than 16 code points; not typed` for a cluster too long. In a CP437 document a lone mark whose joined cluster has no CP437 glyph is refused naming the cluster (`q U+0302 has no CP437 code; not typed`), where it used to name the mark alone.

**Selections and transforms.** A selection is cells, not clusters, and doesn't snap: a half inside it is copied as a space in the pair's colours, and clearing or moving one half leaves the other a space (the settle). Flip horizontal swaps each (continuation, lead) it produces back into (lead, continuation) and leaves the glyph as it is (a wide glyph has no mirror form); flip vertical keeps pairs whole. Rotation refuses, all or nothing, a selection that holds either half of a pair, with a status message: there is no way to turn a two-cell glyph a quarter turn. A lone wide cell flips and turns as a glyph with no mirror or turned form does. Tests show a lone wide 中 surviving a move, a paste, both flips, a rotation and an edit of the cell beside it.

**Compositing.** Per cell, bottom up, as §5 has it, then one pass over the rect: a pair shows only where both its halves do, so a lead whose continuation doesn't show, or a continuation whose lead doesn't (each judged by its own layer's cell beside it), shows as a space in its own colours, and a layer above covering one half (a glyph, a half block, a pixel) blanks the other. A pair shows in one set of colours, those laid last over either half (its lead's when one layer laid both), so a colours-only layer over one half recolours both. A styled layer (§15) is settled after its style is drawn and before it composites: a fill that gives a pair's halves different colours (`recolourSources`, the rows and columns fills) shows the pair in its lead's, and an effect's glyph (outline, shadow, glow) on one half breaks the pair in the drawn layer, the other half a space. `recomposite(rect)` widens the rect by a column each side.

**Rendering.** VGA 8×16 stays the font for every CP437 glyph. The fallback for the rest is GNU Unifont, its BMP set and its upper-plane set (`unifont_upper`, which holds the emoji), 8×16 narrow and 16×16 wide on the same grid, in `third_party/unifont/` with its licence (dual SIL OFL 1.1 and GPL 2+ with the font embedding exception, either of which suits this GPL-3.0-or-later program; credited in `NOTICE`, as #177a's fonts are), compressed in the binary and decoded on first use. The file is `third_party/unifont/unifont.bin.z`, which `scripts/gen-unifont` writes from the release's two `.hex` files: zlib data behind a 4-byte big-endian length (the form `qUncompress` reads) of `BWUF`, a u32 little-endian glyph count, a LEB128 varint per glyph of 2 × the gap from the previous code point + 1 if it is 16 wide, then the bitmaps in the same order (16 bytes narrow, 32 wide, as in the `.hex` file). The private use areas (U+E000–F8FF, planes 15 and 16) are left out, so `unifont_all`'s ConScript glyphs don't come in with a system build. Unifont 18.0.01 makes 115 053 glyphs, 1 203 138 bytes compressed and 3.6 MB decoded, kept for the process. A CMake option (`BLOCKWRIGHT_SYSTEM_UNIFONT`, the directory of the distribution's Unifont `.hex` files) builds the same file from the system's copy instead (the script takes `unifont.hex` with `unifont_upper.hex` or `unifont_all.hex`, plain or gzipped), for the Debian package, which build-depends on `unifont` and `python3` and leaves `third_party/unifont/` out of the binary. The `--version` credit and the About box name the release the table came from: the bundled copy's with its copyright years (`third_party/unifont/README.md`), or in a system build the release its `.hex` files' names carry (`unifont-16.0.04.hex`), without years, and none when they carry none, as Debian's `unifont.hex` and `unifont_all.hex` don't (`gen-unifont --release`). Fedora ships only the BMP `unifont.hex`, without the upper planes, so the rpm compiles in the bundled copy and says so (`Provides: bundled(unifont)`), as the AUR packages do. A lead draws the glyph's left 8 columns and the continuation its right 8; in 9 px mode the lead's ninth column repeats its eighth, as 0xC0–0xDF do, so the glyph has no gap, and the continuation's is background. A glyph Unifont draws 16 wide in a single cell (a lone wide cell, or a narrow code point such as U+0B95 that Unifont draws wide) is squeezed to 8, each pair of columns OR-ed into one, so it shows whole at half width. A cluster draws its NFC precomposed form when there is one and Unifont has it, else the base with each mark's bitmap OR-ed over it; emoji are Unifont's monochrome outlines in the foreground colour; a joined sequence draws each of its clusters' bases. A mark 8 wide over a 16-wide base is drawn over its middle, and a 16-wide mark is squeezed; the default-ignorable marks (U+034F, the Khmer inherent vowels, the Mongolian and Unicode variation selectors) and the non-marks in a cluster (a joiner) draw nothing. A code point neither font has draws as `?`, as now; Unifont draws an unassigned BMP code point as its box with the code in it. Only a 16-pixel-high font draws from Unifont: with any other a continuation stays a space and anything else without a CP437 byte a `?`. The cursor frames both cells of a pair when it is on either, and an edit repaints each changed cell's neighbours, since a cell's half depends on the cell beside it; for the same reason a tool's cells in progress (a drag, a floating selection or paste) are rendered from the composite a column wider each side and only their own cells drawn, so a pair across the edge of their rectangle shows whole. A cell that isn't a lead and holds a CP437 code point takes the font path as before, so CP437 art renders pixel for pixel as it did and no slower. `AtlasRenderer` caches non-CP437 glyphs by (glyph, half, colour) and matches `renderCanvas` pixel for pixel, as the existing test requires of it.

**Writing.** A lone wide cell is written by each as today, except by the UTF-8 `.ans` writer (below).
- UTF-8 `.ans`: the lead writes all of its cluster's code points, the continuation nothing. A cluster with a code point `utf8Writable` refuses is written as `?` and listed. A lead is written whole only when the width table makes its glyph two columns and every code point is writable, since a terminal would otherwise draw it one column wide and the rest of the row would move left; any other lead is written `?` and its continuation a space, listed as wide (or as a control when a code point isn't writable), as CP437 writes one. Two cells that aren't leads are written `?` for the same reason, so the row keeps its columns when it is reopened or drawn by a terminal, and listed under their own kinds (`utf8LoneLoss`): a lone wide cell (`Loss::LoneWide`), which the reader would lay out as a pair, pushing the row right and wrapping its last cell, and a terminal draws over the next cell; and a lone mark, a cell whose base is zero-width (`Loss::LoneMark`), when anything was written before it on the row or the row before it was written full width with its wrap pending, since the reader joins it to the cluster before it and a terminal draws it there, so every cell right of it would move a column left. A no-op escape between them wouldn't help: it doesn't move the cursor, and a terminal draws the mark on the cell before it either way. A lone mark at the start of the file or of a row after a line break is written as it is and is no loss: the reader gives it a cell of its own again. The CLI's `convert` and `shade` warn of both, and the app's Save asks, as for the other kinds.
- CP437 `.ans`, XBIN and BIN: a lead writes `?` and its continuation a space in the same colours, so the columns stay; the lead is listed. A combined cluster whose NFC form is one CP437 code point writes that byte (e + U+0301 is 0x82, é) and is no loss; otherwise one whose base has a byte writes the base and is listed with the marks it drops; otherwise `?`. `Unwritable` holds the `Glyph` and what was lost (no code, marks dropped, wide, control), and `lossDescription`, the CLI's `convert` warning and lint word each (`中 at 3,0 written as ?`, `♥ U+FE0F written as ♥`). A CP437 project's snapshots write CP437 and so warn the same way, except that their question counts the wide glyphs (`12 wide glyphs written as ?`) instead of naming each, since a CJK or emoji piece has hundreds. The CLI's `convert` warning keeps its 1-based `column C, row R` positions and adds `, written as ♥` where the written glyph isn't `?`; a stamp CP437 can't hold is saved as UTF-8 instead (#336), so its message counts each kind only of what UTF-8 can't write either (`1 cell holds a control character`, `2 wide glyphs have no cell for their right half`).
